Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop): the button in both the File Manager and the Note Files Explorer toolbars; clicking it with no image on the clipboard shows "There is no image on the clipboard." without touching the folder. **The full round trip — a real image actually landing as a file — could not be verified live**: this sandbox has no working OS clipboard at all (`Set-Clipboard`/`System.Windows.Forms.Clipboard` fail outright with "Requested Clipboard operation did not succeed", the same finding "Links between pages of web apps" already records), and even the webview's own `navigator.clipboard.write()`/`.read()` — granted permission, confirmed via `navigator.permissions.query`, and with the document focused — writes without error but reads the item back with an empty `types` list, never `image/png`, so the type never round-trips through whatever this environment's clipboard actually is. The code path itself is not new risk: `writeFromFile` is the exact, already-proven call `uploadFiles` makes for a real picked file.

**Text editors and single-line boxes** (`components/TextFieldMenu.tsx`, mounted once in the admin-app and in Notes): while a `textarea` or a text-like `input` (text, search, url, tel) has the focus, a small "…" button sits at its corner and opens a menu — **Select paragraphs** (a single-line box: **Select all**), **Copy to / Paste from the app's clipboard**, **Copy to / Paste from the clipboard** (the OS's). Pasting replaces the selection. A **paragraph** (`lib/paragraphs.ts`) is a run of text between blank lines; selecting takes the paragraph(s) the selection touches — or the caret is in — without the whitespace around them (a caret in a gap takes the paragraph before it). The box keeps its focus and selection throughout (the button and items don't take the focus). A box that closes when something else is pressed opts out with `data-no-text-menu` (the page-list popover). A new text editor needs nothing: the menu finds it.

**Popups can be maximized** (`components/Modal.tsx`, `lib/modalStack.ts`): a button in the header and the chord Ctrl+K, M make the popup on top take the window less a margin (`clamp(6px, 2.1vw, 40px)`: thin on a phone, 40px at Full HD), so it still reads as a popup. (The file editors used to be a maximizable popup of their own, `components/EditorPanel.tsx` — see "Text editors are now dedicated pages, not popups" — so this is `Modal`-only today.)

**A dialog that was maximized last time starts maximized again** (asked for directly — `lib/modalMaximize.ts`): one entry per dialog *kind*, keyed by its own `title` (the one thing every `Modal` already has, usually distinct per kind of dialog — "New note…", "Go to a path…" — so nothing new had to be added just to name one), in the app's own app-state (`appState.ts`, not `global_settings` — a dialog's title only means anything within the app that shows it, so this is naturally per-app the same way everything else there is). `useMaximizable(title)` now takes the dialog's title: a mount effect checks the remembered value and flips to maximized only if it says so (a dialog never remembered, or remembered as *not* maximized, still starts plain, exactly as before this existed); `toggle()` — the one and only way `maximized` ever changes — persists the new value every time, so there's no separate "remember this" step to miss. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP): maximizing "Go to a path…" persisted `{"Go to a path": true}` to `get_app_state('modalMaximized')`; closing and reopening it started maximized again; restoring it to its plain size, closing and reopening it again started plain — both directions round-trip correctly, not just the one that happens to look right by default.

**A freshly opened modal's own autofocused field could look focused but take no real keystrokes — reported live** ("I tried creating a root note and the title textbox was readonly while also being empty… I remember not being able to type anything after clicking the input"). Reproduced live, not by inspection: opened a secondary window, let its OS focus go elsewhere (the admin window brought forward), then pressed a button in it that opens a `Modal` — `document.activeElement` was correctly the new field (so the component's own code has no `readOnly`/`disabled` logic at all; there was nothing to find there) while `document.hasFocus()` for that window read `false` — WebView2 delivers no real keystrokes to a window in that state, which looks exactly like a read-only box: nothing happens when you click it or type, no visible caret. Clicking the field with the mouse doesn't reliably fix this either, since the click can land while the OS is still mid-restoring the window's own focus. **Fixed in `Modal.tsx` itself, not any one dialog**: a mount effect calls `getCurrentWebviewWindow().setFocus()` — asking the OS for real focus explicitly the moment any modal opens, regardless of what left the window without it; a window that already has focus is unaffected. Needed a new capability, `core:window:allow-set-focus` (`user-apps.json` — a window asking for its own focus doesn't reach anywhere else, the same low-risk shape as `window_go_back`'s own entry in `docs/app-security.md`'s "Accepted by Me"). Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP): reproduced the exact precondition (a Notes window open, then stripped of OS focus by foregrounding another window), clicked "New note…" — before the fix, `document.hasFocus()` read `false` for the window and a real keystroke dispatched into the "focused" field landed nowhere; with the fix, `hasFocus()` read `true` immediately once the modal mounted, and the same real keystroke sequence (`Input.insertText`, not a DOM event) landed correctly in the field, confirmed by reading its value back.

**A floating field menu could keep floating over a second, later-opened modal — reported live, in the same investigation**: "I tapped the create note button, the create note popup modal showed, I tapped the custom actions button, the custom actions popup showed, but the custom actions buttons at the top of the previous modal were overlapping on top of the header of the current modal." The "custom actions buttons" are `TextFieldMenu`'s own floating strip (the "…" trigger, plus Notes' User Action ⚡/⚡✕ pair via its `extra` prop) — anchored to whichever text field has the focus, and deliberately drawn at `z-index: 3000` (`.text-menu` in `App.css`), *above* every `Modal`'s own `z-index: 1000`, so the strip is never hidden behind the very dialog its own field sits in. That same unconditionally-higher z-index is exactly the problem the moment a *second* modal opens on top of the first without the first field ever losing focus (right-clicking — not left-clicking — the floating ⚡ button to open `UserActionModal` moves no DOM focus at all, so `TextFieldMenu`'s existing "hide when something that isn't a text field gets focused" check, built for the ordinary case, never fires): the strip stays exactly where it was, now floating above a dialog it has nothing to do with. **Fixed** by giving the frame loop that already repositions the strip every animation frame (`CodeEditor.tsx`'s neighbor in spirit — see "Wrapped-aware line numbers" for the same "measure every frame" shape) one more check: find every `.modal-overlay` in the document (they mount in the order they open) and compare the *last* one against the one (if any) containing the anchored field — if they differ, hide the strip (`setPlacement(null)`) without touching `field` itself, so it reappears automatically, at its original position, the instant the newer modal closes, with no fresh focus event needed. A field with no enclosing modal at all is treated the same way the moment *any* modal opens over the whole page — the identical root cause, whether or not the original report happened to be about two nested modals specifically.

**The exact reported trigger (right-clicking the floating ⚡ button to open `UserActionModal`) could not be driven through this session's own CDP tooling** — real `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` right-clicks, and a direct synthetic `contextmenu` dispatch, both confirmed reaching `document` (a capturing debug listener counted them) but neither caused `UserActionModal` to actually open, for a reason not tracked down in the time available (plausibly something specific to how `onContextMenu`'s React synthetic handler interacts with a CDP-driven right-click in this WebView2 build, rather than anything about the fix itself). Rather than leave the fix's own logic unverified over that gap, it was exercised directly: with `NewNoteModal` open and its title field genuinely focused (the floating strip showing, confirmed at a measured screen position), a second, bare `.modal-overlay` element was inserted into the DOM by hand, after the first — the exact shape a second real `Modal` produces — and the strip disappeared on the very next animation frame; removing that element made it reappear at the identical position it held before. This proves the detection and recovery logic this fix actually added, even though the one specific UI gesture named in the report wasn't the thing that triggered it in this test.

`npm run build` and `cargo build` (the new capability) both clean throughout.

**A follow-up to the same bug class, asked for directly: "check all the modals where we have the 'Launch Custom Action' button below another row of buttons and make sure the 2 rows of buttons never overlap."** Unlike the "second modal opened on top" bug above, this is about the floating strip's own default placement *within a single, still-topmost modal* — an autofocused field sitting close enough to the modal's own header (`NewNoteModal`'s title, `FolderPairModal`'s, `LocationPicker`'s "New folder"/notebook-title fields, and others) that the strip's own "float just above the field" rule lands the ⚡/⚡✕/"…" row right on top of the `Modal`'s own Maximize/Close row (and any `actions` passed to it). **Reproduced live before touching any code**: opened `NewNoteModal`, measured both rows' real `getBoundingClientRect()`s — the strip's "Close the User Action window" button at `[633,667]×[267,301]` genuinely overlapped the header's own "Maximize" button at `[631,665]×[244,278]` (an 11px vertical band, 32px horizontal), and the "…" trigger likewise overlapped "Close" — a real, clickable overlap, not just a visual one. The same measurement on `FolderPairModal`'s title field showed the identical shape (a 9px overlap, by hand-calculation, before the fix).

**Root cause**: `TextFieldMenu.tsx`'s placement effect decided "is there room to float above the field" by comparing the field's own `box.top` against a fixed `TRIGGER_SIZE + 4` (38px) — measured from the **viewport's** top edge, with no idea a `Modal`'s own header might be sitting *right above* the field well before the viewport edge is anywhere close. A field 60–100px down the screen easily clears that check even while the real free space between it and its own dialog's header is only 10–30px. **Fixed** by computing `ceiling` — the nearest enclosing `.modal-panel`'s own `.modal-header` element's real bottom edge (its full box, padding included, not just its buttons' own rects) via `field.closest('.modal-panel')?.querySelector('.modal-header')?.getBoundingClientRect().bottom ?? 0` — and judging "is there room above" against *that*, not the viewport: `box.top - ceiling >= TRIGGER_SIZE + 4`. A field with no enclosing modal keeps `ceiling` at `0`, the exact pre-existing behaviour for every non-modal field (editor pages, page-level search boxes) — so this is additive, not a change in shape for anything that wasn't actually affected. The multi-line (`textarea`) branch is untouched: it already always floats *inside* the field near its own top (the module's own comment: "above it there is usually a dialog's header with buttons of its own"), so a textarea in a modal — `ClipboardManagerModal`'s edit box — was never at risk of this in the first place.

Verified live (isolated **debug** data folder — a scratch `DEFAULT_DATA_FOLDER`/WebView2 profile for this pass, reverted and the debug binary rebuilt clean afterward, confirmed by an empty `git diff` on `layout.rs`; desktop, real CDP, a fresh build + Rust rebuild in between to pick up the frontend change — the embedded dist needs a Rust rebuild to take effect, not just `npm run build`) against four real modals, each with its field genuinely focused (`autoFocus`, confirmed via `document.activeElement`): **`NewNoteModal`**'s title field — before the fix, the strip overlapped the header exactly as reproduced above; after it, the strip landed *inside* the field (`top ≈ fieldTop + 2`, the field being only 27px below the header's own real bottom, under the 42px room threshold) with a clean 29px gap to the header, zero overlap. **`GoToPathModal`**'s path field — already had enough real room (61px) even before the fix, so its placement was unchanged (still floats above the field, 23px clear of the header) — a useful negative control confirming the fix doesn't move anything that wasn't actually broken. **`FolderPairModal`**'s title field — by hand-calculation using the button rects alone this one *was* affected (a field 334px down with a header ending near 306px, 28px of apparent room, just over the naive 38px-from-viewport check since `box.top` itself was comfortably past 42 — the real bug was the *ceiling*, not the viewport check); after the fix it correctly fell back to the inside-the-field placement, 31px clear. **`NotebooksPage.tsx`'s `TitleDialog`** (its own "New notebook" title field — not `LocationPicker.tsx`, corrected here) — same shape, correctly placed inside the field with the real `.modal-header`'s own full bottom edge (351px, not the 338px a button-rects-only calculation would have used) as the ceiling, confirming the fix reads the header's whole box, padding included, not an approximation of it. **Not separately live-tested**: `MarkdownLinkModal`, `IndexesModal`, `PasteNotesModal` and `ClipboardManagerModal`'s own `<input>`/`<textarea>` fields — each goes through the identical, now-shared placement code already proven correct above (a textarea's own branch is unaffected by construction, as noted), so this rests on that shared mechanism rather than a fifth live click-through. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

**Reported live immediately after, with a screenshot of exactly the "fixed" `NewNoteModal`: "Those 3 buttons are now overlapping the textbox."** A real regression this same fix introduced — correctly stopping the strip from overlapping the *header* pushed it into the *other* fallback the placement code already had ("inside the field, near its top," the same branch a field close to the bare viewport top always used) — and for `NewNoteModal`/`FolderPairModal` specifically, that fallback was now the **guaranteed** outcome, every time, not a rare edge case: `.modal-body`'s own padding is 14px, `TRIGGER_SIZE + 4` is 38px, and neither modal has anything (a hint line, a label stacked above the input) between the header and the field to make up the missing 24px — so the strip landing on top of the field's own typed text wasn't incidental, it was structurally inevitable for these two. The one that only showed the bug *barely* (`TitleDialog`, 37px of real room against a 38px requirement — a single pixel short) confirms the same root cause at the opposite edge.

**No amount of cleverness in `TextFieldMenu.tsx`'s own placement math fixes this — there simply isn't 38px of room between the header and the field to put the strip in, wherever it's placed.** The real fix is the layout itself, not the placement algorithm: give these three fields genuine extra space above them, so the strip has an actual gap to float in rather than being squeezed between two things it can't overlap. A new CSS class, `.notes-field-leading` (`notes.css`, `margin-top: 28px`), applied to `NewNoteModal.tsx`'s and `FolderPairModal.tsx`'s own Title `<label className="notes-field">` and to `NotebooksPage.tsx`'s `TitleDialog`'s `<label className="notes-field notes-title-field">` — the three, and only three, fields confirmed affected (every other autofocused field checked — `GoToPathModal`'s path box, `MarkdownLinkModal`'s link-text box — already has a hint line or label-above-input ahead of it, giving natural clearance past 38px; `PasteNotesModal` and `IndexesModal` have no autofocused field at all). 28px brings the worst case (14px of bare `.modal-body` padding) to 42px, 4px past the 38px threshold, with headroom for sub-pixel rounding; the already-closer `TitleDialog` case lands comfortably past it too. A blanket fix — padding every modal's header-to-body gap, or every `.pair-modal`'s first child unconditionally — was avoided on purpose: `.pair-modal` is also used by `PdfConvertModal.tsx`, `PasteNotesModal.tsx` and `DeleteNotesModal.tsx`, none of which have this problem, and widening every modal in the app by 28px of top padding for three dialogs' sake would be a far bigger, unasked-for visual change.

Verified live (isolated **debug** data folder, desktop, real CDP, the same scratch-folder/rebuild-and-revert discipline as the fix above — confirmed by an empty `git diff` on `layout.rs` afterward) against all three: `NewNoteModal`'s title field — header bottom `277`, strip `[281,315]`, field top `319` — a clean 4px gap on *both* sides, the strip floating free of everything, confirmed both by direct rect measurement and a screenshot matching the clean, non-overlapping layout the person was expecting; `FolderPairModal`'s title field — header bottom `305`, strip `[311,345]`, field top `349` — the same 4–6px clearance on both sides; `TitleDialog`'s own "New notebook" field — header bottom `337`, strip `[364,398]`, field top `402` — comfortably clear (27px above the strip, since this one already had partial natural clearance before the 28px margin was added on top). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

**The file editor** (`components/CodeEditor.tsx`, used by the Files tab and by Notes — a new editor of a file uses it too, the note editor included). It is a `textarea` — so the clipboard menu, selection, drafts and the on-screen keyboard are unchanged — with a **syntax-highlighted copy of its text drawn behind it**: Markdown, HTML, CSS, JavaScript and **JSON** by the file's name (`lib/highlight.ts`: `languageOf`, `highlight`; `lib/highlightCode.ts` has the CSS, JavaScript and JSON scanners, also used for a `<style>`/`<script>` in HTML and for ```css / ```js / ```json fences in Markdown — `languageOf` accepts `.json`, `.jsonc` and `.json5`; any other file is plain; a text over `HIGHLIGHT_LIMIT` characters is left plain). JSON's own scanner colours strings (a key — one followed by `:` — in the same colour as an HTML attribute, a value string in its own), numbers, `true`/`false`/`null`, and the structural punctuation (`{ } [ ] : ,`); forgiving like the others, so a file that isn't quite valid JSON still gets whatever of it matches coloured rather than nothing at all. The box has a transparent text and the same font, padding and wrapping as the copy; it is as tall as its text and the frame around both scrolls, so nothing is kept in step but the height. `highlight` keeps one rule — **the text content of its result is exactly its input** (escaped, only wrapped in `<span class="tok-…">`), checked on 4 000 random texts — because the two layers must line up character for character (no italics, whose synthesized slant misaligned one glyph run on Android; checked visually with the box's text made visible over the copy, wrapped lines included). The colours are the `--tok-*` variables of `App.css` (light and dark) — imported by every page that uses `CodeEditor`, Notes' `system/notes/main.tsx` included, so the note editor gets the exact same palette as the Files tab. `data-text-menu-anchor` on the frame puts the clipboard menu's button at the frame's corner rather than the (tall) box's.

**The clipboard menu's button — and, in Notes, the User Action ⚡/⚡✕ pair beside it — now has a reserved slot in the editor's own toolbar, instead of floating over the code** (reported live: the "…" trigger, and especially the three-button cluster Notes adds via `extra`, sat on top of whatever text happened to be at the top-right corner of the box, covering it). `CodeEditor.tsx`'s `.code-editor-toolbar` (the row with Undo/Redo, hidden only when `readOnly`) gained a plain `<span className="code-editor-menu-slot" data-text-menu-target />` after the Undo/Redo buttons — an 80px-wide (`App.css`, `align-self: stretch` so it follows the toolbar row's own height), otherwise empty flex child reserved purely as a landing spot. `TextFieldMenu.tsx`'s placement effect now looks for `[data-text-menu-target]` as a sibling of the `[data-text-menu-anchor]` frame it already recognises, and — when present — positions the button cluster relative to *that slot* (right-aligned within it, vertically centred) instead of the frame's own corner; a field with no such slot (every other text box in the app) keeps the original corner-floating behaviour unchanged, in the `else` branch. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP, measured rects and a screenshot, not just a visual glance): in the admin Files tab's own editor (one button, the trigger alone) the slot and the rendered menu agreed exactly; in Notes' File Manager editor (three buttons — the User Action ⚡/⚡✕ pair plus the trigger, all contributed through `extra`) all three sat inside the 80px slot with 4px to spare and no overlap with each other, the Undo/Redo buttons to their left, or the code text in the frame below.

**A plain click opens a file in the text editor only when its extension is a recognized text one** (`lib/textFiles.ts`: `isDefaultTextFile`, a deliberately broad allowlist — `.txt`, `.md` and common programming/config languages — shared by the Files tab and Notes' File Manager/Note Files Explorer, so the two behave the same way). Reported live: clicking an unrecognized file (an `.mkv`, at the time not even known as a video — see "Media" below) fell through to the text editor and showed a size-based error that had nothing to do with why it really couldn't be opened; worse, the Files tab's own read path used `TextDecoder` with no `fatal` option, which never throws — a genuinely binary file just showed as garbled replacement characters, no error at all. Fixed two ways: **`isDefaultTextFile`** decides the *default* action ahead of any read — an unrecognized extension shows a clear message (`notDefaultTextMessage`) instead of attempting one; **`decodeText`** (moved out of `NotesApp.tsx` into the shared module, and adopted by the Files tab too) is the one safe read path everywhere — too large, a NUL byte in the first 8000 bytes, or invalid UTF-8 (`fatal: true`) all refuse cleanly rather than showing garbage. **"Edit as text" is now offered for every file** whose default action isn't already "open as text" (any media file — svg included, no longer a special case — or any other unrecognized extension), not just `.svg`, so the flexibility of forcing a text read is still there for whoever wants it; choosing it still goes through the same safe `decodeText`, so a truly binary file is refused even when asked for by name. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop): a `.json` file opened as text correctly (default), an unrecognized extension showed the new message with "Edit as text" offered, and forcing it open still worked. **Media** gained `.mkv` to its video list (`lib/media.ts`) — reported live as defaulting to the text editor's size error; now offered as video like any other.

**Text editors are now dedicated pages, not popups** (the note editor already was one — `NoteEditPage`, above — the remaining two weren't): the Files tab's own file editor and Notes' File Manager/Note Files Explorer's own file editor (`NotesApp.tsx`'s `editing` — the *same* component serves both the File Manager and the Note Files Explorer, `NotesApp` with a `scope`, so one change covers both) each used to float as a modal (`components/EditorPanel.tsx`, now deleted — nothing uses it any more) over a dimmed, still-visible listing behind it. Both now **replace the listing in place**: the tab's own content area shows either the folder listing or the editor, never both, with its own small header (`ArrowLeft` "Back", the file's name, "unsaved changes" when dirty, Save, and — Notes only — *New branch…*/`CacheMenu` where the source has them) reusing the `notes-page-header` class `NoteEditPage` already used, so the three editors read as one consistent shape. The state and logic underneath are **untouched** — this was a rendering change only, not a rewrite: the same `editing` state, the same `save`/`closeEditor`/`newBranchFromEditor`/`refreshEditor`/`openLinkFromEditor` functions, just returned from a different branch of the component's own JSX instead of wrapped in `EditorPanel`. In Notes, editing a file was already *part of the tab's place* before this change (`Location.edit`, part of the resource id — see "Editing a text file is part of the tab's place" above) — reloading or switching tabs back already reopened the editor; only its on-screen *shape* changed today, from a popup to a page. The Files tab's own editor has no equivalent place-persistence (its `SavedLocation`/`filesTab.location` doesn't carry an `edit` field) — that gap is unchanged, and wasn't part of this conversion.

**The media viewer gained real place-persistence it never had, alongside the same rendering question.** `MediaViewer.tsx` already opened "over the whole window" (its own long-standing doc comment) — visually not a `Modal`-style dimmed popup to begin with, so there was no overlay to remove there — but unlike the editor, viewing a picture, video or sound was **not** part of the tab's place: reloading the window, or switching to another tab and back, silently lost it, showing the plain listing instead. Fixed by extending `Location` the same way `edit` already worked: a new `viewing?: string` field (`tabs.ts`, `m=` in the resource id, alongside `edit`'s `e=`) — a path relative to the source's root, restored through a `viewingToRestore` state and effect that mirrors `editToRestore`'s own exactly (list the file's folder — always the same as the restored `path`, since `viewMedia` is never called with a different one — find the file by name, rebuild the `items`/`start` `viewMedia` itself would have built, and open the viewer on it). **One accepted simplification**: only the file the viewer was *opened* on is persisted, not wherever its own arrows/PageUp/PageDown have since taken the person browsing within it — `MediaViewer` doesn't report that back, and teaching it to was judged not worth the added complexity for what reopening a suspended window restores to. `withoutEdit` (the helper that strips "what a place was *doing*" from a plain fallback location) now strips `viewing` the same way it already stripped `edit`. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop): the Files tab's editor and Notes' File Manager's editor both render as a full-page swap with no `.editor-overlay` in the DOM (created a file in each, typed, saved — confirmed the byte-identical content on disk — then Back returned to the listing); the media viewer opened on a real image reported `m=test-image.png` in the tab's live resource id (`list_secondary_windows`); **suspending and reopening the window automatically reopened the viewer on that exact image** (`<img src>` confirmed unchanged after the round trip) with no listing shown first; closing the viewer dropped `m=` from the resource id again.

**The note editor's highlighting was reported as "only bolding of the bold segments"** (an older complaint) — re-verified live this session and **not reproduced**: opening a `.md` file through Notes' own File Manager (the same `CodeEditor`, the same `fileName`-based language detection `NoteEditPage.tsx` already used) showed every token — heading, quote, list marker, fenced code, a link's text, a url, `` `inline code` ``, `*emphasis*` — with its own distinct colour (`getComputedStyle` checked against the body's own text colour, live), not just `**bold**`'s font-weight. Whatever caused the original report looks to have already been fixed by earlier work on `highlight.ts`/`highlightCode.ts` and the `--tok-*` palette; nothing further was changed for markdown itself. **JSON was the one genuinely missing language** — added this session (above), and verified live the same way: a written `.json` file's keys, values, numbers, `true`/`null` and punctuation each got their own colour, and the highlighted layer's `textContent` matched the `textarea`'s value exactly (including a negative number with an exponent, `-3.5e2` → `-350`, staying one token).

**Open link** (`lib/textLinks.ts`, in the clipboard menu of a `CodeEditor` that is given `onOpenLink`): when the caret — or the selection — is inside an address, the menu offers **Open link**. An address is a web address (`http`/`https`, bare or in `<…>`), the target of a Markdown link, image or reference, or the value of an `href`/`src` attribute; a **web** address is asked about first and then opened — in Notes as an external web site of its window (the same box and rules as a web app's, `open_external_site`), in the admin-app's Files tab in the OS browser after a native box (`open_web_address`, admin only: it has no tab to list a site under) —, a **path** is resolved against the file being edited (relative to its folder, or absolute from the root of its folder or account, `resolveLinkedPath`) and opens what it names: a page (html, markdown) as a web app in Notes, another file in the editor, a folder in the listing. Changes held in the editor must be saved first (the linked file takes their place), a missing target is said next to the menu. A note's address (a query with the key `note`) opens that note, as a link in a page does (below).

**"Insert a path…"** (an `IconButton` beside Save, everywhere `CodeEditor` shows a markdown file — the Files tab's own editor, `NotesApp.tsx`'s File Manager/Note Files Explorer editor, and `NoteEditPage`) writes a path *into* the file being edited, the opposite direction from "Open link" above. `CodeEditor` exposes an imperative handle (`CodeEditorHandle`, `forwardRef`/`useImperativeHandle`: `insertAtCursor(text)`, which splices at the real `textarea` selection, goes through the same `applyEdit` every other change does — so it is one step of the editor's own undo history, Ctrl+Z included — and refocuses the box) that the three hosts each keep a `ref` to. The picker is two small, deliberately generic components, not tied to Notes: `components/FileExplorerModal.tsx` (a plain folder browser over any `list(path)` callback, breadcrumbs, Up, Cancel) and `components/PathStyleModal.tsx` (once a file is picked, offers **Relative** or **Absolute**, each shown in full so the choice is informed, never guessed). `lib/relativePath.ts` computes both, storage-agnostically: `relativePathFrom(fromFolder, targetPath)` (the shortest `../`-based form from the file being edited's own folder) and `absolutePathFrom(targetPath, root)` (strips `root` and answers a `/`-rooted path under it, or — when `root` is `null`, or the target isn't under it — the plain storage-root-absolute path). The Files tab (no notebook concept) always passes `root: null`; Notes' wrapper, `system/notes/InsertPathModal.tsx`, first asks `notebookContaining` (already used by User Action, above) whether the file being edited sits inside a listed notebook and, when it does, passes *that notebook's folder* as `root` — so a note's absolute paths are notebook-root-relative (`/999/0-Hello[note].md`), matching how a notebook is actually addressed elsewhere, and fall back to the plain storage root exactly like the Files tab when the file isn't in one. `InsertPathModal` adds a second way in on top of the generic explorer — a **Notes** mode (notebooks → a note → *Pick this note* — its real markdown path, found with `findMarkdown`, never guessed from its title — or *Its files…*, which switches to the generic explorer already opened on that note's `01` folder, or says the note has none yet) — with a plain button to switch between the two, since it's "another way in", not two permanently separate tabs. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop) in all three hosts: the button opens the explorer, folder navigation and breadcrumbs work, picking a file shows the correct Relative/Absolute pair (a same-folder file with no `../`, a nested one, and — in a note inside "Desktop Test NB" — the notebook-root-relative absolute path `/999/0-Hello[note].md` alongside the plain `../999/0-Hello[note].md`), the chosen text lands at the real cursor position, Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y undo and redo the insertion, and a save persists the byte-identical result to disk; Notes' notebook browsing, *Pick this note*, and *Its files…* (both its "no files yet" message and, once a `01` folder was created, switching in and picking an attachment from it) were all exercised against real on-disk notebooks.

**"Edit link" and "Insert a link"** (`lib/textLinks.ts`'s `markdownLinkAt`, `components/MarkdownLinkModal.tsx`; two more items of `TextFieldMenu`'s own clipboard menu, beside "Select paragraphs" — asked for directly, the same way that one already works) are specifically about **markdown** link syntax, `[text](address)`/`![text](address)`, unlike "Open link" above, which also follows a bare web address or an HTML `href`/`src` and so has only an address to show, never a separate text to edit. `markdownLinkAt` is `linkAt`'s own sibling — the same line-scoped, "what the caret or selection touches" search — but returns the construct's *whole* span (from `[`/`![` to the closing `)`) along with its text and address kept apart, since editing needs to replace the whole thing, not just the address inside it. **Edit link** is offered only when the caret is actually in one (gated the same way "Open link" is: an editor with `onOpenLink` wired — `editorLinkHandlers`, see "Open link" above — *and* a hit); clicking it opens `MarkdownLinkModal` (a plain two-box popup, "Link text" / "Link address", `Modal`-based, styled with `components/GoToPathModal.tsx`'s own admin-app-safe classes — `modal-field-label`, `toolbar-actions` — rather than `notes-field`/`dialog-actions`, which only `notes.css` styles and so would look unstyled in the admin-app's own Files tab, one of this popup's two hosts) pre-filled from the hit, and replaces exactly that span with what comes back (keeping whether it was an image, `![…]`, untouched — never offered as a choice, since turning a link into an image or back isn't what either command is for). **Insert a link** is offered whenever the editor can open links at all (no existing link needed) and opens the same popup empty, except the text box starts with whatever was selected (so turning a selected word into a link needs no retyping) — the new `[text](address)` replaces the selection (or lands at the plain caret). Both write back through `replaceSelection` (`field.setRangeText` + a real `input` event) — the same mechanism "Paste from the clipboard" already uses on these editors, not `CodeEditor`'s own `insertAtCursor` ref (`TextFieldMenu` has no ref to whichever `CodeEditor` owns the currently-focused field, only the raw DOM node), so the edit still lands in the editor's own undo history for free, Ctrl+Z included, through the exact path typing already does.

**A real bug was found live, not by inspection, and it was serious: *Save* silently did nothing** — the popup stayed open, the typed text visibly sitting in its own boxes, forever, no matter how many times the button was clicked. Root cause, confirmed by tracing it through CDP rather than guessing: `TextFieldMenu`'s own focus-tracking effect (`onFocusIn`, built for the ordinary case of the *trigger* floating over a plain editor) sets its tracked `field` to `null` the instant anything **that isn't itself a recognized text field** receives the focus — which a click on the popup's own *Save*/*Cancel* button (a `<button>`, not a text field) does, as a completely ordinary part of clicking it, **before** the resulting `click` event ever reaches the button's own `onClick`. The component's render was structured as an early `if (!field || !placement) return null` guarding the *whole* floating menu — including, in the version that had this bug, the popup itself, which was returned from *that very branch* when there was nothing else to show. The instant `field` went null, React's reconciler saw a **structurally different return value** (a different branch of the same `if`/early-return, which React treats as not-the-same-tree) and tore down and rebuilt the popup's own DOM mid-click — destroying the exact `<button>` the pointer was on before its `click` could ever fire. Nothing about the popup's own code was wrong; the bug was entirely in the host component's shape changing underneath it at the worst possible moment. **Fixed by making the component's returned tree shape identical regardless of `field`** — a plain `<>{menu && createPortal(menu, document.body)}{linkEditModal}</>`, `menu` now a plain nullable variable computed in an ordinary `if` block rather than a function-level early return, so `linkEditModal` is always the Fragment's same second child whether or not the floating trigger has anything to show. The bug reproduced with perfect consistency (confirmed via direct `elementFromPoint` and `getBoundingClientRect` checks that the click coordinates and the button were exactly right every time) and was undetectable by reading the code alone — it only shows up once the exact sequence of a real pointer click (focus-then-click, two separate browser events) is driving it, which is exactly why this was caught live and not before.

Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP mouse clicks — not `.click()`, which doesn't carry real OS focus in this environment and silently no-ops the whole feature; genuine `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`/`Input.insertText` throughout, including bringing the actual OS window to the foreground first, since WebView2 only fires focus events for a window that genuinely has it): a hand-written `[first link](https://example.com)` — caret placed inside it — showed **Edit link** (and not **Insert a link**'s sibling case) in the menu; the popup opened pre-filled with exactly `first link` / `https://example.com`; editing both boxes (real selected-then-typed input, not a prototype-setter shortcut) and pressing *Save* replaced the construct with `[edited link text](/new/address.md)`, byte-exact, with everything else in the file untouched; **Ctrl+Z undid it in one step**, back to the original text exactly; selecting the plain word "another" and choosing **Insert a link** (no existing link there, so this was the only link item offered) opened the popup with "another" already in the text box; typing an address and saving replaced the selected word with `[another](https://inserted.example)`, leaving the rest of the sentence untouched; the same file's *first* link was still intact throughout both operations. All of this was exercised in the admin-app's own Files tab editor specifically (not a Notes page), confirming the popup's choice of CSS classes actually renders correctly outside `notes.css`. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `cargo build`/`cargo test --lib` (263 tests, untouched — this is a pure frontend feature) both clean throughout.

**Open in external browser, and the clipboard options, asked for directly as additions to this same popup.** The one genuinely missing piece was **Open in external browser** — offered only while the address box holds a plain `http(s)://…` address (the box also accepts a relative or account-absolute *path*, which has nothing to open in a browser), and wired to *the exact same dispatch* "Open link" already uses (`editorLinkHandlers`, looked up by the popup's own `target` textarea) rather than inventing a second copy of the admin-app-vs-Notes distinction `open_web_address`/`open_external_site` already needs (see "Open link" above) — `MarkdownLinkModal` itself still knows nothing about which host it's in, it just calls back with a plain `{ kind: 'web', target: address }`. **The rest of the ask — "easy copy title and url to clipboard, paste from clipboard, and push/pop/peek + manage internal clipboard options" — needed no new code at all**: both boxes are ordinary, already-recognized text fields (a plain `<input>`, no `type` override), so `TextFieldMenu`'s own floating clipboard menu — copy/paste to both clipboards, and (since item 10 above) **Manage the app's clipboard…** — already appears the instant either one has the focus, the same as any other text box in the app. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP) rather than assumed from reading the code: opened **Edit link** on a real `[my link](https://example.com)` in the admin-app's Files tab editor — the new button appeared (enabled, since the address is a web one) and pressing it raised the real native **"Open a web address?"** box (`open_web_address`'s own, confirmed by enumerating its actual buttons — "Open"/"Cancel" — not assumed from the title alone), declined to avoid actually launching a browser as a side effect of a test; separately, focusing the popup's own address box showed the familiar floating strip, and opening it listed the same six items — Select all, both clipboards' copy/paste, and **Manage the app's clipboard…** — confirming the second half of the ask was already there, not added redundantly a second time inside the popup itself. **Not separately exercised live**: the Notes host (`openExternalSite`'s own two-stage confirmation) — the wiring is the identical `editorLinkHandlers` dispatch already proven for the admin-app's path, with nothing left for this popup's own code to get wrong differently per host.

**"Use the address (without its scheme) as the link text"** (asked for directly: "a button for repeating the url, without the https scheme if present, in the title textbox") — a plain `ArrowUp` `IconButton` added beside the Link text box in `MarkdownLinkModal.tsx`, replacing its value with the address's own (`addressWithoutScheme`: a case-insensitive `/^https?:\/\//i` prefix strip, not a URL parse, since the same address box also accepts a bare relative or account-absolute path, which has no scheme to strip at all — the regex simply doesn't match and the path is used exactly as typed). Disabled while the address is empty, the same as every other address-dependent control in this popup. No backend change — pure frontend, the address and text boxes' own plain React state. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP — a real `[sample link](https://example.com/some/path)` written directly to a markdown file, opened in the admin-app's Files tab editor, caret placed inside the link via a real `setSelectionRange` + `select` event, "Edit link" opened through the real clipboard-menu trigger): the modal opened pre-filled with `sample link` / `https://example.com/some/path`; clicking the new button replaced the text box with `example.com/some/path` — the scheme stripped, the address itself untouched; **Save** persisted `[example.com/some/path](https://example.com/some/path)` into the file, confirmed byte-exact on disk afterward. Two more cases checked in the same session: the button is correctly `disabled` the moment **Insert a link** opens with an empty address box (nothing to repeat yet); typing a scheme-less relative path (`../docs/readme.md`) into the address box and pressing the button copied it into the text box completely unchanged, confirming the regex's non-match leaves a path exactly as it was rather than mangling it. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change, so no `cargo test` impact.

**Slim windows.** Under 600 px wide (a phone, a narrowed window) a record's row of action buttons falls **below its text and tags** (`App.css`, the last block: `display: contents` on the row's header lets the buttons take `order` last; file tables become a block per entry). New listings use the existing classes (`window-item`, `row-actions`, `file-table`) and get it.

**A `<td className="row-actions">` is a real table cell above 600px, not a `flex` box.** The narrow-window rule above needs `.row-actions` to be `display: flex` (a flex item of its now-flex `<tr>`, and a flex container for its own buttons at once) — but applying that at *every* width, directly on the `<td>`, silently breaks the ordinary case: a `flex`/`inline-flex` box doesn't get a table cell's automatic "stretch to fill the row" behavior, and `height: 100%` doesn't reliably resolve against a table row's height either (it's content-derived, not "explicitly specified" for percentage purposes) — so the cell sat at its own natural size, top-aligned, leaving a gap below the icons whenever the row was taller than the icons alone needed (found live, and only confirmed by measuring both cells' `getBoundingClientRect()` directly — a screenshot alone read as "only the first row is broken" when every row actually was, just less visibly for a shorter one). `@media (min-width: 601px)` — the exact inverse of the narrow breakpoint — puts `.file-table td.row-actions` back to `display: table-cell` with `vertical-align: middle`, laying the buttons out with plain inline flow (`text-align: right`, a margin between them standing in for `gap`, which only a flex/grid container provides) rather than `flex`; they still wrap, the same as text wrapping, when they don't fit on one line. Verified live (both widths): at normal width every row's two cells now measure the same height and top position; under 600px `row-actions` still measures `display: flex` exactly as before.

**Compact row actions** (`components/RowActions.tsx`, `lib/rowActionsCompact.ts`; Settings → Lists). Every list's per-record row of icon buttons — Files, Filen.io, the four levels of the System/User Apps tabs, Storage, and every list of Notes (the file manager, notes, notebooks, search results) — is built with `RowActions` in place of its own sequence of `IconButton`s: `<RowActions actions={[{ icon, label, onClick, disabled?, danger? }, …]} />`, rendered with **no wrapper of its own** so it drops straight into whatever the list already had (a `<td className="row-actions">`, a `<div className="row-actions">`), keeping every existing layout — the slim-window CSS above included — unchanged. One setting for the whole app (`rowActionsCompact` in `global_settings`, read/written through the ordinary `get_/set_global_setting` — no new backend command was needed) decides, **everywhere at once**, whether a row's buttons show inline as they always have (off, the default) or collapse behind a single **⋯** button that opens them as a menu (`ContextMenu`, the same component a right click/long press opens elsewhere) — never a per-row or per-list choice, and the **⋯** button itself always sits inline. `RowActions` subscribes to the setting itself (`subscribeRowActionsCompact`), so every mounted row updates the moment it's changed in Settings, in the same window; other windows pick it up next time they load (as `listPageSize` already works). Left deliberately outside `RowActions`, at a fixed position beside it, because each is its own self-contained trigger rather than a plain action: `CacheMenu` (its own dropdown; CLAUDE.md's "Cache options of one item") and NotePage's own three-dots "More…" button (a different, larger menu of its own, for things that were never icon buttons — Select, F2/Shift+F2 renaming, tab management). `IconButton` forwards its ref (`forwardRef`) for callers that need it, though `RowActions` itself computes the menu's opening position from the click event's own target — never a ref read during render. Verified live (isolated data folder): the Settings toggle, off by default with every list's buttons inline exactly as before; switched on, the Files tab's rows, the System Apps tab's app and window levels, and Notes' notebook list all collapsed to one **⋯** per row, each opening the right actions for its state (checked in particular against a suspended window, which offers *Reopen* rather than the open state's *Focus*/*Reload*/*Suspend*) — including the row that keeps `CacheMenu` at a fixed position beside `RowActions`. **Not separately exercised live**: Filen.io, Storage, the tab-groups/tabs/external-sites levels of the Apps tabs, and Notes' other lists (the file manager, search results) — they go through the identical component and pattern as what was checked, so they are expected to behave the same, but weren't clicked through one by one.

`ContextMenu` itself — used here and everywhere else a right click/long press opens one — **has a transparent backdrop under the menu** (`.context-menu-backdrop`, `position: fixed; inset: 0`, `z-index` just below the menu's): a press outside the menu used to both close it *and* reach whatever was behind it at those coordinates (found live: closing a row's overflow menu this way could press the row underneath it, e.g. opening another row's own menu). The backdrop is a real element that catches that press first, so it never reaches the background — but **it closes on `onClick`, not `onPointerDown`**, which mattered more than it looked: a first version closed it on `pointerdown` with `preventDefault()`, reasoning that this would suppress the browser's own follow-up `click`. It doesn't — a real tap or mouse click fires `pointerdown` and `click` as two *separate* events, and `preventDefault()` on the first does not cancel the second. Since `onClose()` had already unmounted the backdrop synchronously inside the `pointerdown` handler, the browser re-hit-tests the later `click` against whatever the DOM looks like *at that moment* — with the backdrop gone, it lands on whatever is now exposed underneath, reaching through exactly like the bug this backdrop exists to prevent, just one event later (found live on the Android emulator: a real tap on a compact row's **⋯** menu, aimed at a point `elementFromPoint` confirmed was backdrop-covered, closed that menu but the follow-up `click` reopened the *next* row's menu at almost the same position — event-target logging showed `pointerdown` correctly hitting `.context-menu-backdrop` and the very next `click` hitting the row's button instead). Waiting for `onClick` instead fixes it at the source: the backdrop is still mounted — still the actual element under the pointer — when the browser dispatches the click, so the backdrop itself receives and stops it (`preventDefault()` + `stopPropagation()`), and only then does the handler unmount it, with no event left to leak through. **A synthetic `pointerdown` dispatched by a test script never reproduces this** (nothing then fires a follow-up `click`), which is why an earlier desktop-only test with synthetic events passed while the real bug remained. Escape and scroll/resize still close it the same way as before (a window-level listener, kept for that). Verified live on the Android emulator with real taps and event-target logging (not synthetic events): opening a compact row's overflow menu, then tapping exactly where another row's own **⋯** button sits underneath the menu's open area — both `pointerdown` and `click` landed on `.context-menu-backdrop`, the menu closed cleanly, and no second menu opened.

**`Pagination`'s own "go to page" popover has the identical backdrop fix** (`.page-popover-backdrop`, `Pagination.tsx`) — it is a separate component from `ContextMenu` with its own popover markup, so the `ContextMenu` backdrop fix above didn't cover it: it used to close only via a plain `window`-level `mousedown` listener that checked whether the click landed outside the popover's own element, which — exactly like `ContextMenu`'s old behavior — let the same press also land on and activate whatever was underneath (a file/folder row, say). Replaced the same way, **on `onClick` rather than `onPointerDown`** — the same fix, for the same reason, as `ContextMenu`'s backdrop above (a `pointerdown`-based version passed a desktop test built on synthetic events, then reappeared live on real touch input, because a synthetic `pointerdown` never fires the follow-up `click` a real tap does). Verified live on the Android emulator with a real tap and event-target logging: a point `elementFromPoint` confirmed was backdrop-covered (deliberately away from any row's own button, since this layout's narrow-window row buttons sit to the popover's side rather than under it) had both its `pointerdown` and its `click` land on `.page-popover-backdrop`, and the popover closed cleanly with every folder left untouched.

**Huge folders.** The Files tab asks for sizes only for the entries of the page on screen (as Notes always did for a local source), never for the whole folder — a folder of 100 000 files used to take over four minutes to show (a call per entry) and now takes about 0.2 s. What a listing costs — the entries all travel, in one JSON message, and sit in memory in Rust, in the webview and, for Filen, in the cache — is measured and analysed in `docs/huge-listings.md` (limits, what a failure looks like, and the paged-listing design that would remove them).

## External web sites

A user web app or a system app can ask to open an **external web site** — a page of the web, http or https — in a window of this app. It is **not a tab** (an external page has no `init_window_tab`/`update_tab_resource` to call, and can't be told anything): it is listed as a **child of the tab whose page asked for it**, one level below the tabs in the window manager (`external_sites.rs`, the `external_pages` table in `data.db`: guid, `tab_guid`, `window_guid`, `initial_url`, `url`, `title`, `created_at`; its tags are ordinary `window_tags` on its guid).

**Asking.** `open_external_site(url)` (granted to every window; `openExternalSite` in `src/lib/secondaryWindows.ts`, `TabLib.openExternalSite` in the samples) needs a caller that is a web app or system app with a registered tab. The address is checked first (`external_sites::check_url`): absolute, **http or https only**, with a host, **no user name or password in it**, at most 2048 characters, and **not one of this app's own origins** (`window_host::is_own_origin`: the frontend, `csuser`, or any `*.localhost` name — those have access to the backend). **One such request waits for the person at a time** (a second `open_external_site` meanwhile is refused at once). The command answers immediately with a request id; what the person chose arrives as an event, **to the window that asked and to no other** (listen with `getCurrentWebviewWindow().listen`, as for `tab-navigate`, and before asking):
- `external-site-response` `{ requestId, url, confirmed, pageGuid, error }` — the answer (`pageGuid` names the site in the events below; `error` says why it wasn't opened when the person said yes);
- `external-site-changed` `{ pageGuid, url, initialUrl, title }` — the site's address or title changed (the title as the page sets it, when it has one; the address also when a single-page site changes it without loading);
- `external-site-closed` `{ pageGuid }` — its window was closed (its entry stays in the window manager).

**Two native boxes, not one, decide whether it opens at all and where** (`external_sites::confirm`, `AskWhere`): the person can say no, open it in a window of this app (the existing behaviour) or hand it to their own OS browser instead — asked for live, after opening an external link "froze the entire app" on Android with no way out but the OS's own "app isn't responding" offer to force-stop it. A single three-choice box (Open in-app / Open in browser / Cancel) can't also carry **"Prevent this app from showing prompts"** — `docs/app-security.md`'s own standing requirement, and `prompt_guard::ask` only ever inserts it *inline* into a box of exactly two choices (there's no fourth slot on a native box, which has at most three) — so cramming the new option into the first box would have quietly dropped that guarantee from this specific prompt outside of the rare five-in-30-seconds burst escalation. Instead: **box one** asks *whether* at all — unchanged, still "Open" / "Cancel", still with its own inline Prevent exactly as before — and only if that's "Open" does **box two** ask *where* — "In your browser" / "In this app", also exactly two choices, so it gets its own inline Prevent too. The same two-boxes-of-two-choices shape `link_navigation.rs` already uses ("Open… / Copy… / Cancel", then "In this tab / In a new tab / Cancel") for the identical reason. Box two's **last** label is "In this app" — the pre-existing, more contained behaviour — on purpose: a box that is closed (Esc, the X) answers as its last button, so dismissing it without choosing lands on what this app already did before the option existed, never on the newer, less contained one. Opening in the browser reports **`confirmed: false`, `pageGuid: null`** to the asking page — the same as a plain "Cancel" — since nothing is listed as a site of this app then (no window, no tab entry); what the person does next in their own browser isn't the asking page's business to know about. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real native dialogs — `SendMessage`'d `BM_CLICK`, not synthetic events, since the point is exactly whether the real OS boxes and their real button layout behave as designed): box one's three buttons are "Open" / "Prevent this app from showing prompts" / "Cancel", confirmed by enumerating its real child controls; choosing "Open" shows box two with "In your browser" / "Prevent this app from showing prompts" / "In this app"; "Cancel" at box one shows nothing further and reports `confirmed: false`; "In this app" opens a real site window (a new CDP target for `https://example.com` appeared) and reports `confirmed: true` with a `pageGuid`; "In your browser" opens no in-app window (CDP's target count stayed the same) and reports `confirmed: false, pageGuid: null`.

**The Android freeze itself is not confirmed fixed.** The report was that opening an external link once froze the app completely, ending in Android's own "app isn't responding" prompt — a genuine ANR (the *main* thread blocked, not just a slow background task). Code review turned up a real, plausible mechanism: `android_jni::on_activity` — the bridge every Android JNI call from Rust into Kotlin goes through, `ExternalSites.open`/`openInBrowser` among them — posts its closure to Android's UI thread and then **hard-blocks the calling (Tokio) thread** on `mpsc::Receiver::recv_timeout` for up to `HELPER_TIMEOUT` (120 seconds) waiting for it, rather than yielding asynchronously; if the UI thread is ever slow to run that closure (busy with something else, or itself waiting on a Rust round trip that needs the very thread now blocked), the calling side hangs for up to two full minutes with no way to know why. This is a real architectural risk, not a guess pulled from nowhere — but it's also **long-standing and used everywhere on Android** (`SecureKey.kt`, `DeviceFiles.kt`, the folder picker, external sites' own `open`/`close`/`focus`/`drain`), extensively live-verified elsewhere in this file for years of features, so confidently pinning *this one report* on it — as opposed to something specific to the external-site path, or a one-off — needs a live Android reproduction this pass didn't do (it would mean an emulator boot and an APK build, not something to invest without checking first whether that's still wanted given the two-stage confirmation above already gives a working escape hatch — "In your browser" never touches `ExternalSites.open` or the in-app site path at all, only `openInBrowser`, itself on the same `on_activity` mechanism but a much simpler Kotlin call with far less that could go wrong on the other end).

**The site has no way into the backend.** On desktop it is an OS window labelled with the page's guid, showing the address as an external URL: no capability names a remote page, so every command is refused for it (verified live: a site calling `get_app_state` gets "not allowed on window…"), and it has no Tauri globals. Its window may only navigate to what `may_navigate_to` accepts — what `check_url` accepts, or `about:blank` — checked on **every** navigation, so a link, a redirect or a script can't take it to `tauri://`, `csuser://`, `*.localhost`, `file:` or `javascript:`, where a page *would* have access; and it can't open windows of its own (new-window requests are denied). On **Android** it is shown full screen in a plain `WebView` in an activity of its own (`ExternalSiteActivity.kt`, started through `ExternalSites.kt`; an activity rather than a dialog, because a dialog never got the keyboard focus and Back went to the app instead of the site), **not** the app's webview, so it has no JavaScript interface or Tauri bridge either, with the same navigation rule; **Each site is a task of its own in the Recents screen**, like a window (`csdrive-site://<id>`, `intoExisting`; *focus* starts it again to bring it forward). Rust starts it over JNI and **polls** `drain()` for its address/title/closed events every 300 ms while any site is open, as it does for the folder picker. Back goes back in the site's history, then closes it (the entry stays, suspended). The confirmation box is shown **by the window that asks** (`android_windows::ask`). A release build of the Android app blocks **cleartext http** (the manifest's `usesCleartextTraffic`), so there only https sites load; a debug build allows both. iOS: not implemented (the command answers with an error).

**Listed as children of the tab.** The address and the title are learnt from outside — desktop: page-load, navigation and document-title events, plus a look at the window's address every 1.5 s for single-page sites (no event is raised when those change it); the address at the start is kept as `initial_url`. In the admin-app's Apps tabs (`AppsTab`, both kinds) each **tab item** has a globe button (with a count) that opens the **external web sites level**; the level is one more in the navigation (apps → windows → tab groups → tabs → external web sites), saved like the others (`…navigation.tab`). An item shows the **globe icon**, the **title on the top row** (or "No title" until the page has one) and the **address on the bottom row**, with tags (`TagList`, as everywhere), a status dot (open/suspended), **Copy the address** and **Copy the address it was first opened at** (the two differ once the site redirects or the person navigates), bring-to-front, **Suspend**, **Reopen** and **Close**. **They can be reordered within their tab** (Sort, saved per tab as `…externalOrder`, as the other levels' orders are) and **nowhere else** — there is no cut/paste or "move to" for them, so a site can never sit under a tab other than the one that opened it (and `move_tab_to_group` takes the tab's sites along: their `window_guid`, who hears about them, follows the tab).

**Lifecycle — suspend and close, as for windows** (see "Suspending vs closing"). **Suspending** a site (`suspend_external_site`) closes only its window: the entry stays, as *suspended*, and **Reopen** (`reopen_external_site`, no confirmation — the person is asking in the admin-app itself) brings the window back at the address it was at. **Closing** it (`close_external_site`) closes the window *and* removes the entry with its tags. The same follows the tab it was opened from: **suspending the window a tab belongs to suspends all the sites opened from its tabs** (`handle_window_destroyed`, so every route to a suspend does it — the buttons, "suspend all", closing the tab being shown, Android leaving the app), while **closing** a tab, deleting its tab group or closing/removing its window entry **closes** its sites (`close_tab`, `delete_tab_group`, `delete_window_and_tags`; the DB work is `external_sites::delete_for_tabs/_for_window`, the windows are closed by the caller). Entries survive a restart, as suspended. (An app can have sites open for one tab at a time only in practice — a window shows one tab.)

## Links between pages of web apps (`link_navigation.rs`)

A web app's window never moves itself, so a click on an **anchor** to another page of its own storage (a path, with or without a query), a `GET` form, `target=_blank` / `window.open` and `history.pushState('…')`/`replaceState('…')` (turned into `location.assign` by the guard script) all reach the window's navigation rule as a **request** (`Nav::Request`, `OwnPage.on_request` on desktop, `nativeNavigation` on Android). The window stays where it is and the person is asked, in native boxes that belong to the window (`window_host::choose`: the dialog plugin on desktop — with **no parent**, a box with the window as its parent never showed —, `android_windows::ask` on Android). A native box has **at most three buttons** and one closed with its X/Esc answers as its last button, so every box ends in **Cancel**: *Open… / Copy… / Cancel*, then *In this tab / In a new tab / Cancel* or *To the clipboard / To the app's clipboard / Cancel*. The first box says what the link is: **Type** (Web page (HTML), Markdown document, Note, File or folder), **Path** (in its storage — `/docs/a.md`, the address that is copied and that a "Go to a path" box takes; a query is shown with it) and **In** (the user folder, "the folder X", the Filen account and branch). A link that can't be opened in a window (a picture, a folder) goes straight to *To the clipboard / To the app's clipboard / Cancel*. **A note's address is opened like a page**: `Open…` → *In this tab / In a new tab* — its markdown file is looked for when the person chooses (`link_navigation::note_page`: the file in the note's short folder whose name ends with `[note].md`, in the user folder, a picked folder or a Filen account/branch) and the tab shows that page; a note with no markdown says so.

**Addresses.** The browser already resolved a relative link against the page's own address, so it is relative to the file that was opened as a web app. An **absolute** path is relative to the root of the page's **storage** — a picked folder's root or the Filen account's (branch's) root — not the user folder the browser took it for: `link_navigation::resolve` puts it back under the page's `@device/<root>` / `@filen/<user>/<branch>` prefix (in the user folder an absolute path is just a path). A link can't lead out of the storage its page is in (another folder, account or branch, or from the user folder into one): refused with a box saying so. A query with the key **`note`** makes the address a note's — not a folder's or a file's (`classify`; opening notes comes with the notes themselves). Copying uses `tauri-plugin-clipboard-manager` for the OS clipboard (Rust only: no capability grants its commands to a page; retried a few times, as another program can hold the clipboard for a moment) and the app's own clipboard (`internal_clipboard`).

**In this tab / in a new tab** (`secondary_windows::open_link_in_window`). A web app's window has tab groups and tabs, and until now every tab of it was the same page (the app itself decided what a tab showed). A tab can now show **another page** (which does not stop it being a tab of its window's *app*: `tabs.relative_path` is not changed, so it can still be moved to another window of the same app with `move_tab_to_group` and goes on showing its page there): `tabs.link_page` (the page's path as a URL has it, and its query) is set when the person opens a link in it — the tab keeps `relative_path`, its window's app, which is what lets it be moved to another window of the same app (`move_tab_to_group`; it goes on showing its page there), its `resource_id` is that of the new page and its label is cleared until the page registers itself — or a new tab of the same tab group is made with it (it becomes the window's current tab); then the window is **taken there** (`window_host::navigate`: the window's navigation rule is told first — desktop keeps the page each window is at in `OwnPage.url`, Android in its window state — and the webview navigates). `page_to_show` says which page a window shows for a tab (`link_page`, else the page the window was made for): it decides what `reopen_secondary_window` and `activate_tab` open, and `activate_tab` on an **open** window whose tab shows another page takes the window there (a tab with no `link_page` still gets the `tab-navigate` event, as before). `link_page` follows the resource id the page reports with `update_tab_resource`, and `init_window_tab`'s answer names the file of the tab (`relativePath`: its path in the storage) rather than the window's (`fill_origin`). A page that never registers a tab (a plain html file) still works: its window's pending tab is the one that is used. The window's app (its entry's `relative_path`, the key of its app state) stays that of the page it was made for. This holds for **every kind of window, a system app's too**: Notes shows a note's page in a tab of its own window (see "The note pages"); a tab may also carry `tabs.syncs` (the served path of a file it follows — only such tabs are reloaded by `notify_file_saved`).

Tested live on desktop (isolated data folder; the boxes pressed with `TDM_CLICK_BUTTON`): a `#fragment` and `pushState('#h')` ask nothing, `pushState` to another origin throws, a relative / absolute / query link and a `pushState` path each show the box with the right type, path and place, *In this tab* and *In a new tab* (one tab / two tabs of one group; activating each takes the window to its page), the two clipboards' box (the app's clipboard checked), a picture and `target=_blank`, Cancel, a markdown link, and suspending and reopening (the window comes back at the tab's page). Not verified live: the OS clipboard's content — this machine's session had no access to the OS clipboard at all (`Set-Clipboard` fails there too), only that a refusal is reported in a box.

## No network access for any window (CSP)

The policy is written once, in `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` (`app.security.csp`; `devCsp` is the same plus the Vite dev server's websocket). Tauri applies it to the admin-app's own pages (adding hashes for the inline scripts in `index.html`), and `respond` in `lib.rs` sends the very same string, read back from the config, as a header with every `csuser://` page — so user web apps and the admin-app alike are under it. A script can reach only its own origin's files, `data:`/`blob:` URLs, and the IPC channel to this backend (`ipc:` / `http://ipc.localhost`, depending on the OS): `fetch`, XHR, WebSocket, EventSource, beacons, external scripts/styles/images/fonts/frames and form posts to anything else are all blocked, and `default-src` is `'none'` so anything not listed is too. The one place a page of ours is shown *inside* another is Notes' User Action popup (see "User Action"): `frame-src` names `csuser:` and `http://csuser.localhost` besides `'self'`, and the CSP test lets those two through for `img-src`, `media-src` and `frame-src` only. Inline scripts/styles remain allowed (`style-src-attr` is spelled out because Tauri's script/style hashes make `'unsafe-inline'` inert for `style-src`, which would otherwise break inline `style=""` attributes); `eval` is not. **A click on a link to the web opens the OS default browser**, not this app: `lock_down_navigation` hands every http/https address a window would navigate to (an anchor, `location.href`) or open in a new window (`target=_blank`, `window.open`) to `external_sites::open_in_browser` (desktop: `rundll32 url.dll,FileProtocolHandler` / `open` / `xdg-open`; Android: an `ACTION_VIEW` intent, `ExternalSites.openInBrowser`) and the window stays where it is; only what `check_url` accepts is opened. Opening a site *inside* this app happens only programmatically, with `open_external_site` (below). CSP doesn't cover navigating the window itself, so `lock_down_navigation` (`on_navigation` + denying `window.open`) confines every window to our own origins — web app windows to `csuser` only, the main window also to the admin-app's (`tauri://localhost`; `http://tauri.localhost` on Windows and Android; the dev server under `tauri dev`) — applied to the main window and every secondary window, so any new window builder must use it. A web app that needs anything from outside talks to it through a backend command (as Filen does), never directly. To add an exception, edit `tauri.conf.json` and the test that reads it (which asserts the policy names no external host).

## Android (and every other platform)

**Standing rule: everything in this app — what exists and everything added from now on — must work on Android as well as on desktop.** Check each new feature on the emulator, and keep desktop-only code behind `#[cfg(desktop)]` with a real Android counterpart (or an honest "not available on this platform" that the UI hides).

**Building.** The crate is a lib (`src/lib.rs`, `pub fn run()`, `#[cfg_attr(mobile, tauri::mobile_entry_point)]`) plus a two-line `src/main.rs`. The Android Studio project is `src-tauri/gen/android` — generated by `cargo tauri android init` but **committed and hand-edited**: `MainActivity.kt` (edge-to-edge + the safe-area bridge), `SecureKey.kt` and `DeviceFiles.kt` (Kotlin helpers Rust calls over JNI), `WindowActivity.kt` + `WindowBridge.kt` (the windows of web apps and system apps — see "Windows on Android"), `ExternalSites.kt` + `ExternalSiteActivity.kt` (external web sites) and `FolderPicker.kt`. From `csdrive-webhost-tauriapp`: `cargo tauri android build --debug --target x86_64 --apk` (it runs `npm run build` itself; needs `ANDROID_HOME`, `NDK_HOME`, and **JDK 17** as `JAVA_HOME` — Android Studio's bundled JDK 25 breaks Gradle). Testing without a device: an emulator (`quick_notes_pixel_34`, headless), `adb install -r` (keeps app data), and Chrome DevTools remote debugging (`adb forward tcp:9222 localabstract:webview_devtools_remote_<pid>`, then the CDP HTTP/WebSocket API) to run `window.__TAURI__.core.invoke(...)` in the pages. In Git Bash set `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` for adb. The emulator's app data is its own — nothing touches the desktop data.

**Windows on Android** — every window is an activity of its own (`android_windows.rs`, `WindowActivity.kt`, `WindowBridge.kt`; the reasoning is in `docs/strategies/android-windows-strategy.md`). Tauri creates exactly one webview on Android (the one in `MainActivity`, which shows the admin-app and nothing else), so each secondary window is a plain `android.webkit.WebView` in a `WindowActivity`, with a bridge of our own to the backend. Each is **its own task in the Recents screen** (`documentLaunchMode="intoExisting"`, the task keyed by the intent's data `csdrive-window://<guid>`; opening a window that is showing brings it to the front), so the person switches between windows with the system's task switcher, and opening app B never disturbs app A. A window entry is **open** while its activity exists; **Back** — or swiping it away in Recents — **suspends** it (entry, tabs and tags kept, its task removed; an accidental swipe must not delete a tab group), the admin-app's *Suspend*/*Close* finish the activity from Rust (*Close* also deletes the entry), and the cascade rules of "Suspending vs closing" apply as on desktop (suspending a window suspends the external sites and the web apps opened from its tabs).

**Back undoes one navigation before it ever suspends** (`secondary_windows::go_back`, `WindowActivity.back()`, `WindowBridge.nativeGoBack`): a tab switch that shows another tab in the *same* window — `activate_tab`, and a link followed "in this tab" (`open_link_in_window`) — remembers what the window showed a moment ago (`SecondaryWindowsState::previous_shown`, one entry per window, in memory; a link "in this tab" also remembers the tab's previous `resource_id`/`link_page` so undoing it restores the exact page, not just the tab). Pressing Back calls `nativeGoBack` — a plain, synchronous JNI call Kotlin makes directly, the same trust model as `nativeServe`/`nativeAttach`, not a page-originated `invoke` — which pops that one entry (if any) and switches the window back to it, leaving the window **open**; a *second* Back press, with nothing left to undo, falls through to the ordinary suspend. This is Notes' and every web app's Back behavior alike (a note opened as a web app in a syncing tab, or a link followed within a tab), so no top bar or per-tab suspend/close buttons are needed for the common "go back one step" case (see Follow-up 3, item 5 — items 3/4's top-bar design stay postponed). `previous_shown` is cleared once used, so Back only ever undoes the *last* step, not a whole history. Verified live on the Android emulator (isolated app data): opening a note as a web app switched the window to the note's page in place (same activity, same task); the first Back returned it to the notes list with the window still `isOpen: true` and the note's tab kept (`showing: false`, not removed); the second Back suspended it as before (`isOpen: false`, task removed, entry kept). If the process is killed, entries are suspended at the next start; a task the system brings back whose window the (restarted) backend doesn't know starts the app and finishes itself. All the commands (`open_new_/reopen_/suspend_/close_/close_all_/suspend_all_secondary_window(s)`, `focus_`, `activate_tab`) keep their signatures on both platforms.

- **How a page reaches the backend.** `window.CsdriveBridge` is a JavaScript interface `WindowActivity` attaches to *its* WebView for *its* guid — it only says the window's label — and `window.CsdriveInvoke` is a **message port that only the window's main frame can use** (`WebViewCompat.addWebMessageListener`, offered to the window's own origins and told whether a message came from the main frame: a JavaScript interface is injected into *every* frame and can't tell which one calls, so a page shown in a frame — Notes' User Action popup — could have called as the window; a WebView that can't do this gets no bridge at all); `scripts/window-bridge.js` builds `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__` on it (invoke, callbacks, metadata) and the crate's build copies Tauri's own `window.__TAURI__` bundle (`build.rs` → `window_scripts.rs`), so `__TAURI__.core.invoke`, `getCurrentWebviewWindow().listen` and `@tauri-apps/api` work as on desktop. `invoke` goes `CsdriveInvoke.postMessage` → `WindowBridge.nativeInvoke` (JNI) → `android_windows::dispatch` → Tauri's own dispatcher (`Webview::on_message` of `main`), so every command runs exactly as if its page had called it; the answer returns as `runCallback` (or, for raw bytes — an `ArrayBuffer` — the page fetches them once from `http://ipc.localhost/raw/<key>`, so a big file isn't base64ed). Request bodies are JSON only (bytes as base64 in a JSON object, `invokeWithBytes`'s Android form). `plugin:event|*` is kept in the page (listeners live there; Rust delivers with `window_host::emit_if_open` → `__csdriveEmit`, to that window only), `plugin:dialog|*` is refused (a window asks with `confirm_dialog`); **every other `plugin:` command is refused**.
