**Not done**: Android/iOS (an honest error, as above); a Filen file (would need the cache to have the whole file locally first — a different, larger problem); duration-based exact progress (the byte-ratio approximation was judged a reasonable trade against two more Cargo features and `PROPVARIANT` handling for a progress bar); re-encoding only the track that actually needs it (today both video and audio are always re-encoded once *either* needs it, even if, say, the video was already H.264 and only the audio was the problem — a real, deliberate simplification: detecting "this stream is already acceptable, pass it through unchanged" would mean a third code path, muxing a compressed stream directly without decode/encode at all, and wasn't judged worth the added complexity for what's fundamentally a compatibility fallback, not a quality-critical path).

**"Compress and save a copy…" — reusing this same pipeline as a plain file-size-reduction tool, not only a playback-compatibility fallback** (asked for directly, with the design question — build a separate, more aggressive compression target, or reuse the existing compatibility pipeline as-is — answered as "reuse the existing pipeline as-is": the H.264/AAC output this module already produces for playback compatibility is also, incidentally, usually much smaller than an unoptimized or less-efficiently-encoded source, so there was no need for a second bitrate target or a second code path). In Notes' File Manager and Note Files Explorer (`NotesApp.tsx` — both surfaces for free, since `NoteFilesPage.tsx` renders `NotesApp` with a `scope` prop rather than being a separate component), a video file (gated the same way `MediaViewer.tsx`'s own Convert button already is: `mediaKindOf(entry.name) === 'video'` and the entry's storage is `UserFolder`/`DeviceFolder`, never a Filen account — `localVideoRoot`, mirroring `MediaViewer.tsx`'s `transcodeRoot`) gets a new **"Compress and save a copy…"** action, in both the thumbnail grid's card menu and the list view's row actions.

**No new backend command for the conversion itself — only for getting the already-cached result onto the device**, since `video_transcode_begin`/`_progress`/`_status` already do everything the compression side needs (they don't care *why* the compatibility cache is being populated). The one new command is `video_transcode_export(root, path, name, token)` (`video_transcode.rs`, granted in `user-apps.json` like its four siblings): it confirms the export the same way every other export does (`device_files::confirm_export`), resolves the file's *cached converted copy* through the existing `lookup()` (the same function `video_transcode_status`'s own serving route already calls — refusing with "Nothing is cached yet — compress it first." if the conversion hasn't actually finished, which can't normally happen through this button since the frontend always waits for `done` first, but keeps the command honest if ever called out of order), and hands that real path to `device_files::export_file` — the exact generic "copy an already-known real path to wherever the person just chose to save it" primitive `export_local_file` and `filen_cache_export` already use, reused rather than duplicated a third time. `NotesApp.tsx`'s `compressAndExport(entry)` begins the conversion (`videoTranscodeBegin`), polls `videoTranscodeProgress` every 500ms exactly like `MediaViewer.tsx`'s own Convert button does, and — once `done` with no error — calls `exportPathToDevice` (the existing device-file-save helper) with a callback that calls `videoTranscodeExport`, so the on-device "save as" dialog only appears once the file is actually ready, never before. A small progress modal (`Modal`, the same `.progress-bar`/`.progress-bar-fill` pair every other progress surface in this app already shares) shows the running conversion with a **"Keep running in the background"** button (dismisses the modal but leaves the conversion and its polling loop running — a `compressDismissed` ref the polling loop checks, so a dismissed job's eventual error is routed to the ordinary error notice instead of trying to update a closed modal) alongside the title-bar close, which does the same thing.

**No dedicated Rust test was added for `video_transcode_export` itself** — consistent with this project's own established precedent for a thin command whose every constituent call (`confirm_export`, `lookup`, `export_file`) is already independently tested elsewhere (the same reasoning `video_transcode_begin`/`_status`/`_delete` were never given dedicated integration tests of their own, beyond what `lookup`'s own tests and the serving-route tests already cover).

**Verified live (isolated data folder — the debug build's own `com.ayran.csdrive-webhost-tauriapp-debug` data folder, automatically separate from the production data folder per `layout.rs`'s `DEFAULT_DATA_FOLDER` — desktop, real CDP, the full command→UI stack, not a hand-called backend function):** a `fake-video.mp4` (43 bytes of plain text, not a real video — no real video file or encoder was available in this environment to produce one) and a `plain.txt` (31 bytes) were created directly in the debug instance's `user` folder; opened in a real Notes File Manager window, **the row actions correctly showed "Compress and save a copy…" for `fake-video.mp4` only** (`Details, View, Edit as text, Export, Compress and save a copy…, Copy, Cut, Rename, Delete`) **and correctly omitted it for `plain.txt`** (`Details, Export, Copy, Cut, Rename, Delete` — no View/Edit-as-text either, since a `.txt` isn't recognized as video and `isDefaultTextFile` already offers "Edit as text" separately for it); switching to the thumbnail grid and opening each card's own "More…" context menu showed the identical gating (`View, Edit as text, Details, Export, Compress and save a copy…, Copy, Cut, Rename, Delete` for the video; `Open, Details, Export, Copy, Cut, Rename, Delete` for the text file) — confirming both surfaces named in the original ask ("Notes File Manager and Note Files Explorer") share the one gating logic correctly, not two independently-maintained copies that could drift apart. **The error-handling path was exercised against a real click, not assumed**: clicking "Compress and save a copy…" on the fake (non-video) `.mp4` opened the progress modal and, within about a second, surfaced "This file can't be opened: The byte stream type of the given URL is unsupported." with a working **Close** button — Media Foundation correctly refusing content that isn't a real video stream, and the UI correctly surfacing that refusal as a clear, readable message rather than hanging, crashing, or silently doing nothing. **Not verified live: an actual successful compression round-trip** (begin → real progress → a genuinely smaller output file → a real "save as" dialog → a byte-identical exported copy) — no real video file was available in this isolated instance to produce one, and this gap is disclosed honestly rather than assumed to work from the gating/error-path checks above, though the underlying `video_transcode_begin`/`_progress`/`lookup`/`export_file` machinery each already has its own live verification elsewhere in this file (the Convert-for-compatible-playback feature immediately above, and the Filen-cache/local-file export commands `export_file` is shared with). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, and `cargo test --lib` (276 tests, 1 ignored, run single-threaded to avoid the known pre-existing keychain-access race) all passing, both after this change.

## Compressing audio to MP3 (`audio_transcode.rs`)

**"Just like we did the compression feature for videos, let's do a similar compression feature for audio
files: convert lossless (or other large) audio files (like aac, flac, etc) to mp3."** Built as a deliberate
mirror of `video_transcode.rs`'s own "Compress and save a copy…" half — see that section above for the full
pipeline reasoning — rather than a second design: `audio_transcode.rs` has the identical shape (a `Job`/
`AudioTranscodeJobs` pair, `address_of`/`lookup`, `audio_transcode_begin`/`_progress`/`_status`/`_delete`/
`_export`), a sibling cache folder (`layout.rs`'s `FILES_AUDIO_TRANSCODES_FOLDER`, `"n"`, beside `m` — the
same `versioned_cache_file`/`files_cache.rs`'s `n_path`/`n_done`/`n_lookup`/`n_delete` and
`local_branches.rs`'s `local_n_*` counterparts, mirroring `m_*`/`local_m_*` exactly, extension `"mp3"`
instead of `"mp4"`), and the identical `/@audio-transcode/<root>/<path>` serving address
(`notes_pages.rs`'s `Special::AudioTranscode`, parsed and served the same way as `VideoTranscode`).

**Unlike the video module, there is no "does this already play here" question — this is purely a
file-size tool.** Every audio format this app recognizes at all (`lib/media.ts`'s `mediaKindOf`'s `audio`
kind: mp3/m4a/aac/wav/oga/ogg/opus/flac) already decodes in an ordinary `<audio>` element, so there's no
`audio_playability` probe and no "convert so it plays" button — only the begin/progress/status/delete/
export quintet, used the same way video's own compress-only flow already does. **`.mp3` files themselves
are excluded from the gate** (`NotesApp.tsx`'s `localAudioRoot`, checked by extension before anything else):
compressing an already-MP3 file to MP3 has nothing to gain, and the ask was specifically about a *lossless
or otherwise large* source.

**The pipeline** (`platform::convert`, Windows-only) is the audio half of `video_transcode.rs`'s own
`convert` with the video stream and its encode side removed entirely: `MFCreateSourceReaderFromURL` opens
the file, the first actual audio stream is found (`find_audio_stream`, the audio-only sibling of
`find_streams`) and decoded to PCM via `SetCurrentMediaType` — the same format-agnostic decoder-side trick:
whatever codec the stream is actually in, Media Foundation's own topology resolver finds a decoder MFT for
it. The output type is picked from `MFTranscodeGetAudioOutputAvailableTypes(&MFAudioFormat_MP3, …)`
(`pick_mp3_output_type`, the identical "ask the real encoder MFT what it actually supports" pattern
`pick_aac_output_type` already uses) — Windows' own built-in **MP3 Audio Encoder** MFT
(`CLSID_MP3ACMCodecWrapper`, present since Windows 8; a Server edition without the "Desktop Experience"
component may lack it, the same caveat the AAC encoder already has, confirmed via Microsoft's own
documentation before writing a line of this module). `MF_TRANSCODE_CONTAINERTYPE` is set explicitly to
`MFTranscodeContainerType_MP3` (both constants confirmed present in the installed `windows` crate before
using them) for the identical reason `video_transcode.rs` already sets its own container type explicitly:
the real output path is a `.part` file first, whose extension Media Foundation can't infer a container
from. A source with no audio track at all is refused outright (there's nothing for this module to do with
a video-only file — compressing *its* audio would need the video re-muxed back in, which is a different,
unasked-for feature).

**`hstring_url` is duplicated, not shared** — the same extended-length-path fix `video_transcode.rs` already
found and fixed live (`fs_scope.rs` hands back `std::fs::canonicalize`'s `\\?\`-prefixed form, which Media
Foundation's URL parser misreads as a UNC share) is needed here too, but the original is a private function
inside `video_transcode.rs`'s own `platform` module — duplicating four lines was simpler and more honest
than threading a new `pub(crate)` export through a module that otherwise has nothing to do with audio.

**Wired into Notes' File Manager and Note Files Explorer the same way video's own button is** — a single,
generalized `compressAndExport(entry)` in `NotesApp.tsx` now dispatches to either `videoTranscode*` or
`audioTranscode*` by the entry's own `mediaKindOf` kind (rather than two near-identical copies of the
begin/poll/export loop), so the existing progress modal, the `compressDismissed` "keep running in the
background" behavior, and the save-location flow are exactly one implementation shared by both kinds. The
**"Compress and save a copy…"** entry appears in both the thumbnail grid's card menu and the list view's
row actions, gated by `mediaKindOf(entry.name) === 'audio' && localAudioRoot(entry)` — the audio-specific
sibling of `localVideoRoot`, with the same `UserFolder`/`DeviceFolder`-only (never a Filen account) limit.

**No dedicated Rust test for the five commands themselves** — the same precedent `video_transcode_export`
already established: every constituent call (`confirm_export`, `lookup`, `export_file`, the cache's own
`n_*`/`local_n_*` functions) is independently tested elsewhere. Rust-tested (`audio_transcode.rs`): the
served address is the same `/@device/`-shaped form, every time, never a cache-internal name
(`the_served_address_is_the_same_shape_device_paths_already_have`); the extended-length path prefix is
stripped correctly (`the_extended_length_prefix_is_stripped_before_building_the_url`, re-verifying the fix
against this module's own duplicated copy). `files_cache/local_branches.rs`:
`compressed_audio_is_cached_like_converted_video_and_goes_with_the_root` — the exact mirror of
`converted_videos_are_cached_like_thumbnails_and_go_with_the_root` (cache, replace-on-new-version, delete,
forget-the-root-drops-it) — and `local_drop_thumbnails` extended to also remove the `n` folder, confirmed
by this same test. `notes_pages.rs`: `/@audio-transcode/<root>/<path>` parses the identical shape
`/@device/`/`/@video-transcode/` already do.

**Verified live (isolated debug data folder, desktop, real CDP, the full command→UI stack through a real
Notes File Manager window — not a hand-called backend function)**: a `fake-audio.flac` (36 bytes of plain
text, not a real audio stream — no real audio file or encoder was available in this environment to produce
one), a `fake-audio.mp3` (35 bytes, same shape), and a `plain.txt`, created directly in the debug instance's
`user` folder. The card menu correctly showed **"Compress and save a copy…" for the `.flac` only**
(`View, Edit as text, Details, Export, Compress and save a copy…, Select, Copy, Cut, Rename, Delete`) and
**correctly omitted it for both the `.mp3`** (`View, Edit as text, Details, Export, Select, Copy, Cut,
Rename, Delete` — confirming the "already MP3" exclusion works, not just "not an audio file") **and the
`.txt`** (`Open, Details, Export, Select, Copy, Cut, Rename, Delete`). Clicking "Compress and save a copy…"
on the fake `.flac` opened the progress modal and, within about a second, surfaced "This file can't be
opened: The byte stream type of the given URL is unsupported." with a working **Close** button — Media
Foundation correctly refusing content that isn't a real audio stream, the identical error shape
`video_transcode.rs`'s own fake-file test already found for video. **Not verified live: an actual successful
compression round-trip** (begin → real progress → a genuinely smaller output `.mp3` → a real "save as"
dialog → a byte-identical exported copy) — no real audio file was available in this isolated instance,
disclosed honestly rather than assumed to work from the gating/error-path checks above; the underlying
`local_n_path`/`local_n_done`/`local_n_lookup`/`export_file` machinery is independently Rust-tested above,
and the identical begin/poll/export loop is the one already live-verified for video (which hit the same
"no real source file" limitation there too). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, `cargo build` and
`cargo test --lib` (280 tests, 2 ignored, run single-threaded) all clean throughout.

## The config file of the folder pairs and the notes (`config/folder-pairs-and-notes.json`)

**Every constant of the folder pairs and the notes is in one file**, `csdrive-webhost-tauriapp/config/folder-pairs-and-notes.json`: the folder pairs' `.keep` file and its content (`-`), the temporary prefix (`t_`), the longest name part (100), the storage provider named in a cached account's folder (`filen`), the default numbering (what the Filen cache's accounts and branches use: no prefix, from `001` up, three digits, first free), the notes' file names (`[note].json`, `[note-children].json`, `[note-book].json`, `[user-action].json`), the markdown's prefix and suffix (`0-`, `[note].md`), **the five numberings of the note items and sections** — note items `999`→`401` (after the largest), primary sections `199`→`111`, secondary `299`→`201`, ternary `399`→`301`, and the **Note Archive Section** `109`→`101` — and the internals' pairs (`01` the note's files, `02` its internals, `03` the notebook's own; two digits, a constant each, with the name of the full folder).

**It is compiled in**, not read from the device: the Rust backend embeds it (`src-tauri/src/config.rs`, `include_str!`; `folder_pairs::keep_file()`, `temporary_prefix()`, `max_name_part_chars()` and `config::DEFAULT_NUMBERING` read it) and the frontend bundles the same file (`src/lib/appConfig.ts`, a JSON import: `KEEP_FILE`, `TEMPORARY_PREFIX`, `NOTE_JSON`, `NOTE_ITEMS`…), so it is inside every installer and APK, one file serves both languages, and nothing on a person's machine can change what a name means. To change a constant: edit the file and rebuild. `config::validate` (run at the first use and by a test, so a bad file stops the build) checks that the numberings of the note items and sections don't overlap, that a prefix doesn't start with a digit and is a valid file name, that an index fits its digits, that an internal pair is a constant of two digits with a name, and that the names are file names. **A new constant about folder pairs or notes goes in this file**, not in the code.

## User Action (Notes)

**What it is** (`docs/strategies/note-custom-actions.md` is the person's brief; `user_action.rs`, `userAction.ts`, `UserActionButton.tsx`, `UserActionModal.tsx`, `userActionScope.ts`): a web page of the person's own — an `.html` file of any source: the user folder, a picked folder, a Filen account — that Notes launches **in a window of its own**. **The control is two icon buttons and nothing else** (`UserActionButton`): **launch** (⚡) and **close** (⚡ crossed out, on only while the window is open). It is in the header of **every page of Notes**, and the same two sit **beside the "…" clipboard button of every text box** of Notes (`TextFieldMenu`'s `extra`, `UserActionFieldButtons`: one strip; pressing them doesn't take the focus from the box). A right click or a long press on the launcher opens the dialog (`UserActionModal`) that **chooses / changes / resets** the page and **explains** all this; a launch with no page chosen opens that dialog. The dialogs live in one place, `UserActionDialogs`, mounted once (the strip at a box is gone the moment the box loses the focus).

**One reused window per Notes window** (`user_action.rs`). The first launch opens it (`notes_pages::open_child_window`, listed under the Notes tab that launched it — an ordinary `user` window with `origin.role = UserAction`, which is also how the backend finds it again: no registry to lose at a restart; the init answer says `role: 'UserAction'`, `openedBy: 'NotesApp'`); the next launches **bring it to the front** (`window_host::focus`) and tell it again; it is never closed for going out of scope. **Close** (`user_action_close`) closes it and removes its entry. A window shown for another page than the one chosen now is replaced; a *suspended* one (its Notes window was suspended) is reopened. It counts as one of the app's windows — **the limit of ten** (`window_host::MAX_OPEN_WINDOWS`) applies, with its warning box. The Notes window is told whether it is open (`user-action-state` `{ open }`; `user_action_status` at start) so the buttons show it.

**The events** the page may listen to or ignore (`TabLib.userAction` in the samples; `getCurrentWebviewWindow().listen`):
- `user-action-launched` `{ resourceId, context, launchedAt }` — every launch. `resourceId` is the resource identifier of the Notes tab that launched it (`system:notes?…`), `context` says where from: `{ kind: 'button' }`, or for a text box `{ kind: 'input', fieldId, element, type, readOnly }` (`describeField`). **`fieldId` is a stable identifier, distinct for every text box of Notes** — its `data-ua-field` attribute (`notes.search.name`, `notes.notebook.title`, `notes.fileManager.rename`… one on every `<input>`/`<textarea>` of Notes; a box that was never given one reports `"notes.field"`, itself distinct from every real one) — so the page can tell *which* box asked without ever learning *what is in it*: not its label, its selection nor its length. **The bridge for actual text is the app's own clipboard, which the person controls**: they copy the selection to it from the box's “…” menu, the page may read it (`TabLib.internalClipboard`), and what the page puts there they paste back (every text box of Notes has both). The whole context is at most 256 KiB. **A window that was closed is opened first and the event waits for the page's `init_window_tab`** (`user_action::page_ready`, sent 250 ms later so the page can attach its listeners) — so, in effect, a launch's `fieldId` reaches the page as soon as it has completed its init request; a page that missed the event asks `user_action_context` (the last launch it was told of; only the User Action window itself gets one).
- `user-action-scope-left` `{ resourceId, now }` — the person went elsewhere in Notes, so what opened it is out of scope, **once** per launch (`userActionScope.ts`: every place Notes reports — `reportLocation`, `reportView`, `reportNotePlace`, a tab switch — passes the new resource identifier; `o=`, how far down a listing, is not a change). The window stays open: what it does about it (its default state) is the page's own.

**Where the choice is kept** is unchanged. Inside a listed notebook (`notebookContaining`: the notebook whose folder is the place or above it, the innermost when they are nested) it is the **notebook's**, kept in the notebook itself: `{ "Page": { "SourceId", "Path" } }` in `03/[user-action].json`, in the notebook's own internals pair (`03` + `03-[note-book]`, from the config file; made with its `.keep` when the notebook has none — `ensureNotebookInternals`), in the notebook's own source, so it goes with the notebook. Anywhere else — the home page, the notebooks and settings pages, a folder outside every notebook — it is **global**: the Notes app's state, key `notes.userAction` (`data.db`). *Reset* deletes the file (the pair stays) or clears the key. A damaged file says so in the dialog; it is never taken for "nothing chosen". Which place a launch is at is worked out from the resource identifier (`placeOfResource`), so the header buttons and the box buttons agree.

**Why a window and not a frame — the security comparison.** The earlier design showed the page in an iframe of the Notes window, which needed a relay (`postMessage`) so that the framed page could call the backend *as Notes* (a window's identity is the window's, not a frame's), a script injected into every web page, the CSP's `frame-src`, and the guard that refuses IPC from a frame of another origin. None of that is left (`frame-bridge.js`, `userActionRelay.ts`, `FRAME_SCRIPT_PATH`, the `frame-src` directive: removed). The page now has **its own identity** — its own `caller_guid`, its own app state (kept under its own path, so `get_app_state('notes.userAction')` in it is *its* state and Notes' is unreachable), no window state of Notes' — and exactly the rights every window has (nothing above them exists). It can no longer pass itself off as Notes, and Notes' own state can no longer be reached even by a page written to bypass a relay. What it costs: it is one more window (the limit of ten), and it is an OS window the person can see, move and close. (The IPC `Origin` check in `window_host::identify` stays as a general guard.) Exports it asks for are confirmed by the person as everywhere.

**Tested** live on desktop (isolated data folder): the guid, the two buttons in the header, a first launch (a window opened, listed under the Notes tab, the page told — `resourceId` and `kind: 'button'` — after its `init_window_tab`, its init saying `role: 'UserAction'`), its own state (it can't see Notes' setting nor window state), a second launch reusing the window and telling it again, going to the home page telling it the scope was left while the window stayed open, the buttons at a text box (the strip: clipboard menu, launch, close) with only the kind of box in the context, closing (the window gone, the Notes window told) and launching again (a new window, the event after its page started). Also on the Android emulator (isolated, fresh app data): the User Action window as its own task with the page told after it started, its `role`, its own state, **Back suspending it and the next launch opening it again and telling it once its page had started**, the scope-left event, and the ⚡ / ⚡✕ strip at a text box on a phone-width screen (it launched with the box as the context; the strip is placed by `requestAnimationFrame`, so it only appears while the Notes window is the one in front — a backgrounded Android window doesn't run it). Also on the emulator: the themes reaching an open Notes window at once and the `color-scheme` snippet, the refusal of `set_appearance` to Notes, the editor's Undo/Redo buttons, root guids, local branches (the branch bar, a window in a branch, a new window on the main view), and the orange icon and splash accent. **Not exercised live:** the cache options on a real Filen account (only the Rust tests and the fact that a folder of this device shows none), committing/discarding a local branch through the dialogs (unit tests only), and the *New branch…* prompt path of the same-window exception (the rule itself is Node-tested).


## A notebook's own viewer script

**"For any notebook let's give the user the option to load a script to be inserted in the DOM for Note
Viewer and markdown/html File Viewer pages in the scope of such notebooks. This script will be specified
by path per notebook and will be saved in a json file inside the notebook's `[notebook]` folders pair."**
**A security-relevant change — `docs/app-security.md` was read first, per the standing rule, and the
framing below was confirmed with the person before writing any code.** The script runs with **exactly the
same rights as the page itself** — not a sandboxed window, not a separate origin — the same spirit as a
note's own `<script>` tag already being allowed to run in a markdown file ("Markdown web apps" above): the
person is configuring their *own* notebook, the same trust level as hand-authoring a `<script>` tag into
one of their own files, not a new attack surface opened up to anyone else. This is a deliberate difference
from User Action, which stays a separate window with its own identity for its own, independent reasons
("Why a window and not a frame" above) — confirmed directly with the person that this script's own design
doesn't change that.

**Storage: `03/[note-viewer-script].json`, in the notebook's own internals pair** — the exact convention
`03/[user-action].json` already uses for User Action's own per-notebook page (`config/folder-pairs-and-
notes.json`'s `notes.files.noteViewerScript`, `config.rs`'s `NoteFilesConfig.note_viewer_script`,
`appConfig.ts`'s `NOTE_VIEWER_SCRIPT_JSON`). `{ "Script": { "SourceId", "Path" } }` — the script file itself
can be anywhere the person points to (this folder, another one, a Filen account), not necessarily the
notebook's own source, the same as a User Action page can be. `system/notes/noteViewerScript.ts` mirrors
`userAction.ts`'s own per-notebook half exactly (`readNotebookScript`/`saveNotebookScript`/
`resetNotebookScript`, `ensureNotebookInternals`). **Notebook-only — there is no "global" script outside
every notebook** (unlike User Action's own global fallback): the ask was specifically "for any notebook."

**Delivery: resolved server-side, fresh on every page load, through the existing code-snippets mechanism
— no new plumbing through the many "open a file as a web app" commands.** Notebooks are a frontend-only
concept (`notes.notebooks`, in Notes' own `app_state`); rather than threading a resolved script reference
through every one of `open_file_as_web_app`/`open_note_tab`/`open_related_web_app`/a link followed inside a
page (which would need a race-free way to attach it before the page's own first `init_window_tab` call, and
would still miss the link-followed case), the new `note_viewer_script.rs` module reads `notes.notebooks`
directly **by SQL**, from the one choke point every tab's `codeSnippets` already passes through
(`secondary_windows::fill_origin`) — called from `init_window_tab`, `add_window_tab` and `activate_tab`'s
`tab-navigate` event alike, with no new command and no change to any "open as web app" signature. This also
means a link followed *inside* a page, into a different notebook (or out of every notebook), picks up the
*new* notebook's script for free the next time that tab's `codeSnippets` are built — `fill_origin` already
re-derives the shown page's real location on every call, link-following included.

`fill_origin` already computes, by the time this runs, `response.storage`/`response.relative_path`/
`response.filen` (plus the window's own `origin.root`, in scope) — the file's **current** location,
whichever way it got there (a plain open, Filen, a picked folder, or a link followed inside the page).
`note_viewer_script::snippet_for` turns that into a frontend-style `source_id` (`local:user`,
`local:<rootId>`, `filen:<userId>` — never a branch, the same restriction `notebooks.ts`'s own `sourceId`
already has) and the file's folder, mirrors the frontend's own `notebookContaining` (`pick_notebook`, a pure
function, Node-for-Rust tested directly — the innermost listed notebook whose folder contains the place,
both trimmed of leading/trailing slashes before comparing, so a Filen path's leading `/` never throws the
match off), and — if one is found and has a script configured — reads the config file and then the script
itself (`read_from_source`: `FsScope::check_in` + `std::fs::read` for `local:`, `filen_cache::prepare` +
`Cache::read` for `filen:`, the same primitives `fs_commands.rs`/`filen_cache.rs` already use) and appends
it to the tab's `codeSnippets` as a `javascript` entry. **Never for the User Action page** (`response.role
!= user_action::ROLE`, checked before the lookup) — a deliberate exclusion, confirmed with the person: User
Action's own `Origin` *does* carry real storage/path (unlike the "one reused window" `NoteWebApp` role,
which never does, since its underlying file changes across launches without a new window), so without this
check a User Action page whose chosen file happens to sit inside a notebook's folder would also get the
notebook's own viewer script injected — a different mechanism with its own separate design, never meant to
be affected by this one.

**Never an error — a missing notebook, a missing config file, or an unreadable script is simply "no extra
snippet," the same as the page having nothing more to apply.** `app: Option<&AppHandle>` is `None` only for
`navigation_payload`'s own `tab-navigate` event (`activate_tab`'s own ordinary reaction is a full
`location.reload()` anyway, which calls `init_window_tab` fresh, with `app` present, and picks the script up
there) — the one, already-disclosed gap, mirroring how `fill_origin`'s own pre-existing Filen-email lookup
already degrades for the identical `app: None` case.

**UI: a new per-notebook row action, "Set its viewer script…"** (`NotebooksPage.tsx`, beside "Show its
folder in the file manager" — offered for both file-based and folder-based notebooks alike, unlike "Change
its title," since a script applies regardless of the notebook's own kind), opening `NoteViewerScriptModal.tsx`
— choose / change / reset, and "What is this?", the same shape `UserActionModal.tsx` already has.
**`LocationPicker.tsx` gained a fourth mode, `'file'`** (alongside `'folder'`/`'notebook'`/`'page'`): picks
any file, not just an `.html`/`.htm` page — needed since the script is a `.js` file, which `mode="page"`'s
own `isPageFileName` filter would have refused.

**The one-time explanatory note, asked for and confirmed directly** ("yes, a one-time explanatory note...
shown in the picker dialog itself... before the first script is chosen"): a plain, non-blocking
`warning-banner` in the modal's own body — "This script runs with the same rights as the page itself... Only
choose a script you wrote yourself, or trust completely" — shown **whenever no script is yet configured for
this notebook** (not tracked as a separately-dismissible "seen it once, ever" flag; it disappears the
instant a script is chosen and doesn't come back unless the notebook's script is reset) — the modal's own
"What is this?" button has the fuller explanation for afterward.

**A real finding, confirmed live, not assumed**: a note's markdown page and a plain, hand-authored `.html`
file behave differently here for the same reason they already do for every *other* code snippet
("Android"'s own "Web apps must apply the snippets they receive") — a bare `.html` file with no script of
its own never calls `init_window_tab` in the first place, so it has nothing to apply a snippet *to*; a page
that does call it (a note's own markdown, rendered server-side with `init_window_tab` baked into its
bootstrap, or a hand-authored page that includes the ordinary `TabLib`-style bootstrap) picks the injected
script up exactly the same way it already picks up every other snippet (`SAFE_AREA_CSS`, the top-bar CSS).
This is not a gap in the feature — it's the pre-existing contract every code snippet already has, and this
feature rides on top of it rather than inventing a second delivery mechanism.

Rust-tested (`note_viewer_script.rs`): `folder_of` strips a leading Filen slash and the file name correctly;
the innermost containing notebook wins over an outer one, and a root-folder notebook (`folder: ""`) matches
everything else in that source; a different source never matches; a leading or trailing slash on either
side of the comparison never throws the match off, and a sibling folder sharing a name prefix doesn't
falsely match. Verified live (isolated **nvscript-test** scratch data folder — a dedicated `DEFAULT_DATA_
FOLDER` **and** a separate `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER`, both reverted afterward and confirmed by a clean
`git diff` on `layout.rs`; desktop, real CDP, through the real backend command stack — `fs_write_file`,
`open_file_as_web_app`, `user_action_launch` — not a hand-called Rust function): a folder-based test
notebook (`nvtest`) with a real markdown note, a real plain `.html` file and a real self-bootstrapping
`.html` file (calling `init_window_tab` and applying its own `codeSnippets` the CSP-safe way — appending a
real `<script>` element, **not** `eval`, which this app's CSP explicitly disallows and which a first test
attempt wrongly used, producing a false negative before this was caught and corrected) inside it, its own
`03/[note-viewer-script].json` pointing at a real `.js` file that sets `window.__nvScriptRan` and
`document.title` — opening the markdown note and the bootstrapped `.html` file as web apps **both** ran the
script correctly; a bare, non-bootstrapping `.html` file correctly did **not** (confirmed, after the eval
mistake was caught and fixed, to be the pre-existing "a page must call `init_window_tab` to get any snippet"
contract, not a gap); a file **outside** the notebook, opened the same way, correctly got nothing; editing
the script's content and opening the note **again** (a second, independent window) correctly ran the
**new** content while the **first**, already-open window kept running the old one it had already loaded —
confirming the "re-read fresh on every load, no caching" design directly, not merely asserting it;
`user_action_launch` on the same bootstrapped `.html` file (inside the same notebook) correctly got **no**
injected script, confirming the User Action exclusion. **Not exercised live**: Android (the mechanism is
server-side Rust resolution with no platform-specific code path, so nothing here is expected to differ) and
a link followed *inside* a page from one notebook's scope into another's (the `fill_origin` re-derivation
this relies on is the same, already-tested mechanism "In this tab / in a new tab" above already proves
correct for the *storage*/path side of a link; this feature's own notebook lookup on top of it wasn't
separately click-verified for that specific case). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, `cargo build` and
`cargo test --lib` (286 tests, 2 ignored) all clean throughout.


## Prompts and windows: the rules of `docs/app-security.md` (`prompt_guard.rs`, `window_host::MAX_OPEN_WINDOWS`)

**Read `docs/app-security.md` before widening what a page or a window may do**, and put a warning about any of its requirements a change touches in the final reply. What it asks, and what implements it:

- **Prompts can't be abused by a page.** Every native box the app shows goes through `prompt_guard` (`window_host::choose` *is* `prompt_guard::ask`; the raw box is `choose_unguarded`, called only from there): **one prompt at a time, in a queue the app itself keeps** — the OS queues nothing, it shows what it is told to on top of each other, so a prompt is handed to it only when the one before has been answered (`Guard::take_turn`: a turn held while a box shows; at most 8 wait, one more is refused; nobody waits more than 60 s; the ones waiting are dropped when the person prevents prompts) —, and a box nobody answers (its window can't be seen) gives up after 120 s so it can't hold the rest back for ever. The OS's own file dialogs a window can ask for (the folder picker, "save as") take their turn too (`os_dialog`).
- **"Prevent this app from showing prompts"** is an **in-memory flag, `false` at every start, that only a full restart of the app clears**, and **every kind of prompt can reach it**: a box with two choices carries it as a third button, *between* the choice and *Cancel* (a box that is closed answers as its last button, so closing is still *Cancel*); and the queue **counts every prompt of any kind** — a one-button box, a three-button link box, an OS file dialog, a page's own dialog — and **five within 30 s make the next one be preceded by a box that asks only that** (*Keep showing prompts* / *Prevent this app from showing prompts* / *Cancel*; a native box has at most three buttons, and this one uses them all), the mark of a page that keeps asking (*Keep showing* starts the count again). While the flag is set nothing is shown: a command that needs a prompt rejects with "Prompts are prevented until the app is restarted." (`choose_save_location`, `pick_folder`, `open_external_site`, an Android export), and the person's own destructive confirmations in Notes answer *no* — that is the price of the option, and the person restarts the app to get them back. Settings shows the state and how many windows are open (`prompt_guard_status`, admin only).
- **A page's own `alert`/`confirm`/`prompt`.** On **Android** they are Kotlin dialogs (`WindowActivity`): they ask the backend first (`nativeDialogBegin`/`nativeDialogEnd`: the same turn and the same flag; a page's thread can't wait, so one that finds the turn taken is cancelled), are **titled "A web page says"** so it is plain that the *page* is talking, and carry the option. On **desktop (Windows, WebView2)** — `page_dialogs_windows.rs`, installed in every secondary window and every external web site's window — three things: (1) Tauri's dialog plugin replaces `window.alert`/`window.confirm` in the main frame of every window with async calls to *its own* commands (boxes outside these rules, and commands no window is granted any more), so `DIALOGS_INIT_SCRIPT` (`code_snippets.rs`, after the plugin's) puts the app's own **`alert_dialog`** and **`confirm_dialog`** in their place — native boxes that say who asks, wait their turn and can prevent prompts; they keep Tauri's shape (`alert` doesn't wait, `confirm` returns a Promise of the answer, `false` when refused); (2) `prompt` — and every dialog a *frame* raises (the plugin's script isn't in frames) — is the webview's own, so the webview's own dialogs are **turned off** (`AreDefaultScriptDialogsEnabled = false`) and the `ScriptDialogOpening` event is answered from Rust: the dialog waits its turn, is counted, never shows once prompts are prevented, and is shown in **a window of the app** (`system/prompt/index.html`, label `prompt-…`, over the window it belongs to; not counted among the ten) that says who talks *as the app knows it* (`"ua/other.html" says`, `A page shown in "Notes" says`, `The web site "host" says` — never a title the page chose), shows the message and the default as plain text (never markup), gives a `prompt` its text box, has *OK* / *Cancel* and the option to prevent prompts, and treats closing it as *Cancel*; (3) `beforeunload` is accepted without asking. **macOS and Linux** webviews are not covered. A window's yes/no questions are `confirm_dialog`'s (`lib/dialogs.ts`'s `confirm` — Notes uses it; **the caller supplies only the question** (at most 1 500 characters): the box's title says who asks — the window's page as the app knows it, `"Notes" (or a page it shows)` for a system app's window, `prompt_guard::asker_label` — and its buttons are always *OK* and *Cancel*), the dialog plugin's `dialog:allow-confirm` being the admin-app's alone (`admin.json`). The bridge no longer offers a page a native dialog of its own (`CsdriveBridge.dialog` and `plugin:dialog|*` are gone on Android).
- **At most 10 secondary windows are open at once** (`MAX_OPEN_WINDOWS`, checked in `window_host::open` — the one place every window opens through, including an external web site's — and so for opening, reopening, a link's *new tab* that needs a window, `open_note_tab` with `new_window`…). At the limit nothing opens: the caller gets "10 windows are open already. Close or suspend one to open another." and the person a native box saying so (in the window that called last, else the admin-app's; `LAST_CALLER` is used only to choose where the warning shows, never to decide anything). A window that is showing is only brought forward, never refused. Suspending or closing one makes room. The external web sites' windows count too (on desktop they are windows like the rest; on Android their activities are counted from `external_sites`).
- **Not done** (the file says "maybe"): suspending every secondary window and freeing the admin-app's memory when the RAM gets critical.

Tested (Rust: the flag, the queue — one at a time, the short queue, dropped when prompts get prevented, the Kotlin turn —, the burst rule, the limit, the origin check; live on desktop and the Android emulator, isolated data): ten windows open and the eleventh refused with the box (Android: counting the external sites), a close making room, suspend/reopen, two windows asking at once with the second waiting for the first's answer, the third button in a two-choice box, *Prevent* setting the flag and every prompt kind refused after it (the confirmations, "save as", the folder picker, an Android export, a page's alert), a restart clearing it, the box before the fifth prompt in a row — one-button boxes counted —, and, on desktop, a page's `alert`/`confirm`/`prompt` (the boxes and the prompt window: who talks, plain-text message, text box and default, OK/Cancel/closing, no markup injection, a page in Notes' frame).

## Branches of a folder of this device (the File Manager's branches)

`files_cache/local_branches.rs` (a child of `files_cache.rs`), `local_branch_commands.rs`, `picked_roots::root_guid_of`, `sources.ts` (`BranchApi`, `localSource(root, branch)`, `filenBranchApi`, `localBranchApi`). The File Manager works on the user folder and on every picked folder in **branches** just as on a Filen account: changes (files written, folders made, deletions, files checked out) stay in the app's own folder until they are **committed** (applied to the real folder, checked against what the folder looks like now — a file that changed underneath is a conflict, refused unless forced) or **discarded**. Reading through a branch shows the real folder with the branch's changes on top. **There is no cache**: the real folder is always the base and is read as it is (through `FsScope::check_in`, so links out of the root and the app's own data are refused here too); the only things kept are the changed files — `files/a/NNN/b/MMM/<the folder's layout>`, inside the root's own folder in `a` — and, in `files/data.db`, `local_branches` and `local_branch_changes` (path, kind, and the version — size and modification time — each change is based on). A folder can't be renamed inside a branch (files can), as for Filen. The branch bar (a select, *New branch…*, the changes, *Commit*, *Discard*, *Check out* on a file, *New branch…* in the editor) is the one Filen has, through one `BranchApi` for both; only the cache's own row (interval, clear) is Filen's.

**Root guids.** Every root — the user folder and each picked folder — has a **guid**, made the first time it is asked for and kept in `data.db` (`root_guids`, keyed by the root's opaque id, so it lasts as long as the id does — a picked folder picked again has the same). It is what the app's own folders for the root are named after: **`local-fs@@<guid>`**, as a Filen account's are `filen@@<email>@@<id>`. It is shown on the root pills' **context menu** (right click, long press — *Show its ids…*: the guid, the root id and the name, each copyable; a Filen account shows its email and user id) in Notes and in the admin-app's Files tab (where it is also a field of the root's details). The layout, each pair with its readable half beside it:

```
files/a/NNN-local-fs@@<guid>/     only a .keep: says whose NNN is
files/a/NNN/b/MMM/…               what branch MMM of the root has changed (MMM-<branch name> beside it)
files/a/NNN/t/…                   thumbnails of the root's files      (kept: they used to be made again each session)
files/a/NNN/tb/MMM/…              thumbnails of the files branch MMM changed (goes with the branch)
```

A folder of this device sits in `a` beside the Filen accounts — the same numbering, told apart only by its part (`local-fs@@…` rather than
`filen@@…`) — so its branches and thumbnails live inside its own `NNN` exactly as an account's do.

Committing or discarding deletes the branch's `b` and `tb` pairs (the root's own pair in `b` goes with its last branch); **forgetting a root deletes its thumbnails but keeps its branches** (with their pending changes; they come back with the folder and its id). A page opened from a branch view is served **as that branch has it**: the address is `/@device/<root id>~<branch>/<path>` (`notes_pages::split_root`; the user folder is `user~<branch>`), so links, the media viewer and *Open as a web app* follow the branch. Unit-tested (`local_branches::tests`: overlay, layout on disk, commit, conflicts, checkouts, versions and rebase, renames, discard, thumbnails) and live on desktop.

**Per window.** The branch a Notes window works in is **the window's**: kept in the window's own state (`get_window_state`/`set_window_state`, `window_state` in `data.db`, deleted with the window's entry and kept while it is suspended), one branch per source (`notes.branches`, `windowBranch.ts`). Changing it in one window doesn't change it in another; **a new window starts on the main view**; every tab of a window shares it (a tab switched to takes the window's branch — the branch in a place's own resource identifier only counts in a scoped explorer). The "last place" the app remembers no longer carries a branch to other windows. **A change of the window's branch is refused when another tab of the same window holds unsaved edits** (drafts, `notes.drafts`) to a file that isn't the same in the two views (the branch it was edited in or the one the window is going to has changed it: `draftsInTheWay`) — the message names the files: save or discard them in that tab first. **The exception**: from the main view to a branch that has just been made (*New branch…*, also from the editor) is never refused: a new branch starts as the main view, so what the other tab has open carries on into it (a draft comes back in whichever branch the window is in now, with a note). Commit and discard follow the same rule (they leave the branch). Unsaved text is never lost by switching tabs: it is kept as a draft when the tab is left and comes back with the editor.

## Cache options of one item (Notes)

For everything the cache holds — a Filen account's files and folders — every place the Notes app shows an item offers, through `CacheMenu.tsx` (a button opening a menu, and `cacheMenuItems` for the context menus): **Soft refresh** (show it again from the cache: what is cached is used while valid, fetched when it has expired — the ordinary read again), **Hard refresh** (`filen_cache_hard_refresh`: fetch it again from Filen and replace what the cache holds — a file's version and content; a folder's listing, with everything below it looked at again when opened) and **Clear its cache** (`filen_cache_clear_item`: content, listings and thumbnails of the item — of everything below a folder — thrown away, fetched again when needed). **Files locked against caching keep their frozen copy** (a hard refresh of one refuses; clearing skips it). **A file that is being edited can't be hard-refreshed**, and can't be soft-refreshed while it has unsaved changes (the menu says why). Where: the rows of the File Manager and the Note Files Explorer (list and thumbnail cards' menus), search results (files and notes), the notes lists (a note's short folder), the notebooks list (the notebook's folder), the two editors (the File Manager's popup and the note page) and the media viewer's bar (`renderCache`; the media loads again with a `?r=N` on its address). **The folder shown itself**, not just its rows, has the same three options: the File Manager's and the Note Files Explorer's toolbar and a notes page's header each carry a `CacheMenu` for the current folder (`source`, the current path, `isDirectory`) beside the plain *Refresh* button (a soft refresh of the listing, which still works for a folder of this device) — so a stale or discarded Filen listing can be force-refreshed or its cache cleared without picking a single row to do it through. A folder of this device has no cache, so it has none of them — `CacheMenu` renders nothing when `source.cache` is absent, at every level. Rust-tested with the in-memory remote (`hard_refresh_…`, `a_locked_file_refuses_…`, `clearing_one_item_…`); the toolbar-level additions verified live on desktop (isolated data folder): absent for a local folder's listing (File Manager and notes list alike), present and wired to the same component already verified against a real Filen account.

**A large file being fetched into the cache now shows real progress, asked for directly after the `.mkv` window-freeze investigation above** ("can we show the progress for that file (if its a large file and its currently being downloaded) in the file info popup modal" — and, confirmed in the same conversation, the media viewer's own blank screen while a large Filen file is still being fetched, which has the identical root cause: `cached_file_account`/`cached_file_account_unlocked` download the *whole* file into the cache before any of it can be served, however the player asks for it — see "Media: the viewer…" below — so a multi-gigabyte file can leave either surface looking stuck with no explanation). A new `download_progress` method on `Cache` (`files_cache.rs`) answers bytes-fetched-so-far for one `(user_id, path)`, backed by a small `HashMap<(i64, String), Arc<AtomicU64>>` (`download_progress`, a sibling of `download_locks`): `download_file` (the one function both locked and unlocked download paths already shared) inserts an entry before it starts, updates it from the same chunk callback that already writes to the `.part` file, and removes the entry when it ends — success or failure — so "no entry" means "not downloading right now," told apart from "0 bytes so far" only by *presence*, not by the value. The command, `filen_cache_download_progress` (`filen_cache.rs`, `user-apps.json` like its siblings), and the frontend wrapper, `FileSource.cache.downloadProgress(path)` (`sources.ts`, alongside `hardRefresh`/`clear`, carried through `scopedSource` the same way), are read-only and need no new capability beyond the existing cache ones.

**Two call sites, the same polling shape** (every 500 ms while relevant, stopped on unmount): the file details popup (`DetailsModal`'s `children` slot, which already existed for exactly this kind of addition — `NotesApp.tsx`'s new `CachingProgress`, shown only for a file, only when `source.cache` exists, and rendering nothing at all unless a poll actually returns a number) and the media viewer (`MediaViewer.tsx`'s new `downloadProgress` prop, polled the same way). **The media viewer's own fix needed one real design correction, caught before it shipped**: a first version showed the progress overlay *instead of* the `<video>`/`<img>` element while downloading — but the browser's own request (the one `download_progress` is actually reporting on) only starts once that element actually renders with its `src`; hiding it would have meant nothing ever asked for the file in the first place, so `download_progress` would stay `null` forever and the overlay would never appear. Fixed by keeping the media element rendering unconditionally once its address is known, and layering the progress overlay as an absolutely-positioned sibling on top of it (`.media-downloading`, a translucent black panel) — the same shape `.media-notice` already uses, but opaque enough to hide the blank/stalled media underneath while its own (now-visible) request keeps running underneath. The overlay has its own "Back to the listing" button (`onClose`), the one plain ask from the same conversation. A shared `.progress-bar`/`.progress-bar-fill` pair (`App.css`) backs both surfaces — themed (`var(--accent)`) in the details popup, overridden to plain white (`.media-progress-bar .progress-bar-fill`) in the viewer's own always-dark overlay, which doesn't track the app's theme at all (see `MediaViewer.tsx`'s own module doc).

Rust-tested (`files_cache::tests`): `download_progress_is_reported_while_in_flight_and_cleared_once_done`, using the same `gate_next_download` test hook the account-lock-split tests above use — an entry exists (at `0`) the moment a download starts, even before any bytes have arrived, and is gone again the instant it finishes. **Not verified live**: the actual UI, on either surface, against a real in-flight Filen download — this pass had no live Filen account available in its isolated test instance (the same gap the account-lock fix above left open), so this rests on the Rust test above (which exercises the exact backend state machine the UI polls) and on the frontend's own type-checked, built-clean wiring through the identical `cache.hardRefresh`/`cache.clear` pattern already proven live elsewhere in this same file.

**A download that fails partway through used to leave nothing to explain why — reported live on Android**: caching a large Filen file stopped before finishing, with no error anywhere, while the person was looking at the file's own details popup; reopening the file in the viewer just started the whole download over from nothing, silently. Two gaps, both now closed, plus a third thing asked for in the same report as a general escape hatch:

- **A real error, with a retry.** A new `download_errors` map on `Cache` (`files_cache.rs`, `HashMap<(i64, String), String>`, a sibling of `download_progress`) records the exact error of a file's own most recent failed download: `download_file` clears any entry the moment a *new* attempt starts (so a retry never shows the previous attempt's stale error while it's running) and records one only if that attempt itself fails. `download_error(user_id, path)` answers it (`None` once nothing has failed since the last successful or still-running attempt); the command, `filen_cache_download_error`, and the frontend wrapper, `FileSource.cache.downloadError(path)`, follow `download_progress`'s own shape exactly, including through `scopedSource`. Both `CachingProgress` (the details popup) and `MediaViewer` now poll this alongside progress, every 500 ms: the details popup shows the message with a *Retry* button (`cache.hardRefresh`, already the right call — it forces a fresh fetch regardless of what's cached); the viewer shows its own error panel, the same shape as the downloading overlay, with *Retry* (reloads the media element, which naturally starts a fresh backend attempt since nothing was ever successfully cached) and *Back to the listing*.

- **The details popup's "cached" state used to go stale the moment a download finished while the popup stayed open** — `detailFields()`'s `State` field reads from `details.entry`, a snapshot taken when the popup opened, never refreshed on its own. `CachingProgress` gained an `onCached` callback, fired once — comparing each poll's `(bytes, error)` against the previous one — the instant a download that was genuinely in flight (bytes was a number) ends with no error. The caller (`NotesApp.tsx`) optimistically flips `details.entry.cached` to `true` right away and kicks off a real `load(false)` in the background, so the row the popup was opened from agrees too.

- **Manual cache-seeding — "let the user do the caching manually… like a file upload, only here the stream goes where the downloaded stream would normally go."** The escape hatch for a file whose download keeps failing: a new *Cache it manually from a file you already have…* button in the details popup (shown for every Filen file, not only after a failure — opens the device's own file chooser) hands a local copy of the file — a previous export, a copy from another device, anything — straight to where a real download would have put it, piece by piece, with **no network call at all**. `Cache::seed_begin`/`seed_push`/`seed_finish`/`seed_abort` (`files_cache.rs`, a new `SeedJob`) mirror the existing `UploadJob`/`upload_begin`/`_push`/`_finish` shape exactly — `filen_cache_seed_begin`/`_chunk`/`_finish`/`_abort` (`filen_cache.rs`, a new `SeedSessions`, literally `UploadSessions` with the Filen-specific upload type swapped for a plain local file) and `FileSource.cache.seedFromFile(path, file)` (`sources.ts`, chunked through `invokeWithBytes` exactly like `writeFromFile`) follow the identical chunked-session pattern used everywhere else in this app for a big payload. **`seed_finish` refuses — and caches nothing — if what was actually sent doesn't match the size Filen's own listing expects** for that file, so a mismatched or unrelated file can't silently become "the cache" of this one; on success it's exactly what a finished download leaves behind (the `.part` file renamed into place, `mark_cached`).

  **A real correctness gap was caught and closed before this shipped, not found live but by reasoning through the design**: a manual seed and a real download of the *same* file both write to the identical `.part` file — running at once, whichever finished last would silently clobber the other's work. `seed_begin` now takes the exact same `download_locks` entry (`files_cache.rs`'s per-`(user_id, path)` lock, already used to coalesce two concurrent downloads of the same file) and holds it for the seed job's whole lifetime (`SeedJob` owns the `OwnedMutexGuard`, released when the job finishes, is aborted, or is simply dropped) — so a seed and a download of the same file now wait for each other exactly like two downloads already did, never run concurrently, and whichever asked first wins.

Rust-tested (`files_cache::tests`): `a_failed_download_is_recorded_as_an_error_and_cleared_by_the_next_attempt` and `starting_a_new_attempt_clears_the_previous_error_even_before_the_new_one_finishes` (both using the `offline` flag on the in-memory remote, with an initial listing warmed while online first — otherwise going offline fails the *listing* step inside `cached_file_account_metadata`, before the download this is actually testing is ever reached); `manually_seeding_a_file_caches_it_without_any_download` and `seeding_the_wrong_size_is_refused_and_nothing_is_cached` (confirmed via `remote.reads` staying `0` for a correct seed, and a real network download still happening for a refused, mismatched one); `aborting_a_seed_leaves_nothing_cached`; and — the correctness fix above — `a_seed_and_a_real_download_of_the_same_file_never_run_at_once`, using the same `gate_next_download` hook: a gated download holds the lock, a concurrent `seed_begin` attempt is confirmed to not return until the gate is released (timed from a shared start point, not just "didn't crash"), proving the wait is real and not a race that happened to pass once. 263 tests total, all passing together. **Not verified live**: same gap as the progress feature above — no live Filen account in this isolated instance, so the actual UI (the error panel, the retry buttons, the manual-seed file picker and its upload) rests on these Rust tests plus the frontend's type-checked, built-clean wiring through patterns (`UploadSessions`, `invokeWithBytes`, `cache.hardRefresh`) already proven live elsewhere in this file.

**Removing a folder's cache recursively, for several entries at once, with a branch-scope choice** (CLAUDE.md's item 13: "an option to remove the cache for an entire folder... both from the listing of its parent folder and from the folder itself... for multiple files and folders at once... split into: only for the current branch / for all branches"). The single-item `clear_item` (above) already was recursive for a folder — its own doc comment: "the cached content of a file — of every file below a folder, its listing and theirs — and its thumbnails are thrown away" — and the plain `CacheMenu` already offered it both on a row of the parent listing and on the toolbar for "the folder shown itself," so neither of those needed changing. What was genuinely missing: doing it for **several selected entries at once**, and a way to also reach a **branch's own thumbnail cache** (`tb`) — which `clear_item` alone never touches, since it only ever clears the account's own, shared cache (`c`/`t`).

**Backend** (`files_cache.rs`): `BranchClearScope` (`AccountOnly | Current(i64) | All`, adjacently tagged — `{"kind":"accountOnly"}` / `{"kind":"current","branch":N}` / `{"kind":"all"}`, confirmed against a standalone throwaway crate before wiring the frontend, rather than guessing serde's exact wire shape for an enum with both unit and tuple variants) and `Cache::clear_items(user_id, paths, scope)`: calls the existing, unchanged `clear_item` once per path (so every path gets exactly the same recursive behavior a single call already has), then — only if `scope` asks for it — clears the chosen branch's (or every branch's) own thumbnail subtree under each path too, via a new `clear_branch_thumbs_below` (the branch-thumbnail counterpart of `clear_item`'s own account-thumbnail-clearing block: tries both a folder's whole mirrored subtree and a file's own entries beside it in its parent's mirror folder, defensively, since the caller doesn't have to know in advance which shape a given path is). **Never touches a branch's own pending *changes* (`b`)** — only `AccountOnly`/thumbnails ever move; deleting an uncommitted edit would be data loss, not cache-clearing, and nothing in this feature does it. `filen_cache_clear_items(user_id, paths, scope)` (`filen_cache.rs`, granted in `user-apps.json` beside its single-item sibling) is the one new command.

**Frontend** (`sources.ts`): `BranchClearScope` (the matching TypeScript type) and `FileSource.cache.clearMany(paths, scope)`, wired in the Filen source builder (`invoke('filen_cache_clear_items', ...)`) and in `scopedSource`'s cache pass-through (so the Note Files Explorer gets it for free, the same as every other cache method). **A new, general multi-select mechanism for the File Manager and Note Files Explorer** (`NotesApp.tsx`) — there was none at all before this, unlike `NotePage.tsx`'s notes list, which already has one and was the model followed here: a `selected: Set<string>` of entry names, scoped to the folder shown and cleared the moment `sourceId`/`branch`/`path` changes (the same `useEffect` that already resets `kbdFocus` for exactly this reason); a **Select/Deselect** entry added to *both* places an entry's own actions are built — `menuFor` (the thumbnail grid's card menu) and the list view's own hand-built `RowActions` array, which is a *separate* list `menuFor` was never wired into at all (a real gap caught live, not by inspection: the first version only added Select/Deselect to `menuFor`, and testing against the list view — the default view — found no way to start selecting anything, since the list view's row actions never call `menuFor`); a checkbox on every row/card once anything is selected, matching `NotePage.tsx`'s own "nothing to click until you've started" design; and a selection bar ("N selected", **Select all**, **Clear the selection**) that appears the moment `selected.size > 0`.

**The branch-scope choice itself**, reached two ways — selecting several entries and pressing a new button in the selection bar, or pressing a new toolbar button beside the plain `CacheMenu` for the folder currently shown (covering "both from the listing of its parent folder and from the folder itself") — both call one `clearCacheWithBranchChoice(paths)`: skips the dialog and clears right away with `AccountOnly` when the account has no branches at all (the two choices could never make a real difference then, so asking would be pure friction), otherwise opens a small `Modal` with two buttons — **"Only for the current branch"** (or, from the main view where there is no "current branch" to name, **"Just the account's cache"**) and **"For all branches"** — matching the two-way split asked for exactly, with the account's own cache always cleared regardless of which is pressed (said plainly in the dialog's own text, so the distinction being asked about is only ever about the thumbnail side-effect, never the baseline clear).

Rust-tested (`files_cache::tests::clear_items_clears_the_account_for_every_path_and_the_chosen_branch_scope_of_thumbnails`): the same file changed (so it has its own thumbnail) in two different branches; `AccountOnly` clears the account's content for every given path and touches neither branch's own thumbnail; `Current(one)` clears branch one's own thumbnail for the path and leaves branch two's alone; `All` clears both; and, throughout, each branch's own pending *content* change (`cache.read(..., Some(branch), ...)`) is read back unchanged at the very end, confirming nothing here ever touches a branch's own edits. 277 tests total (276 + this one), all passing.

**Verified live (isolated data folder — the debug build's own `com.ayran.csdrive-webhost-tauriapp-debug` data folder — desktop, real CDP, through the real UI)**: three plain local files created directly in the debug instance's `user` folder (a local folder has no cache at all, so this exercises the general multi-select mechanism — the part that doesn't need Filen — while the cache-clearing itself rests on the Rust test above) — the toolbar's new "Clear this folder's cache, recursively, with a branch choice…" button and the selection bar's own cache-clear button were both confirmed **correctly absent** for this local folder (`source.cache` is `undefined` there, the same gate `CacheMenu` already uses); "Select" appeared correctly in both the list view's row actions and the thumbnail grid's card menu for the same file, and pressing it opened the selection bar ("1 selected") with a checkbox now showing on every row/card; **Select all** correctly checked every entry ("4 selected"); unchecking one row's own checkbox correctly dropped the count to 3; **Clear the selection** correctly hid the bar and removed every checkbox, back to the uncluttered default. **Not verified live**: the actual cache-clearing execution and the branch-scope dialog itself — this isolated debug instance has no Filen account connected (`filen_accounts` confirmed empty by a direct query) and connecting one needs real credentials this session doesn't have — so that half rests entirely on the Rust test above, which exercises the exact `Cache::clear_items`/`clear_branch_thumbs_below` code path the command calls into, plus the frontend's type-checked, built-clean wiring through the identical `invoke`/`scopedSource` pattern already proven live for every other cache method in this file. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean throughout.

## Delete all cache for a branch

**"Let's also add a feature to delete all cache for a branch"** — asked for directly, and clarified in the
same breath once the ambiguity was raised: what to delete (the thumbnail cache **and** the cached files and
folder listings — but never the branch's own pending changes), scope (both Filen and local-device-folder
branches), and where it's reached from (the branch bar itself, next to New branch…/Commit/Discard). **Unlike
`discard_branch`/`local_discard_branch`, the branch itself is never touched** — it still exists afterward,
with its edits exactly as they were; only what's purely re-makeable goes.

**Filen** (`files_cache.rs`'s `clear_branch_cache(user_id, branch)`): `branch_exists` first (refused, not a
silent no-op, for a branch that doesn't exist — the same convention every other branch-targeted method here
already follows), then `clear_item(user_id, "/")` — the exact same recursive clear "Clear this folder's
cache…" already does from the root, since an unchanged file the branch reads through falls back to the
account's own shared cache, so clearing it is part of what "this branch's cache" means in practice even
though it isn't branch-exclusive on its own — and finally the branch's own thumbnail pair (`tb/MMM`),
removed with `NUMBERING.delete` rather than a raw `remove_dir_all` (the same convention
`delete_branch_files_and_rows`'s own `tb` handling already uses), so the marker goes with it instead of
being left orphaned. **No extra lock is taken around this** — `clear_item` already takes its own account
lock internally (a `tokio::sync::Mutex`, not reentrant — wrapping a second `self.lock()` around a call that
itself locks would deadlock, the exact class of bug this project's own clipboard `pop` once had), and
`clear_items`'s own existing scope-handling loop already has this same looseness between its `clear_item`
and `clear_branch_thumbs_below` calls, so there was no new atomicity guarantee to invent here either.

**Local** (`files_cache/local_branches.rs`'s `local_clear_branch_cache(guid, branch)`) is narrower, because a
local root has no cached *content* to begin with — "There is no cache: the real folder is always the base
and is read as it is" (this file's own earlier doc comment on local branches) — so there's nothing
equivalent to `clear_item("/")` to call: this comes down to exactly the branch's own thumbnail pair,
removed the identical `NUMBERING.delete`-based way `delete_local_branch`'s own `tb` handling already does.

**One `clearCache(branch)` method on the shared `BranchApi` interface** (`sources.ts`), not two unrelated
commands bolted on separately — the same interface the file manager, its editors and a note's page already
treat identically for list/create/changes/commit/discard, so a new branch-bar button written against
`BranchApi` works for both kinds with no per-kind branching in the UI itself. `localBranchApi` wires it to
`local_branch_clear_cache`; `filenCache.clearBranchCache` + `filenBranchApi` wire it to
`filen_cache_clear_branch_cache`. `scopedSource` (the Note Files Explorer) already sets `branches: undefined`
for a scoped source — "a scoped source has none of its own" — so this needed no change there: branch
management, cache-clearing included, only ever happens from the un-scoped File Manager view.

**The branch bar** (`NotesApp.tsx`, the row with the branch `<select>`, New branch…, Show the branch's
changes, Commit and Discard — shown for both a Filen account and a folder of this device, the same as every
button there already is) gets one more button, between Commit and Discard: **`clearBranchCache()`** asks
first (`Eraser` icon, matching the account-level "Clear the cache" button's own icon language) — worded
differently for the two kinds, since only the Filen case actually has an account-wide cache to mention:
*"Delete all cache for "name"? Every file's cached content and folder listings for the whole account, and
this branch's own thumbnails, are cleared and fetched again when needed. The branch's own changes, and
files you locked against caching, are kept."* for Filen, *"Delete all cache for "name"? Its own thumbnails
are cleared and made again when needed. The branch's own changes are kept."* for a local root — then calls
`branchApi.clearCache(branch)` and `load(false)` to refresh the current listing, the same aftermath the
account-level `clearCache()` already has. **Deliberately does *not* call `reloadBranches()`** — unlike
discarding, the branch's own change count is never affected by this action, so there's nothing about the
branch list itself that could have gone stale.

Rust-tested: `files_cache::tests::clear_branch_cache_clears_account_content_and_the_branchs_own_thumbnail_but_never_its_pending_change`
— a file cached at the account level, then changed and thumbnailed inside a branch; after `clear_branch_cache`,
the account's own cached content and thumbnail are gone, the branch's own thumbnail is gone, the branch's
pending change reads back byte-for-byte unchanged, the branch is still listed, and clearing the cache of a
branch index that doesn't exist is refused rather than silently doing nothing.
`files_cache::local_branches::tests::clear_branch_cache_clears_the_branchs_own_thumbnail_but_never_its_pending_change`
— the identical shape for a local root: the branch's own thumbnail pair is gone (confirmed on disk, not just
through the lookup command), its pending change reads back unchanged, the branch is still listed, and an
unknown branch index is refused. 282 tests total (280 + these two), all passing together.

**Verified live (isolated debug data folder, desktop, real CDP, through the real UI and a real native
confirm dialog — not a direct backend call)**: a real file written to the user folder, a real branch created
over it (`local_branch_create`), the file changed inside the branch and a fake thumbnail put into the
branch's own thumbnail cache (`local_thumb_put`) — confirmed present on disk before the test (`thumbLen: 4`,
the pending change reading back as `"changed-in-branch"`, the branch listed with `1 change`). Through the
real Notes File Manager window: selected the branch in its own `<select>`, confirmed the new button
**"Delete all cache for this branch — its changes are kept"** appears between Commit and Discard exactly as
placed; clicking it raised the real native confirm box (`"Notes" (or a page it shows) asks`, enumerated by
its own real child controls — OK / "Prevent this app from showing prompts" / Cancel, the same shape every
other system-app confirm in this app already uses) and **OK was clicked via a real `BM_CLICK` sent to the
dialog's own button handle**, not a synthetic DOM event. Afterward: the branch's own thumbnail was
confirmed gone (`thumbLen: 0`), the pending change read back **byte-for-byte unchanged**
(`"changed-in-branch"`), and the branch was still listed with its same `1 change` — the exact three-way
outcome the feature promises, observed through the real command stack a real button click drives, not
assumed from the Rust tests alone. **Not separately verified live: the Filen path** — no Filen account was
connected in this isolated instance to exercise `filen_cache_clear_branch_cache` through the real UI; that
half rests on the Rust test above (which exercises the exact `Cache::clear_branch_cache` code path the
command calls into) plus the frontend's type-checked, built-clean wiring through the identical `BranchApi`
interface and confirm-dialog pattern already proven live for the local case. `npx tsc --noEmit`,
`npm run build`, `cargo build` and `cargo test --lib` (282 tests, run single-threaded) all clean throughout.

## Appearance: themes and light/dark mode

`src/lib/themes.ts` (the catalog), `src/lib/appearance.ts`, `components/AppearanceSettings.tsx` (Settings → Appearance), `appearance.rs`. **Fifty-eight themes**, each with a light and a dark palette of the css variables `App.css` is written in (`--bg --fg --muted --border --accent --accent-fg --panel-bg --row-hover`, the editor's `--tok-*`, the error and status colours), in families: the app's own (**Ayran orange** — the default, replacing the blue —, Classic blue), **hot desert**, **scorching earth**, **tropical volcanoes**, **sunny autumn**, **rainy autumn**, **misty swamps**, **snow and mist**, **clay, rust and copper** (Red clay, Rust, Copper), **coffee and chocolate** (Arabica coffee, Chocolate), **wood** (Oak, Walnut, Birch), **metals** (Gold, Silver, Platinum, Gun metal), **jewel tones** (Ruby, Magenta, Violet, Velvet, Cherry, Pink), **teal and seaweed** (Teal, Dark teal, Kelp, Nori, Sea lettuce), **green forest** (Deep woods, Pine canopy, Mossy glade), **blue** (Cobalt, Sky, Midnight), **steel blue** (Steel blue, Cold steel, Slate harbour) and **Mars** (Red planet, Olympus dust, Martian dusk). A script checked every palette's contrast (text on the page and on panels ≥ 4.5:1, the text of an accent button ≥ 3.6:1). The mode is **follow the device / light / dark**.

**Who chooses.** **Any window may choose the theme and the mode** (`set_appearance`, granted in `user-apps.json`; the person decided a web app may; `list_themes` says which ids there are — `appearance::THEME_IDS`, which a test keeps equal to the catalog's, in the same order), and it is one choice for the whole app, kept in `global_settings`; an id that isn't a theme is refused. **Only the admin-app sets the rotation** (`set_appearance_rotation`, `admin.json`), and the generic `set_global_setting` refuses every `appearance.` key. `set_appearance` **tells every window** (`appearance-changed` `{ theme, mode, dark, rotation }`, sent to the admin-app and every open window by `window_host::emit_to_all`, never to an external site's window): the system apps apply it at once (`applyAppearance` sets the variables on the root element; `initAppearance` reads it before the first render and listens); **a web app keeps its own colours** but may listen and follow the mode, or choose the theme (`TabLib.appearance.get/set/themes/onChange`). Beside the event, a manual mode reaches every window by itself: on desktop `App::set_theme` puts it on the webviews (their `prefers-color-scheme` follows — verified live in the admin-app, Notes and a web app), and every page gets a `color-scheme` snippet (`code_snippets`, from `appearance::color_scheme_css`), so form controls and scrollbars follow. Android's WebView takes `prefers-color-scheme` from the system, so a web app there learns of a manual mode from the snippet and the event. The launcher icon and the native splash accent are orange too (`src-tauri/icons`, the Android mipmaps, `csdrive_accent`).
