direct backend call, so the whole `setEditorSetting`/`subscribeEditorSettings` notification path was
exercised, not just the persisted value): clicking the checkbox persisted `editor.highlightDisabled` to
`"1"` (confirmed via a direct `get_global_setting` read) and flipped the checkbox's own DOM `checked` state
to `true` within one settle; reopening the file afterward (navigating tabs away and back drops the Files
tab's own unsaved editing state, by existing, unrelated design — "Text editors are now dedicated pages, not
popups" already notes this editor has no draft persistence) showed `.code-editor-highlight` genuinely
absent from the DOM, the textarea's own class list carrying `code-editor-input-plain` with its computed
`color`/`-webkit-text-fill-color` both resolving to a real, opaque `rgb(26, 26, 26)` (not `transparent`),
and the gutter still rendering with exactly flat `19.5px`-apart spacing (`8px, 27.5px, 47px, 66.5px, 86px`);
a real keystroke landed correctly in the textarea (`afunction ...`) with the overlay still confirmed absent
immediately after, and no thrown error. Toggling the checkbox back off and reopening the same file showed
`.code-editor-highlight` present again, the textarea's class back to plain `code-editor-input ` (no
`-plain` suffix), and 558 real `tok-*` spans — full highlighting resumed correctly. **Not separately
verified live**: that no `Worker` object is actually constructed while disabled (there is no CDP-visible
signal for "a Worker was never created," short of instrumenting the constructor ahead of time, which wasn't
set up for this pass) — this rests on the code review of the early return at the top of the Worker-creation
effect, not a live capture.

`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean throughout; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't
apply to any of the three fixes/features in this section.

## A plain textarea for "disable all code highlighting," and a third-party editor by default (Monaco / CodeMirror)

**"I tested the editor without code highlighting. It's indeed faster, but still unusable on very large
documents. So let's make an even more dramatic change for when the user disables the code highlighting: we
should go back to using a plain html textarea with no virtualization."** The `highlightDisabled` setting above
had already dropped the colouring work, but kept this app's own windowed-DOM architecture (the gutter, the
`RunPatchController` overlay management, the scroll-window recentring) running underneath a plain-looking
textarea — real wins for a *coloured* huge file, but pure overhead once there's no overlay left to patch and
no gutter measurement worth window-scoping. `CodeEditor.tsx` split into two genuinely separate components:
**`HighlightedEditor`** (the file's original export, renamed, unchanged in substance — every virtualization/
highlighting mechanic this file's own history documents stays exactly as it was) and a new, deliberately
minimal **`PlainTextEditor`** — a bare, **native-scrolling** `<textarea>` with no overlay, no gutter, no
windowing of any kind: `UndoHistory` (unchanged — the editor's own undo stack is independent of rendering),
Tab/Shift+Tab via the existing `applyTab`, the clipboard-menu anchor (`data-text-menu-anchor`) moved directly
onto the textarea's own wrapper since there's no separate highlighted layer to anchor it beside, and the
browser's own native Page Up/Down (nothing to intercept — a plain textarea already pages itself correctly,
unlike `HighlightedEditor`'s virtualized one, which has to simulate it). **Line numbers can't be shown in this
mode** — asked for directly, and structurally true regardless: the gutter's own positioning has always been
computed from the wrapped-row measurement of the highlighted overlay, which doesn't exist here, so
`SettingsTab.tsx`'s "Show line numbers" checkbox is `disabled` while "Disable all code highlighting" is
checked, with a note explaining why, rather than silently drawing a gutter line numbers can't actually be
kept in step with.

**The far bigger change in the same conversation, from a follow-up todo file**: *"Let's use a third party
code editor: I would go for Monaco for desktop and you choose an appropriate one for mobile. This third party
editor will be used by default, while using our editor we developed so far would be done by checking an
appropriate option in the settings (and keep the disable code highlight option we added)."* **Monaco** for
desktop (what VS Code itself is built on — real, mature virtualization, a worker-based tokenizer, all the
editing conveniences this app's own editor was never trying to reinvent); **CodeMirror 6** for mobile, chosen
specifically for being touch-first and genuinely lightweight next to Monaco's multi-megabyte footprint, with
its own virtualized `.cm-scroller`. `CodeEditor.tsx` is now a three-way picker (`editor.useBuiltInEditor`, off
by default, `lib/editorSettings.ts`): off mounts `ThirdPartyEditor` — `lazy(() => import('./MonacoCodeEditor'))`
on desktop, `lazy(() => import('./MobileCodeMirrorEditor'))` on mobile, decided once from `isMobile`'s static
user-agent read, so Monaco's bundle is never fetched on Android and CodeMirror's is never fetched on desktop;
on, it mounts `HighlightedEditor` or (if `highlightDisabled` is also on) the new `PlainTextEditor` above —
this app's own editor, exactly as it already was. **`highlightDisabled` means something different for the
third-party editors**: Monaco/CodeMirror never get the dramatic drop to a bare textarea (both already
virtualize their own rendering, so there's no equivalent win from doing so) — it switches their language mode
to plain text instead (`monacoLanguageId(language, highlightDisabled)` returns `'plaintext'`; CodeMirror's own
language compartment is reconfigured to no language extension).

**Monaco's integration is ESM-only, deliberately, for CSP reasons.** This app's CSP has no `'unsafe-eval'`
(see "No network access for any window (CSP)"), and Monaco's classic AMD loader (`vs/loader.js`) uses `eval`/
`new Function` to load its modules — a flat violation. `monaco-editor/editor/editor.api` (note: *not*
`monaco-editor/esm/vs/editor/editor.api` — the package's own `exports` map already prepends `esm/vs/`, so the
correct import omits it; the wrong path surfaced as a plain `Cannot find module` and, confusingly, a second
error that `self.MonacoEnvironment` didn't exist, both symptoms of the same failed resolution) is the package's
ESM entry, with no loader of its own. Workers (`editor.worker.js`, plus `json`/`css`/`html` language workers)
are created lazily, each with a literal `new Worker(new URL('monaco-editor/<path>', import.meta.url), { type:
'module' })` inside `self.MonacoEnvironment.getWorker(moduleId, label)` — the literal string per branch is what
lets Vite statically discover and code-split each worker; a computed path would defeat that. **Monaco's own
scrolling is not native `overflow:auto`** — it renders at a fixed position and manages its own virtual scroll
in JS (`editor.getScrollTop()`, `onDidScrollChange`), so it never fires a DOM `scroll` event at all (confirmed
live with a diagnostic capture-phase listener: zero events despite real, visually-confirmed scrolling) — see
"Bug: the top/editor header no longer autohides under Monaco" below for what that broke and how it's bridged.

**Three real bugs, found live, not by inspection**, while first building these two files: (1) the import-path
bug above; (2) both `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx` and `MobileCodeMirrorEditor.tsx` initially used
`markdownLinkAt(...) ?? linkAt(...)` for "Open link" — wrong: `markdownLinkAt` (`lib/textLinks.ts`) is only
for "Edit link"/"Insert a link" elsewhere in this app (`TextFieldMenu.tsx`), never for "Open link," which has
always used plain `linkAt` alone; fixed by dropping the `markdownLinkAt` half in both files; (3) a first draft
had dead, superseded code in `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx` (a redundant disposables map alongside the real
`nudgeActions` array, a stray unused debug binding, two separate `onDidChangeModelContent` listeners that
should have been one) — cleaned up before it shipped.

**What's deliberately not reproduced in the third-party editors**: neither Monaco's real editing surface nor
CodeMirror's is a plain `HTMLTextAreaElement`/`HTMLInputElement`, so `TextFieldMenu`'s floating clipboard menu
(the "…" trigger that attaches to any recognized text field elsewhere in the app) doesn't attach to either.
Each gets its own small toolbar instead — Undo/Redo (mirroring their own internal undo stacks, so this app's
buttons and a real Ctrl+Z agree), **Copy the selection to the app's clipboard** / **Paste from the app's
clipboard** (`internalClipboard.push`/`.peek`, `lib/clipboard.ts` — the one piece of `TextFieldMenu`'s
functionality duplicated here, since it's the one most worth having) and **Open link at the caret** (same
`editorLinkHandlers`/`linkAt` dispatch every other editor's "Open link" already uses). "Select paragraphs" and
the full clipboard-manager popup are not reproduced — a real, disclosed gap, not an oversight.

**Bundle size, disclosed, not hidden**: Monaco adds roughly 2.7 MB minified (~708 KB gzipped) to the desktop
build; CodeMirror adds roughly 613 KB minified (~210 KB gzipped) to the Android build. Both are lazy, code-split
chunks — neither ships to the platform that will never mount it — but each is a real, permanent increase to its
own installer, accepted as the cost of a genuinely more capable default editor.

Verified live (isolated debug data folder, desktop and the Android emulator): `PlainTextEditor` with
highlighting disabled — a huge file opened with no overlay/gutter DOM at all, native scrolling, Tab/Shift+Tab
and Ctrl+Z/Y all working, "Show line numbers" greyed out with its note visible; Monaco mounted by default on
desktop (real syntax colours, Undo/Redo, both clipboard buttons round-tripping through `internal_clipboard_*`,
"Open link" on a real markdown link, the worker files loading with no CSP violation in the console); CodeMirror
mounted by default on the emulator (the same checks, plus real touch-scroll); switching `useBuiltInEditor` on
and off live remounted the correct component each time with no console error. `npx tsc --noEmit` and
`npm run build` both clean; no Rust change.

## Masking Monaco's own scrollbar flicker when the header above it resizes

**"After the header gets hidden, then scrolling back up reshows the header, but as it's being reshown, there is a slight flicker affecting the monaco editor's vertical scrollbar... I don't think we can downright fix this issue, this must be what monaco does when it's being scrolled up and resized to a lower height at the same time. But we can try to hide this issue by showing an empty vertical scrollbar over the real monaco scrollbar when the header is shown (or hidden, either, for consistency)."** Taken at face value — not something to actually fix inside Monaco (`automaticLayout`'s own resize-driven relayout, Monaco's internal behavior, not a bug in how this app's wrapper drives it) — and masked instead, exactly as proposed.

**`lib/editorHeaderSync.ts` gained a begin-of-transition notification, not just the existing end-of-transition one.** `runAfterHeaderTransition` only ever told a caller once a transition was *over* — nothing told anyone the moment one *started*, which this fix needs to snapshot the real scrollbar before the header's own resize has a chance to move it. `beginHeaderTransition()`/`endHeaderTransition()` (called from `useScrollAutohide`, unchanged in when they fire) now also notify a new `subscribeHeaderTransitioning(fn)` listener set with `fn(true)`/`fn(false)`, alongside their existing jobs — a thin addition, not a new mechanism of its own.

**`MonacoCodeEditor.tsx`'s `captureScrollbarGhost(host)`** reads Monaco's own real scrollbar track (`.scrollbar.vertical`) and thumb (`.slider`) straight from the live DOM — their `getBoundingClientRect()` and `getComputedStyle(...).backgroundColor`/`borderRadius` — rather than computing or guessing any of it, so the decoy is pixel- and colour-identical to whatever the real one looks like at that exact instant, in whichever theme is active, with nothing to keep in sync by hand. Subscribing to `subscribeHeaderTransitioning`: the moment a transition begins, a snapshot is taken and rendered as two `position: fixed` (`pointer-events: none`) divs — `.monaco-scrollbar-ghost-track`/`-thumb`, `App.css` — on top of the real scrollbar, at the exact viewport coordinates captured; the moment it ends, both are removed, and the real scrollbar (now finished resizing) shows through underneath, having never been visible mid-flicker. `position: fixed` with captured client coordinates needs no relative-offset math at all, since the editor's own horizontal position never moves during a vertical-only header resize.

**A frozen decoy only masks a single flip cleanly — a second flip stacked within the same ~250ms re-captures a fresh snapshot, not a bug but a deliberate consequence of `beginHeaderTransition()` notifying unconditionally on every call, not just the first.** Found live, not assumed: Monaco's own real `onDidScrollChange` (fired by `automaticLayout`'s resize reaction, reporting its own real, unrelated scroll position through the same `reportVirtualScroll`/`decideScroll` machinery every other scroller on the page shares) can itself flip `hidden` back again moments after a driven flip, compounding two transitions in quick succession; each flip re-notifies the subscriber, so the ghost's own snapshot refreshes to the newer geometry rather than staying pinned to stale, already-wrong numbers. The common, single-flip case — the only one a person's own single scroll gesture actually produces — stays perfectly frozen for its one ~250ms window, which is what this fix exists for.

Verified live (an isolated copy of the debug build — `DEFAULT_DATA_FOLDER` temporarily pointed at a scratch, never-reused folder name, reverted and rebuilt back to `-debug` the moment the fix was confirmed, with a clean `git diff` on `layout.rs` proving it — a fresh WebView2 profile, real CDP, the Files tab's own Monaco editor on a real file): driving a header flip through the `reportVirtualScroll` bridge (the same mechanism "Bug: the top/editor header no longer autohides under Monaco" above already established, since Monaco fires no native `scroll` event to drive this through real wheel/keyboard input in this harness) — the ghost's track and thumb rects matched the real scrollbar's own rects **exactly** at the instant of capture (`{left:993, top:157, width:14, height:594}` for the track, `{left:993, top:157, width:14, height:56}` for the thumb, both read independently before and compared against what the decoy actually rendered), `pointer-events` computed as `"none"`, and the thumb's captured background (`rgba(100, 100, 100, 0.4)`, Monaco's own real slider colour) was neither blank nor a guess; after the transition's own ~250ms, both ghost elements were confirmed fully removed from the DOM with the real scrollbar's own rect unchanged and correctly visible underneath — no leftover node, no permanent offset. **Not verified by screenshot**: a genuine visual before/after of the flicker itself is structurally out of reach the same way the media-viewer black-frame investigation already found for a different bug — `Page.captureScreenshot`, taken specifically after a resize-triggering scroll cycle had occurred, rendered a full-page `"Uncaught error: ResizeObserver loop completed with undelivered notifications"` banner that a parallel, direct DOM read taken at the identical moment proved was never actually on screen (the real app content — tabs, file listing, editor text — read back completely intact and correct every time this was checked); confirmed this is a `Page.captureScreenshot`-only artifact, not a real crash or a regression this fix introduced, by reproducing the identical banner on a screenshot taken with *no* scroll trigger at all once Monaco had been through one resize cycle, and *not* reproducing it at all before any resize had happened (a fresh mount, no header transition yet) — consistent with a known class of benign, Chromium-suppressed `ResizeObserver` warning (common with any editor's own resize-reactive layout, pre-existing and unrelated to this fix, which adds no `ResizeObserver` of its own) that this particular screenshot-capture path chokes on, rather than evidence of anything wrong with the real, rendered page. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; pure frontend, no Rust change (the temporary `layout.rs` edit used only to reach an isolated instance was reverted, not part of the actual fix).

**Made worse, not better — reported live immediately after shipping the above: "when scrolling up the ghost scroll is not over the real scroll, but just beside it (on its left)."** The `reportVirtualScroll`-bridge test above used a *synthetic* key, not Monaco's own real `host` element, which is exactly why it never caught this: driving the identical scenario through Monaco's own real scroll API (a temporary debug global, `window.__csdriveMonacoDebug = editor`, exposed only for this investigation and removed before shipping) — the actual production code path, `onDidScrollChange` → `reportVirtualScroll(host, ...)` with the real host key — surfaced **two real, independent bugs**, neither of them in the synthetic test's reach:

1. **The snapshot itself was stale — captured before Monaco's own DOM had caught up to the very scroll that triggered it.** `onDidScrollChange`'s callback runs *synchronously* inside `editor.setScrollTop(...)` (or a real scroll gesture), and this whole chain — `reportVirtualScroll` → `decideScroll` → `setHidden` → React's updater → `beginHeaderTransition()` → this module's own subscriber → `captureScrollbarGhost(host)` — stays nested in that *same* synchronous call stack. Monaco updates its own scroll **state** synchronously (`editor.getScrollTop()` already reads the new value at that point) but does **not** repaint its scrollbar thumb's DOM position in that same stack — confirmed live: a snapshot taken here read the thumb still at its *pre-scroll* position (mid-track, where the file used to be scrolled to), not the "about to start resizing from" position the new scroll had actually already committed to. The frozen decoy then spent its whole ~250ms window sitting at a position unrelated to where the real scrollbar was ever going to be. **Fixed** by deferring the capture one `requestAnimationFrame` (checking `isHeaderTransitioning()` again once the frame arrives, in case a very short transition already ended within it) — enough for Monaco's own, already-scheduled paint pass to catch up before the snapshot is taken.
2. **A second, independent bug, found only once the first was fixed enough to see past it: a real, transient *horizontal* shift of Monaco's own scrollbar during the resize — measured live at 993px → 978px → 993px, a 15px round trip.** Root cause: `.code-editor` (the box hosting Monaco) has `overflow: auto` — needed by the *built-in* editors, which rely on it to scroll their own full-height content, but never legitimately needed by Monaco, which manages its own internal scrolling entirely via `automaticLayout`. During the header's own CSS height transition, Monaco's internal relayout (ResizeObserver-driven, not frame-synced to the CSS animation) can lag the box's own shrinking height by a frame or two — leaving `.monaco-editor-host` transiently *taller* than `.code-editor`'s own current height, which, with `overflow: auto`, made `.code-editor` show its own real browser scrollbar for that one frame: stealing ~15px of width and shifting everything Monaco renders — its own scrollbar included — left by that same amount, then snapping back right the instant Monaco's layout caught up. **Fixed at the root**, not papered over: a new `.code-editor-monaco` class (added only by `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx`, never the built-in editors) sets `overflow: hidden` — there is nothing left that could ever need `.code-editor`'s own scrollbar to appear for Monaco's content, so it categorically can't.

Verified live (the same isolated, scratch-folder instance, real CDP, Monaco's own real scroll API end to end — `window.__csdriveMonacoDebug.setScrollTop(...)`, not the synthetic bridge): **before** either fix, the first poll after a real scroll-to-top showed the ghost thumb frozen at `[993, 314, 14, 64]` (the stale, pre-scroll mid-track position) while the real thumb had already snapped to `[993, 111, 14, 64]` — a nonsensical ~200px vertical gap between the two — and the real track's own `left` genuinely swung `993 → 978 → 978 → 978 → 993` across the transition while the ghost sat fixed at `993`, a real, measured 15px misalignment matching the report. **After both fixes**, in both directions (a real scroll-to-top showing the header, and a real scroll-away-from-top hiding it): the ghost's captured rect matched the real scrollbar's own rect exactly at capture time (`[993,111,14,640]`/`[993,111,14,64]` and `[993,157,14,594]`/`[993,299,14,56]` respectively, confirmed against independent reads of the real scrollbar taken in the same instant), and the real track's own `left` stayed flat at `993` for the *entire* transition in both directions — a scripted check computing the max `|ghost.left - real.left|` across every poll of the hide-direction run reported **`0`**. The temporary debug global was removed before this landed (confirmed absent via a direct `window.__csdriveMonacoDebug` check against the final build) and `getComputedStyle(.code-editor).overflow` confirmed `"hidden"` for the Monaco host specifically. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; pure frontend, no Rust change — same isolation discipline as the first pass, `layout.rs` reverted to a clean diff before finishing.

## Monaco's own "Edit or insert a link" — the one markdown-authoring button that never made the move from the built-in editor

**Asked for directly, after the person went looking for it and couldn't find it: "where is the button for editing/adding a markdown link in the note editor?"** My own first answer described `TextFieldMenu.tsx`'s own "Edit link"/"Insert a link" menu items — correct for `CodeEditor.tsx`'s built-in editor, but **wrong for what the person was actually looking at**: Monaco is this app's own *default* editor (`editor.useBuiltInEditor` off by default — "A plain textarea for 'disable all code highlighting'..." above), and `TextFieldMenu`'s floating menu **never attaches to Monaco at all** — its real editing surface isn't a plain `HTMLTextAreaElement`/`HTMLInputElement`, so `isTextField` never matches it, confirmed by this file's own earlier doc comment on `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx`. The person's correction: "There is no ... button at the top-right corner of the editor." They were right, and reading `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx`'s own toolbar confirmed it: **"Edit link"/"Insert a link" were simply never ported to Monaco's own toolbar when it became the default** — only plain "Open link" (follows an address, no editing) made the move, alongside Undo/Redo and the app-clipboard copy/paste.

**Fixed by adding "Edit or insert a link" to Monaco's own toolbar** — one button, not two, unlike `TextFieldMenu`'s dropdown where both can be separate, simultaneously-visible menu items: a toolbar button is a fixed icon with no room to swap labels as the caret moves, so it computes fresh on each click (the same "decide at click time" shape `openLinkAction` already used) whether the caret is in an existing markdown link (`markdownLinkAt` — pure, text-offset based, no DOM dependency, so it works identically against `editor.getValue()`'s plain string as it already does against a textarea's) and opens the shared `MarkdownLinkModal` on it, or, when it isn't, opens the same popup empty, pre-filled from the current selection, to insert a new one. Writing the result back uses Monaco's own `executeEdits` over a `Range` built from the hit's own character offsets (`model.getPositionAt`) — the direct Monaco equivalent of `TextFieldMenu.tsx`'s `replaceSelection`/`field.setRangeText` for a plain textarea — so the edit lands in Monaco's own undo stack for free, Ctrl+Z included, the same as every other edit this wrapper makes. Registered as a real Monaco action with `contextMenuGroupId: 'navigation'` (alongside "Open link"), so it's also reachable from Monaco's own right-click menu, not just the toolbar. Gated on `onOpenLink` being wired, the same way `TextFieldMenu`'s own two menu items are — a signal that this editor is for a file links make sense in, not a strict technical dependency (only "Open in external browser" inside the popup actually needs it).

**"If adding it back, it should never overlap the actual editor, like it once did"** — the person's own explicit caution, referencing the earlier, already-fixed "Redo button was almost completely covered by the floating User Action launch button" bug (`.code-editor-menu-slot` widened to 110px, above). Checked before writing any code, not assumed safe: `.code-editor-toolbar` is `display: flex` with no `flex-wrap` (default `nowrap`) inside `.code-editor-wrap`'s own `flex-direction: column`, so the toolbar and the editor below it are stacked, fixed-height siblings — adding one more `IconButton` to the row only ever makes the row *wider*, never taller, so it structurally cannot grow down into the editor's own space the way a *second line* of buttons could. The `.code-editor-menu-slot` reserved slot that caused the original bug doesn't even apply here: it exists for `TextFieldMenu`'s floating portal to dock into, and that portal never fires for Monaco at all (the same reason this whole feature was missing in the first place) — so there's no floating element left to overlap anything.

Verified live (isolated **debug** data folder, desktop, real CDP, the full command→UI stack through the admin-app's own Files tab — chosen over a Notes notebook for a faster setup, since both hosts share the identical `MonacoCodeEditor` component and wiring): a real markdown file with one existing link (`[first link](https://example.com)`) and one plain sentence with no link, opened in the real editor. **Layout**: the toolbar's six buttons (Undo, Redo, both clipboard actions, Open link, the new Edit/Insert-link button) all measured on one row (`top/bottom` identical across all six), the editor area starting a clean 6px below the toolbar's own bottom edge with zero vertical overlap — confirmed by direct `getBoundingClientRect()` reads, not a screenshot alone. **Edit link**: a real CDP click placed the caret inside the existing link (located via a `Range` over Monaco's own rendered text, not a pixel guess) — the button correctly opened "Edit link" pre-filled with `first link`/`https://example.com`; editing both fields and saving replaced it with `[edited link text](/new/address.md)`, confirmed byte-exact both in Monaco's own live content and, after the Files tab's own Save, on disk. **Insert a link**: a real mouse-drag selection over the plain sentence's `no link` words (located the same way, with a real drag — `mousePressed`/`mouseMoved`/`mouseReleased`, not a synthetic selection API call) correctly opened "Insert a link" pre-filled with `no link`/empty address; filling the address and saving replaced exactly that selection with `[no link](https://inserted.example)`, leaving the already-edited first link and the rest of the sentence untouched. **Undo**: a real click on the toolbar's own Undo button reverted the second edit in one step, back to the unlinked `no link` text exactly, with the first (already-saved) edit left alone — confirming the edit is a real step of Monaco's own undo history, not a bypass of it. **Not separately verified live**: reaching the action through Monaco's own right-click context menu — a synthetic CDP right-click opened no visible menu at all in this harness, matching this file's own earlier, unrelated finding that a CDP-driven right-click doesn't reliably reach a real context menu in this WebView2 build (see "A floating field menu could keep floating over a second, later-opened modal," above) — not evidence against the registration itself, which uses the identical, already-proven `contextMenuGroupId: 'navigation'` pattern the sibling "Open link" action already has. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

## Monaco and CodeMirror: Select paragraphs, an optional link address, and the User Action pair

Three follow-ups to the "Edit or insert a link" work above, asked for together, plus explicit confirmation they should land in **both** the desktop (Monaco) and mobile (CodeMirror) editors.

**An empty link address is now accepted** — `MarkdownLinkModal.tsx`'s own validation used to require a non-empty address (mirroring a plain, non-empty-URL assumption no markdown spec actually makes); asked to relax it to match the title, which was already optional. `valid = trimmedAddress !== ''` is gone: `submit()` calls `onSubmit(text, trimmedAddress)` unconditionally, the address `<input>` no longer gets an `invalid` class, and the Save button's `disabled={!valid}` is gone — an empty address now writes `[text]()`, valid (if unusual) markdown, the same way the title field already allowed an empty value.

**"Select paragraphs"** — offered for the built-in editor since "Text editors and single-line boxes" above, but never ported when Monaco became the default — is back as one plain icon button (`TextCursorInput`) in Monaco's own toolbar, registered as a real action (`csdrive.selectParagraphs`, `contextMenuGroupId: '9_cutcopypaste'`) using the existing, DOM-free `selectParagraphs` (`lib/paragraphs.ts`) against `model.getValue()`/`editor.getPosition()`, applied with `editor.setSelection` + `editor.revealRange`. The person's own framing — "simply add that as an icon button instead of the … button, since it's the only option we're keeping in that menu I think" — was correct: `TextFieldMenu`'s menu for a plain textarea has four items (Select paragraphs, both clipboards' copy/paste), but Monaco already has its own clipboard buttons, so paragraph selection really was the one piece worth a dedicated button rather than a second dropdown.

**The User Action launch/close pair now reaches every text editor, not just the note editor** (asked for directly: "the launch/reset custom action buttons should be added back to editors for any text file (both monaco and codeMirror)"). `CodeEditor.tsx`'s shared `Props` gained `extra?: ReactNode` — rendered only by `ThirdPartyEditor` (Monaco/CodeMirror); `HighlightedEditor`/`PlainTextEditor` already get the identical pair for free through `TextFieldMenu`'s own floating strip (`extra`, see "Details, clipboards and paths" above), so this prop exists purely for the two editors `TextFieldMenu` structurally can't attach to. Both `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx` and `MobileCodeMirrorEditor.tsx` render `{extra}` as the toolbar's own last item, after the reserved slot — the row is `display: flex` with no `flex-wrap` inside a fixed-height column (the same layout reasoning "Monaco's own 'Edit or insert a link'" above already worked through for why this toolbar structurally can't grow into the editor below it), so adding the pair only ever widens the row.

**Two call sites, two genuinely different contexts.** `NoteEditPage.tsx`'s own `<CodeEditor>` already had `UserActionButton` in its own page header; it now also gets `extra={<UserActionButton sourceId={sourceId} folder={folder} />}` on the editor itself, so the pair is reachable from the toolbar too, not only the page chrome above it. `NotesApp.tsx`'s own editor (the File Manager's and the Note Files Explorer's, `editing` truthy) had **no** User Action access at all before this — unlike the plain file-listing view, which already has its own `<UserActionButton>` in `.toolbar-actions` — so this closes a real, pre-existing gap, not just adding a second way to reach something already there: `extra={<UserActionButton sourceId={editing.source.id} folder={...} />}`, the folder derived from `editing.path`'s own parent (`editing.path.includes('/') ? editing.path.slice(0, editing.path.lastIndexOf('/')) : ''`) and the source id read from `editing.source.id` rather than the outer component's own `sourceId` state — deliberate, since `editing.source` can differ from the currently-browsed source when a tab switch leaves an editor open on a file from a different source than the one now shown (`Editing`'s own doc comment already documents this).

**A pre-existing, unrelated bug was found live while verifying the wiring above, not fixed**: `UserActionModal.tsx` shows `The notebook "undefined"` when the current place is a folder-based notebook (no `[note-book].json`, see "A folder can be used directly as a notebook" above) — its own `where` computation reads `scope.notebook.title` directly instead of the `notebookTitle(entry)` helper that already knows how to compute a display title for a folder-notebook. Reproduced identically through both new User Action buttons (`NoteEditPage.tsx`'s and `NotesApp.tsx`'s), which confirms the scope-resolution plumbing this change added is itself correct — only the pre-existing display-title bug is at fault, and it's out of scope for this request.

Verified live (isolated **debug** data folder, desktop, real CDP, through the real UI): a real existing link's address cleared to empty, Save staying enabled with no invalid styling, and `[link]()` confirmed written to disk byte-exact; **Select paragraphs** clicked with the caret inside a real paragraph, confirmed via copy-to-the-app's-clipboard + `internal_clipboard_peek` that the exact full paragraph text (not the whole file) was selected; the full nine-button Monaco toolbar (Undo, Redo, Select paragraphs, Copy, Paste, Open link, Edit/Insert link, User Action launch, User Action close) measured on one row with a clean gap to the editor below, in both `NoteEditPage.tsx`'s note editor and `NotesApp.tsx`'s File Manager editor (identical toolbar-bottom/editor-top measurements in both); the User Action launch button's initial state (enabled) and close button's (disabled, no window open yet) confirmed in both hosts; clicking launch in `NotesApp.tsx`'s editor (a plain `.txt` file, not a note) correctly opened `UserActionModal` with the folder/source resolved from `editing.source.id`/`editing.path`, confirming the scope derivation is correct there too, not just for a note. **Not verified live**: the identical wiring in `MobileCodeMirrorEditor.tsx` — code-complete, with the same `extra` prop rendered in the same toolbar position and the identical `TextCursorInput`/`selectParagraphs` pairing for "Select paragraphs," and confirmed clean by `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build`, but no Android emulator was booted for this pass to click through it live (none was running; standing up one and building/installing a fresh APK was judged too large a cost to spend without being asked, given the desktop half was already fully proven and the code itself is a straightforward mirror of the already-verified Monaco wiring). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

## The two bug reports from the next todo file: header buttons overlapped by a wrapped title, and Monaco's header never autohiding

Two bugs, reported together after the Monaco/CodeMirror work above shipped: *"Layout bug (noticed it on
mobile): when opening the note editor (and I think it applies to all editor pages) and the title of the
document/note wraps on multiple lines, the next lines are overlapped by the editor (this is good so far).
What is not good [is] that in this case the action buttons are vertically aligned on the center and they too
are partially (or totally...) overlapped by the editor. So the action buttons should be vertically aligned to
top."* And: *"The top header and editor header no longer hide when scrolling down - tried on monaco editor."*

### Bug 1: the editor-page header's own buttons, pushed below the wrapped title

**`.notes-page-header`** (`Back`/title/`Save`/etc. — the row shared, by name and by this project's own stated
intent, across `NoteEditPage.tsx`, `NotesApp.tsx`'s File Manager/Note Files Explorer editor, and
`FilesTab.tsx`'s own editor) had two compounding problems, found live in that order:

1. **`align-items: center`**, the original rule (in `notes.css`): once a long title (`h2`, `flex: 1`) wraps to
   several lines, the row's own height grows to fit it, and centering every child within that taller row pushes
   the fixed-size action buttons down toward the middle — overlapped by whatever follows once the header's own
   `max-height: 60px; overflow: hidden` clip (`.editor-top-bar`/`.notes-top-bar`) cuts off anything past it.
   Switching to **`align-items: flex-start`** keeps every button flush with the title's first line instead.

2. **That fix alone was not enough — found live, measuring a real wrapped title at a 390px mobile width,
   before trusting it.** `flex-wrap: wrap` on the same row doesn't just let the title's own *text* wrap within
   its column; it lets the whole **flex row** break onto separate flex lines once the content can't fit beside
   the buttons on one. A real measurement (CDP, a deliberately long file name) showed exactly this: "Back" alone
   on the first flex line (`top: 70, bottom: 104`), the title — now with the *entire* row's width to itself — on
   a second, and the three trailing action buttons pushed onto a *third* flex line at `top: 207.8`, nowhere near
   the header's own 60px clip: invisible, indistinguishable from "overlapped by the editor," and *worse* than
   the plain `align-items` bug alone, since now every one of the trailing buttons was affected, not just the
   ones a taller single row would merely have pushed down a little. Fixed with **`flex-wrap: nowrap`**, so the
   row never breaks into separate flex lines at all — the title (`flex: 1`, plus a new `min-width: 0`, so it can
   genuinely shrink narrower than its own longest unbroken segment rather than forcing the row to overflow) wraps
   its *own* text across as many lines as it needs in whatever width is left once every `flex-shrink: 0` icon
   button (`.icon-button`, already `flex-shrink: 0` — never itself at risk of being squeezed) has taken its own
   fixed 34px.

**A separate, pre-existing gap was found getting to this fix, not caused by it**: the very first live test — in
the admin-app's own Files tab — showed *no* effect from either CSS change at all, measuring `display: block`
on the header, not `flex`. `.notes-page-header`'s actual layout rule lived only in `notes.css`, which
`system/notes/main.tsx` imports alongside `App.css` — but the admin-app's own `App.tsx` imports only `App.css`,
and `FilesTab.tsx` uses the identical class name (confirmed by `App.css`'s own, pre-existing comment naming it
as shared across all three hosts) with no rule behind it there at all: the Files tab's editor header had always
rendered as bare, unstyled block flow. **Fixed by moving the rule itself — `.notes-page-header`'s own
`display`/`align-items`/`flex-wrap` and its `h2`'s `flex`/`min-width` — from `notes.css` into `App.css`**,
right beside the pre-existing comment that already documented the class as shared; `notes.css` keeps only a
pointer comment at the old location. This is what actually made the fix take effect in the Files tab at all,
not just in Notes' own two hosts.

Verified live (isolated debug data folder, desktop, real CDP, a 390px emulated mobile viewport): a file with a
genuinely very long name, opened in the Files tab — `display: flex`, `align-items: flex-start`,
`flex-wrap: nowrap` all confirmed computed (not just specified), the title wrapped to 192px tall, and every one
of its four action buttons (Back, Insert a path…, Discard, Save) measured at `top: 0, bottom: 34` — flush with
the title's first line, fully inside the visible 60px window, none clipped. The same file opened in a real
Notes window's File Manager editor (`NotesApp.tsx`) showed the identical result across its five buttons (the
extra one being "New branch…"), title wrapped to 264px. **The ordinary single-line case was re-checked for a
regression**: a short file name's title (24px tall) and its buttons all measured `top: 0, bottom: 34` too — no
visible difference from the centered behaviour for equal-height content, as intended. Finally, verified against
the literal subject of the bug report — a real note created in a real notebook, opened in the actual
**`NoteEditPage.tsx`** (not a generic file editor) with a genuinely long title (wrapped to 624px, ten header
buttons including the User Action pair) — every button still `top: 0, bottom: 34`; the editor's own internal
toolbar (`.code-editor-toolbar`, Undo/Redo/clipboard/Open-link — a different header again, this one belonging
to the editor widget itself rather than the page, with no title in it to wrap and no `align-items` issue of its
own) was checked alongside it and found unaffected, as expected: all five of its buttons likewise `top: 0,
bottom: 34`.

### Bug 2: Monaco's own scroll never autohides the header above it

**`useScrollAutohide`** (`lib/scrollAutohide.ts` — the shared mechanism behind the Help tab's header, Notes'
top bar, and every `.editor-top-bar`) was built entirely around a single `document`-level, capture-phase
native `scroll` listener: delegation, so any scrollable element anywhere in the page is picked up automatically
with no explicit wiring per host. **Monaco breaks that assumption at the root** — as noted above, it never
fires a native `scroll` event at all, confirmed with a diagnostic listener recording zero events across real,
visually-confirmed scrolling — so the header above a Monaco editor never saw anything to react to and simply
never autohid, exactly as reported ("tried on Monaco editor").

**Fixed by giving `useScrollAutohide` a second input that doesn't depend on a native event, rather than
teaching Monaco to fire one it structurally can't.** A new module-level `reportVirtualScroll(key, top, height,
clientHeight)` dispatches a `CustomEvent('csdrive-virtual-scroll', { detail: { key, top, height, clientHeight
} })` on `document` — `key` is any stable object identity (Monaco's own host `<div>` works fine, even though it
isn't the element that's "really" scrolling); the hook's existing effect now installs a *second* listener, for
this event, feeding the exact same decision logic (`decideScroll`, extracted verbatim from the old inline
`onScroll` body — the hide/show thresholds, the near-the-top special case, the pinned-at-bottom-ignore-upward-
echo guard, all unchanged) that the native listener already used, keyed by `key` instead of `e.target`.
`MonacoCodeEditor.tsx`'s own `onDidScrollChange` handler — Monaco's one *real*, if non-native, scroll
notification — now also calls `reportVirtualScroll(hostElement, editor.getScrollTop(), editor.getScrollHeight(),
editor.getLayoutInfo().height)` on every change. **Deliberately not a fourth return value from the hook itself**
(prop-drilling a reporter function down through `CodeEditor` → `MonacoCodeEditor` was the first draft, discarded
once the module-level-function-plus-`CustomEvent` shape above made that unnecessary): the hook's own call sites
(the Help tab, `NotesTopBar`, every `.editor-top-bar` host) don't change at all, and any future non-native
scroller (another third-party widget, say) only ever needs to call the one exported function, no new prop
threading required anywhere.

CodeMirror needed no equivalent fix — its `.cm-scroller` is a genuine native `overflow: auto` element, so the
existing capture-phase listener already sees it directly, the same as this app's own `HighlightedEditor`/
`PlainTextEditor`.

Verified live (isolated debug data folder, desktop, real CDP wheel events — `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` with
`type: 'mouseWheel'`, not a synthetic `scroll` dispatch, since the whole point is whether Monaco's *own* real
scroll handling reaches the hook correctly): opened the Files tab's own Monaco editor on a real 300-line file,
confirmed the header shown (`height: 60`, no hidden class); a real wheel-scroll down correctly collapsed it
(`hidden: true, height: 0`); scrolling back up correctly restored it (`hidden: false, height: 60`). Re-verified
in a real Notes window's own Monaco editor (not just the Files tab) on the same file: before scrolling,
`hidden: false, height: 48`; after a real downward wheel-scroll burst, `hidden: true, height: 0`; after scrolling
back up, `hidden: false, height: 34` (the editor-page header's own, slightly shorter resting height there) — the
bridge works identically in both hosts that mount Monaco. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean
throughout; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply to either bug in this section.

## Monaco's word-navigation shortcuts, and listing pages' own header-wrap fix

**"Ctrl+LeftArrow and Ctrl+RightArrow (along Ctrl+Shift+LeftArrow and Ctrl+Shift+RightArrow) don't work — they
do nothing — although Ctrl+Backspace and Ctrl+Delete do work as expected."** Confirmed live first, not assumed:
a real CDP keystroke landed `defaultPrevented: false` at a bubble-phase `document`-level listener and a `target`
of `.native-edit-context` — nothing in this app's own code (`useListKeyboard`, `useButtonRowKeyboard`,
`ChordHost`'s capture-phase Ctrl+K handler — each ruled out by reading its own early-return guards, none of
which match a plain, non-chord Ctrl+Arrow) ever touches the event; the gap is inside Monaco's own input handling.

**First "fixed" with `editContext: false`** — Monaco 0.57's default input surface is a `<div
class="native-edit-context">`, built on the browser's EditContext API, not the classic hidden `<textarea
class="inputarea">`, and `editContext: false` falls back to the classic path, where the fix *appeared* to work.
**That fix was reverted** — asked for directly ("using a legacy mode for the monaco editor is not ideal") after
it was found to have its own, separate and worse bug: reported live, "Ctrl+Shift+arrow keys only work well for
1 press… for consecutive presses… the selection is reset after each press… it happened after I press PgDn or
Ctrl+End or scroll down more than a few screens… if I return to the beginning of the document it's ok again."
Confirmed live, repeatedly, on a 2000-line file: jump to the end (`Ctrl+End`), hold Shift, repeat Ctrl+Left —
the real selection collapses to nothing for one keystroke right at the moment the legacy textarea's own content
window has to grow/re-centre, then resumes, matching "scrolled far from the start" exactly (near the start, the
window already reaches position 0, so there's no edge to cross). The legacy textarea controller never mirrors
the *whole* document into the real textarea, only a bounded window of text around the cursor, and Monaco's own
internal selection state visibly desyncs from the browser's native textarea selection exactly at that window's
edge — confirmed with Monaco's own `editor.getSelection()` (via a temporary debug global) showing the *model's*
selection frozen at one character while the textarea's own, merely-cosmetic native selection kept drifting
independently underneath it.

**Root cause of the *original* gap, found by reading Monaco's own source (`wordOperations.js`) rather than
guessing a second time**: the default Ctrl+Left/Right/Shift+Left/Right keybindings carry `kbExpr: ...
ContextKeyExpr.and(CONTEXT_ACCESSIBILITY_MODE_ENABLED, IsWindowsContext)?.negate()` — on Windows, whenever
Monaco's own "accessibility mode" context key reads true, the *default* keybinding is deliberately disabled in
favour of a separate, NVDA-tuned "Accessibility" word-navigation command instead ("Accessibility navigation
commands should only be enabled on windows since they are tuned to what NVDA expects" — Monaco's own comment).
Confirmed live that this is exactly what happens here: calling *any* built-in word-navigation command —
`cursorWordLeft`, `cursorWordLeftSelect`, the `Start`/`End` variants, and the Windows-only "Accessibility" ones
— via `editor.trigger()` does nothing at all on this platform/Monaco build, while the identical call for a
plain, non-word command (`cursorLeft`) works correctly; real Ctrl+Backspace (`deleteWordLeft`, a *different*
command family) still working, as originally reported, is consistent with the gap being specific to the
word-*navigation* commands, not a blanket EditContext-mode failure.

**Fixed for real**: `editContext` is back at Monaco's own default (`true`); `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx` registers
four of its own `editor.addAction`s on the exact same four key combinations, each computing the target position
itself rather than delegating to any built-in command id (there wasn't a working one left to delegate to, by
any means — not just real keyboard dispatch, a bare `editor.trigger()` call fails identically). `wordBoundaryLeft`/
`wordBoundaryRight` (module-level) use the model's own public `getWordAtPosition` for Monaco's actual
word-boundary definition — not a hand-rolled regex guessing at word shape — and only hand-roll the *walk* across
whitespace/line boundaries between one word and the next, which `getWordAtPosition` alone doesn't do; `moveWord`
applies the result with the always-reliable `setPosition`/`setSelection` (keeping the selection anchor fixed for
the `Select` variants, exactly how Monaco's own command would). **A real edge-case bug was caught live, not by
inspection, before trusting this**: `getWordAtPosition` queried exactly at a word's own boundary can match that
word even when the character actually being asked about sits just *outside* it (its own "under or besides"
contract, not a bug in Monaco itself) — found live on a line ending `…lineNumber0() {`: querying right at the
boundary between `lineNumber0` and the `(` after it returned `lineNumber0` itself, whose own end exactly matched
the query column, so the word's reported start was already behind the caret and nothing moved. Fixed by checking
the returned word's own range genuinely contains the character being asked about before trusting it, in both
directions. Deliberately modest in scope — this does not try to replicate every one of Monaco's own corner
cases (camelCase sub-word stops, the NVDA-specific "Accessibility" word shape) — just the ordinary "jump to the
start of the previous/next word" shape Ctrl+Arrow has always meant here, which is what was reported broken.

Verified live (isolated debug data folder, desktop, real CDP `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` keystrokes, via a
temporary debug global exposing `editor.getSelection()`/`getPosition()` directly, removed before finalizing):
confirmed the input surface is back to `hasEditContext: true, hasTextarea: false`; on a small file, Ctrl+Left/
Right move the caret correctly and three held-Shift Ctrl+Left presses grow the selection word by word
(`[1,41,1,46]` → `[1,37,1,46]` → `[1,32,1,46]`); on the 2000-line file, jumping to the very end and holding
Shift through **40 consecutive real Ctrl+Left presses produced zero collapses**, with the selected text visibly
growing correctly the whole way (`"}"` → `"; }"` → `"epsilon; }"` → …); the line-boundary-crossing case (Ctrl+Left
from column 1) and the document-start clamp (no error, no movement past position 1,1) both behave correctly;
Ctrl+Shift+Right from the very start caught the exact `getWordAtPosition`-boundary bug above (stuck at
`"function lineNumber0"` for two presses in a row before the fix; correctly reached `"function lineNumber0("`
on the third press after it) and a regression check (real Ctrl+Backspace) still correctly deletes a whole word.
A final pass with the debug global removed confirmed the same 20-press stress test through nothing but real,
rendered DOM (`.native-edit-context` present, `textarea.inputarea` absent, a real multi-block `.selected-text`
highlight visible on screen afterward) — not just the debug API. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both
clean; no Rust change, and `cargo test --lib` (282 tests) passes unchanged, confirming this is a pure
frontend-only fix.

**"On the listing pages on thin viewports (like mobile), the title is overlapped by the top action buttons…
the listing pages don't have their own top sticky header, like the editor does. Instead, the top action buttons
in the listings pages should still fall below the title."** A regression from the editor-page header fix two
sections up ("Bug 1: the editor-page header's own buttons, pushed below the wrapped title") — that fix's
`flex-wrap: nowrap` was correctly re-scoped to `.notes-page-header.editor-top-bar` alone in that same investigation
(see this file's own account of it, just above), which already stops a listing page's header from ever hiding a
button on an invisible trailing flex line (there's no `max-height`/`overflow: hidden` clip on a listing page to
hide anything behind in the first place) — but confirmed live, at a real 360px width with a genuinely long
notebook title, that plain `flex-wrap: wrap` *alone* wasn't the fix the report actually asked for either: with
the title (`h2`, `flex: 1`, no `min-width: 0` — that's the editor variant's own) left free to shrink, the
browser squeezed it down to its own narrowest unbreakable word (~118px) and still fit several action buttons
beside that cramped column on the same flex line — no pixel overlap (confirmed by rect comparison), but not
"the buttons fall below the title" either, just "the title got squeezed instead of overlapped." **Fixed** with
a narrow-viewport rule, scoped to `:not(.editor-top-bar)` so the editor pages' own already-correct behavior is
completely untouched: below the same 600px breakpoint `.window-item` already uses for its own "give the text
the whole line, push the buttons to one below" layout (CLAUDE.md's "Slim windows"), the title is forced onto an
entire flex line of its own (`flex: 1 0 100%` — a 100% flex-basis in a wrapping row leaves no room for anything
beside it) so every button is pushed onto the next line(s), wrapping among themselves exactly as `flex-wrap:
wrap` already does for any ordinary row of icon buttons; above 600px the rule doesn't apply at all, so the
established, title-and-buttons-share-one-line desktop layout is unchanged. Verified live (isolated debug data
folder, desktop, real CDP, a real notebook with a genuinely long title, `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`
confirmed via `window.innerWidth` *within the same CDP session* — a session-scoped override that silently resets
to the real window width on a fresh WebSocket connection was a real methodology trap hit mid-investigation, not
a property of the app): at 360px, the title's rect spanned the header's full available width (328px) wrapped
across several lines, and every one of the notes list's seven action buttons landed strictly below the title's
own bottom edge (`allBelowTitle: true`) — for a page whose header has a leading icon button *before* the title
in document order (`NotebooksPage.tsx`'s own `House` button), that one button correctly stayed on its own line
above the title (nothing forces an item *before* a 100%-wide item onto the same line as it, which is a case
the ask didn't speak to and isn't a regression — there was never a "leading button beside the title" guarantee
to begin with), with the remaining buttons below the title as asked; at 1200px the same page showed the title
and every button sharing one line exactly as before (`allBelowTitle: false`, no overlap) — the wide-viewport
layout is bit-for-bit unaffected. A parallel check of the Files tab's own file editor (`.editor-top-bar`) at the
identical 360px width confirmed it was untouched by this media query: still `flex-wrap: nowrap`, still clipped to
`max-height: 60px`, title still shrinking to its own narrowest word rather than wrapping the row. `npx tsc
--noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change.

## Text find in the editors: plain text or regex, with a checkbox

**"Let's implement a text find functionality for the text editor: searches both by plain text and by regex
(with a checkbox toggling between the two)."** Discussed first, since this app now has *three* editor
surfaces (`CodeEditor.tsx`'s own `HighlightedEditor`/`PlainTextEditor`, the opt-in built-in editor; Monaco on
desktop; CodeMirror on mobile — see "A plain textarea for 'disable all code highlighting'..." above) and two
of the three are third-party widgets that already ship their own find UI, regex toggle included. The person
chose: **native find for Monaco/CodeMirror** (enable what each already has, rather than suppressing it in
favour of a second, thinner reimplementation) **and a new custom find bar only for the built-in editor**,
which had nothing at all.

**Monaco's own Find/Replace was completely dormant, not working — confirmed live before assuming otherwise.**
`MonacoCodeEditor.tsx` imports `monaco-editor/editor/editor.api`, Monaco's *bare core* — real find/replace,
folding, bracket matching and multicursor all live behind `monaco-editor/editor/editor.main.js`, which
`editor.api.js` never pulls in; the module's own doc comment used to claim these "come from Monaco itself,"
which was aspirational, never actually true. Confirmed directly: `editor.trigger('', 'actions.find', null)`
silently no-ops when the command was never registered, and a real Ctrl+F does nothing. `monaco-editor/editor/
contrib/find/browser/findController.js` is self-contained (its own imports are only base/platform services
`editor.api.js` already sets up for any standalone editor — read, not assumed) and pulls in no other
contribution, so importing it alone — not the whole, much heavier `editor.main.js` — registers Find/Replace
(Ctrl+F/Ctrl+H, the regex/case/whole-word toggles, Find All) with nothing else added.

**A real packaging bug in `monaco-editor` itself, found live, not by inspection.** The find widget renders its
own icons with Monaco's `codicon` font — confirmed live as empty boxes without it — so the two codicon CSS
files needed importing too. Importing them by `monaco-editor`'s own package specifier failed the build
outright: `monaco-editor`'s `package.json` has exactly two `exports` wildcard patterns (`./*.js` and `./*`),
and *both* append a literal `.js` to the resolved target — correct for every other import in this file (a
specifier with no `.js` suffix, like `'monaco-editor/editor/editor.api'`, is assumed to have simply omitted
one, resolving to `editor.api.js`), but wrong for a `.css` path: the bare `./*` pattern resolved `codicon.css`
to a nonexistent `codicon.css.js`. **Vite's own CSS pipeline tolerated this** (the build's first attempt
visibly emitted a real `codicon.ttf` and real CSS output for it), **but Rolldown's separate, stricter
post-transform import-validation pass then failed the *identical* specifier as unresolved** — a genuine build
failure (`npm run build`), not a lint warning, and a real inconsistency between the two tools' own resolvers
for the same broken exports map. Fixed with a plain relative path into `node_modules`
(`../../node_modules/monaco-editor/esm/vs/base/browser/ui/codicons/codicon/codicon.css`) — a filesystem
reference, never a bare package specifier, so neither tool's `exports`-map handling is involved at all; the
standard escape hatch for a package whose own `exports` map doesn't support what's actually needed from it.

**CodeMirror's own Find was already fully working — just invisible, with no button for a touch screen to
reach it.** `codemirror`'s `basicSetup` (used as-is by `MobileCodeMirrorEditor.tsx`) already bundles
`@codemirror/search`'s `searchKeymap`, whose `Mod-f` entry calls `openSearchPanel` — a real keyboard shortcut
that already worked, confirmed by reading `openSearchPanel`'s own source rather than assumed: it self-installs
the rest of `@codemirror/search`'s state (`StateEffect.appendConfig.of(searchExtensions)`) on first use if
`basicSetup`'s own narrower import (`searchKeymap` alone, not the full `search()` extension) hadn't already
added it. The whole fix is one new toolbar button (`Search` icon, beside the existing clipboard/open-link
ones) calling `openSearchPanel(view)` directly — the exact function the keymap already calls — with
`@codemirror/search` added to `package.json` as a direct dependency (it was already physically installed,
transitively, at the exact version needed, but this file now imports from it directly rather than only
through `codemirror`'s own re-export, so it earns its own explicit entry).

**The built-in editor's own find bar** (`FindBar.tsx`, used by both `HighlightedEditor` and `PlainTextEditor`
in `CodeEditor.tsx`) is new from nothing: a query box, **the checkbox** (asked for directly), Previous/Next,
a live match count or a readable regex error, and Close — no Replace, which wasn't asked for here. The
matching logic is pure (`lib/textFind.ts`, Node-tested with an ad-hoc script, the project's own established
pattern for a module with no committed test file yet — a case-insensitive, non-overlapping substring scan for
plain mode; `new RegExp(query, 'g')` for regex mode, a bad pattern reported as `error` rather than thrown, and
a zero-length match (`a*`) advanced past by hand so the scan can't loop forever on one position; both capped
at `MAX_FIND_MATCHES` as a size-based safety net, the same shape `MAX_LINES_FOR_WRAP_MEASUREMENT` already has
for a different cost). Opening the bar (Ctrl+F, or the toolbar's new `Search` button) captures the caret's own
selection once, as the bar's starting anchor and its pre-filled query — the common "search for what I already
had selected" convention every other Ctrl+F already follows — and **re-anchors to the nearest match only when
the query or the regex checkbox itself changes**, not on every render while the document is edited elsewhere
with the bar left open: jumping (and so moving the real selection) on an unrelated keystroke outside the box
would fight whatever the person is actually doing there. Previous/Next simply step the current index by one,
wrapping, independent of that re-anchor.

**The current match is shown by the editor's own native text *selection*, not a second "highlight every
match" overlay.** `RunPatchController` (`lib/runPatchController.ts`) owns every node under the highlighted
`<pre>` exclusively, by design — not a safe target for this bar to also paint into — so `onJumpToMatch` just
calls `setSelectionRange` on the real `<textarea>`, which already paints a visible selection highlight on top
of the syntax-coloured layer behind it, exactly how dragging to select text already looks. Bringing the match
into *view* needed its own handling in each of the two built-in editors, since this project's own earlier
findings (the `fit()`-neighbouring effects, above) already ruled out any native "scroll the caret into view"
behaviour for a programmatic selection change with no text edit: `HighlightedEditor.jumpToMatch` sets
`frame.scrollTop` by hand (the match's line, via the already-memoized `lineStarts`/`gutterRowStarts`, centred
in the viewport) — which fires a real `scroll` event, reusing the *exact* hysteresis mechanism that already
recomputes `windowRange` for a Page Up/Down jump of any distance, with no new window-management code of its
own. `PlainTextEditor.jumpToMatch` is simpler (no virtualized window, no separate frame — the textarea scrolls
itself natively): compute the line fresh via `lineStartOffsets`/`lineAtOffset` and set `input.scrollTop`
directly. Ctrl+F is checked *before* the `readOnly` early-return in both editors' own `onKeyDown` — searching
a file open only for viewing should still work, the same as a browser's own Ctrl+F does on any page.

The floating bar itself (`.code-editor-find-bar`, `App.css`) anchors inside `.code-editor`, which already has
`position: relative` for unrelated reasons — an absolutely-positioned child there stays pinned at the same
screen position while the content below it scrolls, with zero interaction with the virtualized window's own
height math (it never changes `.code-editor`'s own `clientHeight`, so nothing about opening or closing it
could disturb `windowRange`).

Node-tested (`lib/textFind.ts`, an ad-hoc script matching this project's own pattern): plain case-insensitive
matching and its non-overlapping rule, an empty query, three real regex digit-group matches at the right
offsets, a zero-length-match regex terminating correctly rather than looping, a bad regex reported as `error`
with zero matches, and `matchIndexNear`'s forward/backward/wrap-at-either-end behaviour including on an empty
list. **Verified live** (isolated **debug** data folder — a dedicated `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER`/CDP port so
this didn't compete with any other running instance for the shared WebView2 browser host, per "Folder
layout"'s own finding — desktop, real CDP throughout, a real six-line test file with three "hello"s and four
digit groups written via a genuine `fs_write_file` call and opened through the real Files-tab UI, not a
direct backend shortcut):
- **`HighlightedEditor`**: the toolbar's `Search` button opened the bar; a real `Input.insertText` of "hello"
  showed "1 of 3" with the real textarea's selection at the exact byte offsets of the first match (confirmed
  against the file's own text, not assumed); **Next**, clicked as two genuinely separate events (a `.click()`
  called twice in the same synchronous tick was tried first and read as a wraparound bug — both calls closed
  over the same stale `currentIndex`, the identical class of React stale-closure pitfall this file's own
  history already warns about for synthetic test scripts — re-verified correct once each click ran in its own
  real event/render cycle) advanced through all three matches and wrapped back to the first; toggling the
  regex checkbox and typing `\d+` showed "1 of 4" with the real selected substring reading `"42"`, the file's
  first digit group; a deliberately malformed pattern (`(`) showed `"Invalid regular expression: /(/g:
  Unterminated group"` with the error-styled class applied, no thrown error, zero matches; **Escape** closed
  the bar and returned real focus to the editor's own textarea; a real Ctrl+F (genuine OS foreground focus
  confirmed via `document.hasFocus()`, not assumed) reopened it pre-filled with the editor's then-current
  selection.
- **`PlainTextEditor`** (`editor.highlightDisabled` on): the same toolbar button and a plain query ("digits")
  showed "1 of 2" with the real selected text reading `"digits"` — the simpler, non-virtualized scroll path
  confirmed working with no error.
- **Monaco** (`editor.useBuiltInEditor` off, the shipped default): a real Ctrl+F produced Monaco's own actual
  `.find-widget` — `editor-widget find-widget visible` — with its real toggle row (`Match Case`, `Match Whole
  Word`, **`Use Regular Expression (Alt+R)`**, `Find in Selection`, `Preserve Case`), none of them empty boxes
  (confirming the codicon font fix); a plain "hello" query showed Monaco's own `"1 of 3"`; a real Alt+R toggled
  `codicon-regex checked` and a `\d+` query showed `"1 of 4"` — Monaco's own native regex find, genuinely
  reachable for the first time.
- **CodeMirror, on mobile, was not separately exercised live in this pass** — no emulator was brought up for
  it; this rests on `openSearchPanel`'s own source (read directly, not assumed) confirming it self-installs
  whatever state it needs, which is the same mechanism the already-live-verified `Mod-f` keyboard path already
  depends on elsewhere in this app's own Android history, plus the frontend's own clean, type-checked build.

The test file and the two settings this pass flipped (`editor.useBuiltInEditor`, `editor.highlightDisabled`)
were reset afterward, in the same isolated debug profile, back to their shipped defaults (off, off) and the
file removed via a real `fs_remove` call — nothing was left behind for a later session to trip over. `npx tsc
--noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean throughout; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

## Searching and sorting (Notes' File Manager, Note Files Explorer and lists of notes)

**The panel** (*Search and sort…* in the toolbar of a file manager and in the header of a notes page; `SearchPanel.tsx`; the logic is `search.ts`, plain functions and generators, tested in Node with an in-memory source: terms, contents, intervals, sorting, the walk, the pager, notes). **Search by**: **name** (a file's or folder's name, a note's title), **contents** (a file's text, a note's markdown), **last modified** and **created** time stamps and **size** — each an *interval* whose either edge may be left empty (infinity) —, **extension** (a list such as `jpg, png`), and **type** (files / folders). **Every text term can be a regular expression**; a regular expression on the contents is applied **line by line** (`^`/`$` are a line's) or **to the whole contents** (a newline is then the expression's business). A plain term is a case-insensitive substring unless *Match case*. **Depth**: 1 (this folder only) by default, any number of levels, or *No limit* (never deeper than 100, links can make a tree endless). A bad regular expression, an interval that ends before it starts or a wrong depth is refused with a message before anything runs. Enter in a box searches.

**Sorting** (the same panel; effective at once): by name (numbers as numbers), extension, last modified, created or size — *As listed* for files is folders first by name; for notes it is **by index** —, ascending or descending, folders first or not; a value nobody knows (a size, a date) goes last in either direction. Sorting a folder of this device by size or date reads them for the whole folder in **one call** (`fs_read_dir_detailed`: size, modified and created time per entry — `FileSource.listDetailed`; the plain listing has names only, and sizes are read for the page on screen as before); a Filen listing has size and modified time already, no creation time (the panel says so), and a folder of this device may not keep one either.

**Nothing is ever collected in memory.** A search is an *async generator* (`searchFiles`, `searchNotes`) that walks the tree folder by folder — one listing at a time — and yields one hit at a time; a `ResultPager` (`useResultPager`) pulls from it **only as far as the page on screen needs** and keeps the last six pages: the next page continues the walk, a page that was let go is found again by walking again (slower, never more memory), and Stop is just leaving (clearing, changing folder, closing). So a search that finds a million things holds a page or two, whatever the tree. What that costs is the **order**: results are **grouped by folder** — the folder's own hits (sorted as asked) first, then each subfolder's, depth first — and each says which folder it is in (**its ancestors**: the folder's path for files and folders, the titles of the notes above it for a note); a *global* sort of a deep search would need every hit at once, so it is exact within each folder and — at depth 1 — everywhere. The status line says how many were found so far (the total once the whole tree was looked at), how many folders were looked in, and how many files could **not be searched**: contents are read only from files of up to **10 MiB** that are text (no NUL in the first 8 000 characters), so nothing big is pulled into the page for a search, and a Filen file is fetched to the cache only if it is small enough (its size is in the listing).

**Results** replace the listing while a search is on (the folder's pagination and thumbnails give way; `FileSearchResults`, `NoteSearchResults`): a file result shows its name, its folder, the line that matched (`line 3: …`), size and date, opens like a listed file, and *Show it in its folder* leaves the search and lists that folder with the file focused; a note result shows its index, title, the notes above it and the matching line, and offers the web app, the markdown, its children and *show it among the notes next to it*. Changing folder clears the search. **While a list of notes is searched or sorted, everything that reorders, renumbers or converts indexes is off** — the indexes dialog, editing an index (the badge, Shift+F2, the menu item) and *normalize the remaining notes* in Delete: what is in view is not in the order those operations act on (`indexOps`).

Tested live on desktop (isolated data): sorting by size, date (folders first kept), name; searches by name (case), contents at every depth (line and folder shown, the big and the binary file reported), regular expressions line by line and on the whole contents, modified / created / size intervals with an edge left out, extension, a refused expression, a 60-file folder paged 25 at a time (next, next, previous, *Show it in its folder*); notes — sort by title and created, the index tools going off, a title and a contents search at every depth with the parent titles, the list coming back on clear. Tested on the Android emulator too (isolated data): the panel fits the phone's width with no sideways scroll, a contents search at every depth (each result with its folder), sorting by size through the one detailed listing, and the indexes dialog's interval buttons ticking a whole interval and converting it. **Not run live:** a search or sort in a Filen account (the cache's listings feed the same generator; it is covered by unit tests only) and the notes search on the Android emulator (verified on desktop).

## Pagination for "Insert a path…"'s own browsers

**"The folder listing from the modal that opens when I tap on the insert path button from the editor pages: that
listing also needs to be paginated (just like all folder and note listings are paginated — with buttons for
next/prev page and clicking on the current page opens a popover for selecting the new page from a list of all
pages)."** Two browsers, both missing it: `components/FileExplorerModal.tsx` (the generic folder browser "Insert
a path…" and the Files tab's own file editor use) and `system/notes/InsertPathModal.tsx`'s own notes-aware "Notes"
mode (the notebooks list, and the list of notes once inside one). Both now follow the exact pattern
`system/notes/LocationPicker.tsx` already established for this ("Add a notebook…"'s own folder browser) — plain
`page`/`pageSize` state (`DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE`/`getGlobalPageSize`/`setGlobalPageSize` from `lib/listPageSize.ts`,
the same global `listPageSize` setting every other paginated list shares), a slice of the full list for the page
shown, and the shared `<Pagination>` component rendered below the list — not a new mechanism of its own.

`FileExplorerModal.tsx`: `entries` (the full folder listing `list(path)` returns) is sliced to
`entries.slice(currentPage * pageSize, (currentPage + 1) * pageSize)` for the `<ul>`'s own `.map`, with `page`
reset to `0` whenever the listing itself reloads (a folder change, in `list`'s existing effect) so a page number
from the previous folder is never silently kept against a shorter one.

`InsertPathModal.tsx`: one `page`/`pageSize` pair serves *both* of its own lists (the notebooks list and, once
inside a notebook, its notes list), since only one is ever shown at a time — `totalItems` is `notebooks.length`
or `notes.length` depending on `browse.view`, and `page` resets to `0` on every `browse` change (switching
notebooks, or going back to the notebooks list, always starts at page 1 of the new list). The one `<Pagination>`
render reflects whichever list is currently showing.

Verified live (isolated **debug** data folder — a scratch `DEFAULT_DATA_FOLDER`/WebView2 profile for this pass
alone, reverted and the debug binary rebuilt clean afterward, confirmed by a clean `git diff` on `layout.rs`;
desktop, real CDP, real button clicks through the actual UI, not direct backend calls): **`FileExplorerModal`**
— a real folder of 33 files opened via "Insert a path…" showed "1–25 of 33" and "1 / 2"; **Next** correctly
advanced to "26–33 of 33"/"2 / 2" with the remaining 8 files; the page-number button's own popover listed "1" and
"2"; clicking "1" in it navigated back correctly. **`InsertPathModal`**'s notes mode, against a real notebook
with exactly 30 notes created directly on disk for this test (30 folder pairs, `[note-children].json`, a
registered `notes.notebooks` entry — all removed afterward): the notebooks list itself (one entry) correctly
showed "1–1 of 1"/"1 / 1"; opening it showed "1–25 of 30"/"1 / 2" with notes 30 down to 6; **Next** advanced to
"26–30 of 30"/"2 / 2" with the remaining 5; the page popover listed pages "1" and "2" with "2" marked current, and
clicking "1" navigated back to the first 25; changing the page-size `<select>` to 10 correctly re-paged to
"1–10 of 30"/"1 / 3" and persisted `listPageSize` to the backend (confirmed via `get_global_setting`), reset back
to 25 afterward so the change doesn't linger as a side effect of testing. **Found along the way, not fixed, out
of scope for this request**: `system/notes/NotePage.tsx`'s own *plain* notes listing (the ordinary, un-searched
view of a notebook's or note's children) has no pagination at all — it renders every child note in one unbroken
table, unlike `NoteSearchResults`' own paged generator-backed results. The person's own framing ("just like all
folder and note listings are paginated") assumes this one already has it; it doesn't, confirmed live against the
real 30-note test notebook above (30 edit buttons found with no page controls). Left alone here since the actual
ask named only the Insert-a-path modal — raised here so it isn't mistaken for already covered. `npx tsc --noEmit`
and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

## Links planted in `user/`

A directory junction or symlink planted inside `user/` (web apps can't create one — no command does — but the OS user, an unzip or a `git clone` can) is **refused in every direction**: the scope follows it to its real location, so listing, reading or creating files through a link that leads outside is an error (this used to be a gap with the fs plugin, which only resolved paths that already existed). The link itself can still be removed or renamed from inside, and its target is never touched. Tested with real symlinks (unit tests) and a Windows junction (live). On Android the `user` folder is in the app's private storage, so only `adb run-as` on a debug build (or root) can plant a link at all.

## Building and running (the admin-app is the frontend)

`csdrive-webhost-tauriapp` is a standard Tauri project: the frontend (the admin-app: React + Vite + TypeScript) at its root and the Rust backend in `src-tauri/`. `tauri.conf.json` points `frontendDist` at `../dist`, which Tauri embeds into the binary at compile time, so it is in every installer with no separate file and no network access — and there is no way for a user or a web app to swap it out, because it isn't a file in the data folder. (Its saved UI state is keyed by `layout::ADMIN_APP_ID`.)

- **Develop:** `cd csdrive-webhost-tauriapp && npm install && cargo tauri dev` — Vite on `:1420` with hot reload (`devUrl`), the app loading it, `devCsp` in force.
- **Installer:** `cargo tauri build` (runs `npm run build` first, via `beforeBuildCommand`, and turns on Tauri's `custom-protocol` feature so the embedded frontend is used). Android: `cargo tauri android build …` (see "Android").
- **Multi-page build:** `vite.config.ts` builds one html page per app — the admin-app (`index.html`) and each system app (`system/<id>/index.html`, see "System apps and Notes") — into `dist/`; Tauri embeds them all.
