The blank space the person saw to the right was simply every *other*, correctly-narrow row of the inflated-width table/column showing nothing at that extra width — the "page" wasn't actually wider everywhere, just wherever `.tab-content`'s own width had been dragged to by this one cell. **Fixed** by moving the break guarantee from `.api-call` alone onto the shared `.api-table td` rule (`overflow-wrap: break-word`, `App.css`) — covering both columns, and any cell either one might carry in the future, with no dependency on where the long run's content happens to come from. Verified live (an isolated copy of the debug build, its `DEFAULT_DATA_FOLDER` constant temporarily pointed at a scratch, never-before-used folder name so the investigation's own writes could never reach the person's real debug data — reverted and rebuilt back to the normal `-debug` constant the moment the fix was confirmed, with a clean `git diff` on `layout.rs` to prove it): before the fix, `.tab-content.scrollWidth` (1198) exceeded its `clientWidth` (1009) and the offending `.api-category`'s own `scrollWidth` (1182) exceeded its `offsetWidth` (977); after it, `.tab-content.scrollWidth === clientWidth` exactly (1009/1009) and zero `.api-category` elements overflowed their own box — a screenshot of the same, previously-overflowing section showed the long line wrapping correctly within its column, no horizontal scrollbar, no blank strip to the right. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; pure CSS, no Rust change (the temporary Rust edit used only to reach an isolated instance was reverted, not part of the actual fix).

**The sticky header could be squeezed down to a sliver, stacking its own text directly on top of the first section's own heading — reported live with a screenshot.** Two real, independent bugs, both in `.help-header`'s own CSS, found and fixed together:

1. **`overflow: hidden` (needed for the `max-height` collapse animation) gives a flex item an automatic minimum size of `0` instead of its content's own min-content floor** — a plain rule of the CSS flex spec: an item whose overflow isn't `visible` has nothing protecting it from the column's own shrink distribution. `.tab-panel` (`display: flex; flex-direction: column; height: 100%`) is a *fixed*-height container whose Help-tab content (the keyboard-shortcuts section, the long API reference) is routinely taller than that — so on every load, `.help-header` — the *only* item in that column with `overflow: hidden` — absorbed essentially all of the resulting shrink, squeezed down to a measured **17px** (confirmed live: `offsetHeight` 17 against a real `scrollHeight` of 33) while every unshrinkable sibling (plain `overflow: visible` text/table) kept its own full natural height and the column still overflowed regardless, since CSS flex-shrink never goes below an item's own floor — it only shrinks the one item that *has* no floor. Fixed the same way `.notes-top-bar` already was, for the identical reason: **`flex-shrink: 0`** on `.help-header`, restoring its natural ~51px height. `.editor-top-bar` (the Files tab's and Notes' own editor-page headers) has the exact same shape — `position: sticky`, `overflow: hidden`, no `flex-shrink` — so it got the identical, defensive fix even though it wasn't the one reported (an editor page's `.tab-content` doesn't normally overflow under ordinary use, per this file's own earlier "the page itself should not have its own scrollbar" finding, so this one was latent rather than visibly triggered, but the mechanism is identical the moment it ever does).
2. **A second, independent bug was *uncovered*, not caused, by fixing the first**: with the header's own height corrected, the gap to the very next section still measured **-4px** — the two headings overlapping, not merely touching. `.help-header` breaks out of `.tab-content`'s own 16px padding with `margin: -16px -16px 0` (so it sits flush against the very top edge) — but it's **already "stuck"** the moment the page loads (its un-stickied, static position is already at or above its own `top: 0`), and a `position: sticky` element that's stuck is *rendered* pinned to the scrolling ancestor's inner edge, ignoring its own margin for where it's actually drawn — while the flex column's own layout accounting, which decides how much space is reserved before the *next* sibling's `gap`, still treats the `margin-top: -16px` as genuinely having shifted this item (and everything after it) up by that much. The two disagree by exactly the margin's own magnitude: confirmed live by zeroing `margin-top` instead (removing the discrepancy from the other end) and getting the identical, correct 12px gap. Fixed by adding `margin-bottom: 16px` to `.help-header`'s *shown* state — paying back exactly what the shown-state/sticky mismatch quietly takes away — the same genre of hand-matched compensating constant `.help-header-hidden`'s own `margin-bottom: -12px` already is a few lines below (which needed no change: its own, more specific declaration still wins the cascade over the base rule's new value, for either state). `.editor-top-bar` has no margin of its own, so it needed no matching compensation.

Verified live (isolated **debug** data folder, desktop, real CDP, a genuine rebuild-and-reload, not a live DOM-style mutation left standing — an earlier live-mutation pass caught and corrected its own methodology issues first: `header.offsetHeight` read before a CSS-*transitioned* property like `margin-bottom` had actually finished animating gave a stale, misleading reading more than once, a trap worth remembering for any future live check of a header that lists `margin-bottom` among its `transition` properties): a fresh `npm run build` + relaunch showed the header at its real **51px** height (matching `scrollHeight`) and the gap to the Overview section at the exact, correct **12px** — a screenshot confirmed the two headings now read as a clearly separated title bar and page heading, not a stacked double line. The collapse/reshow round trip was re-checked as a regression: scrolling down still correctly collapsed the header (`offsetHeight: 0`, `margin-bottom` correctly `-12px` — the hidden rule's own value, unaffected by the shown rule's new `16px` — and the page-level phantom-scrollbar fix from the earlier "Top bar autohide" investigation still held, `document.documentElement`'s overflow still a flat `0`), and scrolling back up restored both the header and the 12px gap exactly. `.editor-top-bar`'s own `flex-shrink: 0` was checked too, opening a real file in the Files tab's editor: `offsetHeight === scrollHeight === 34`, not squished, `flex-shrink` computed as `"0"`. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; pure CSS, no Rust change.

## The top bar

The iframe-based top bar of the earlier design is gone for good (see "Why a window and not a frame" under "User Action" for the same reasoning applied there); this is the plain-DOM replacement: **only Notes' own pages get one built in** — not a user web app, and not even a note opened as a web app (a genuinely different page, served over `csuser`, outside Notes' own React tree). A user web app that wants the same thing draws it itself, the same way `example-toolbar` does (see "Deploying apps") — using exactly the same backend support Notes does, so the two stay in step.

**`NotesTopBar.tsx`**, mounted once in `NotesRoot.tsx` (above `{view}`, so every place of Notes — home, notebooks, the notes list, an editor, the file manager, settings — gets it uniformly without each page adding its own copy): **Go back one step**, **Suspend this window**, **Close this tab**, **Close this window** (`closeSecondaryWindow`, already granted to every window — unlike Suspend, there is no "Reopen" after this one), the markup below, and its own **×** to hide itself.

**Starting visibility and persistence** (`top_bar.rs`, `tabs.top_bar_hidden` — a nullable column, migrated in like every other tab column). Absent a persisted choice (`NULL`, the ordinary case), a tab's bar follows the global **`topBar.autohide`** setting (`global_settings`, **on by default** — Settings → Appearance → Top bar's checkbox), so a fresh install behaves exactly as the original, deliberately-partial design did: hidden until asked for (the checkbox is in Settings → "Top bar", with the other two below). Pressing the bar's own **×** persists `1` (explicitly hidden) — it stays closed across a reload, a tab switch back to it, or the window being suspended and reopened, until overridden. `show_top_bar` (below) persists `0` (explicitly shown) — not merely "unclose" back to `NULL`, so it stays shown even if autohide is later turned on, until closed again. Both `init_window_tab`/`add_window_tab`/`tab-navigate` (`TabInitResponse.topBarHidden`) and the live `show-top-bar` event report the *current* answer, so a page never has to compute this itself — `NotesTopBar` simply sets its shown state from `topBarHidden` on every tab bind/switch and from the event.

**The markup** (`top_bar.rs`'s `render_html`, delivered as `TabInitResponse.topBarHtml` and — recomputed from the label just set — `update_tab_resource`'s answer): the same two rows of bullet-separated, styled spans the admin-app's own tab row shows (`AppsTab.tsx`'s `TabTextRow`, mirrored server-side: bold/italic via inline `style`, `mono` via a class), the tab's own tags (unless **`topBar.hideLabels`**) and — for a Notes tab that's showing a source — the root it's in with the root's own tags (unless **`topBar.hideRoot`**; `top_bar::root_of` mirrors the frontend's `rootOfTab` exactly, so the two always agree on a root's guid), all HTML-escaped and rendered **server-side, with no JavaScript** — deliberately no "manage labels" button in it (a page just does `element.innerHTML = topBarHtml`). Its classes (`csdrive-tb-*`) are styled by a **code snippet** (`code_snippets.rs`'s `TOP_BAR_INFO_CSS`, appended to the list every window already applies), so a page gets a consistent look for free without shipping its own CSS for it — reusing the exact mechanism CLAUDE.md's "Android" section describes for platform fixes, now used for shared *look*, not just platform quirks.

**The Back button** (`window_go_back`, granted in `user-apps.json` — every window may call it, not just Notes'): the cross-platform front door to `secondary_windows::go_back`, which used to be Android-only (its hardware Back button, `nativeGoBack`) — undoing one step of tab-switch/link navigation for the calling window. `window_host::navigate_to_url` (new, alongside `navigate`, on both the desktop and mobile platform modules) does the actual navigation from the raw `Url` `go_back` hands back, since that address is reconstructed from what the window showed a moment ago rather than built fresh from a `Page`. `Ok(false)`: nothing to undo — the page's own business what to do then (Notes and `example-toolbar` simply do nothing, same as a second hardware Back press falling through to suspending the window).

**`show_top_bar`** (`secondary_windows.rs`, admin-only — granted in `admin.json`, not `user-apps.json`) sends the `show-top-bar` event to **one open window** (`guid`) or **every open window at once** (no `guid`) via the existing `window_host::emit_if_open`/`emit_to_all`, and — this is what makes it a real *override*, not just a for-now nudge — **persists `top_bar_hidden = 0`** first: for one window, on the tab it's currently showing (`SecondaryWindowsState::current_tab_of`); for every window, on **every tab in the database** (`top_bar::show_every_tab`), open or not, so a suspended window's bar comes back shown too when it's reopened. Two triggers, both in the admin-app:
- **A tab's own row**, in the System/User Apps tabs (`AppsTab.tsx`'s `TabRow`) — a `PanelTop`-icon *Show its top bar* action, offered only while `tab.showing` (the same gate `reload_tab`'s button uses — no open window means nothing to tell), calling `showTopBar(tab.windowGuid)` directly (no backend lookup needed: `TabRecord.windowGuid` is already loaded).
- **Settings → "Top bar" → "Show the top bar for every open window"** — one button, `showTopBar()` with no argument, for when a header is hidden somewhere and there's no reason to hunt down which tab. The same Settings section has the three checkboxes above (`topBar.autohide`/`topBar.hideLabels`/`topBar.hideRoot`), read and written through the ordinary `get_/set_global_setting` like `rowActionsCompact` — no dedicated commands needed for the settings themselves.

**`src/lib/secondaryWindows.ts`** adds `showTopBar(guid?)`, `onShowTopBar(callback)` (`getCurrentWebviewWindow().listen('show-top-bar', …)`, the same "this window only, not the global `listen`" rule as `onTabNavigate`), `setTabTopBarHidden(tabGuid, hidden)` (persists the person's own close) and `windowGoBack()` (the Back button); `TabInitResponse` carries `topBarHtml`/`topBarHidden`, and `updateTabResource` now resolves to `{ topBarHtml }` instead of nothing. **`src/system/notes/topBarInfo.ts`** is a tiny module-level pub/sub store (not React state, since the two things that produce this data — `tabs.ts`'s `registerTab`/`onTabNavigate` handling and its three `report*` functions — sit above `NotesTopBar` in the tree with no other reason to hold a ref to it): `setTopBarInfo`/`setTopBarHtml` publish, `subscribeTopBarInfo` is `NotesTopBar`'s hook.

**`example-toolbar`** (see "Deploying apps") is the reference implementation of "a user app adding its own header of this kind": small inline-SVG icon buttons (Back, Suspend, Close tab, Close window, its own × — a plain html page has no `lucide-react`) in place of the earlier text buttons, `#toolbar-info` holding whatever `topBarHtml` says, starting shown or hidden from `topBarHidden`, its own × calling `setTopBarHidden(true)` to persist the close, and `onShowTopBar` revealing it again — the exact same shape as `NotesTopBar`, minus the window-management difference that Notes' pages always have a tab to close while an arbitrary web app might not need one. The Help tab's API reference documents all of it under "Windows & tabs" (`window_go_back`, `set_top_bar_hidden`, the extended `init_window_tab`/`update_tab_resource` shapes) and the three settings under "App state & global settings", since a page needs to know these exist even though most of them it can only *react* to, never trigger on itself (`show-top-bar`).

Rust-tested (`secondary_windows::tests`, `top_bar::tests`): a fresh tab has no markup and follows autohide; a label produces styled, bulleted markup; closing persists `1`, the global "show" persists `0` and survives autohide being turned back on, and a never-touched tab still follows the (now different) global setting; `root_of` agrees with the frontend's `rootOfTab` for the user folder, a picked folder (by its own label) and a Filen account (by its email), and is `None` for a non-Notes tab or a Notes tab with no source yet; `hideRoot` drops the whole root section while keeping the label. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop): the three Settings checkboxes render with the right defaults and persist; a fresh tab's bar starts hidden; the admin's per-tab "Show its top bar" reveals it live with real markup (a bold label row, a plain second row); closing it (×) and reloading the window kept it hidden; the admin's "show" persisted too — still shown after another reload; the Back button, tested against a real backend tab switch (`activate_tab` between two tabs of the same window), switched back correctly and (confirmed with a JS marker left in the page) did **not** trigger an extra reload; the root section showed "User folder" while browsing it and disappeared live the moment `topBar.hideRoot` was turned on; the CSS snippet's rule was confirmed present and applied via `getComputedStyle`. Verified live on the Android emulator too (real app data, not isolated — a fresh Notes window and tab, `rowActionsCompact` on so "Show its top bar" was reached through the row's own **⋯** menu): the Settings checkboxes, the bar starting hidden then shown live with the same markup, **Close this window** (closed the activity *and* removed its entry — confirmed via `list_secondary_windows` returning none), and `window_go_back` against a real two-tab switch (`add_blank_tab` + `activate_tab`), which correctly returned the window to the first tab — the same mechanism Android's hardware Back button already used internally, now reachable from JavaScript on that platform too and proven to work through the real `android_windows::host::navigate_to_url` path, not just in theory.

## Top bar autohide and nested scrollable content

**Scroll-driven autohide is now shared** (`src/lib/scrollAutohide.ts`, `useScrollAutohide`) by three things that used to be independent (the Help tab's own header) or didn't autohide at all (Notes' top bar, an editor page's own header): a single `scroll` listener on `document`, in the **capture** phase, sees a `scroll` event fired on *any* element in the page even though `scroll` itself never bubbles (capture only needs the target to be a descendant of the listening node, not that the event bubbles back up) — so one listener covers both a page whose own `.tab-content` scrolls (an ordinary long list) *and* a page whose outer area never overflows because it's a fixed-height container with its own nested scrolling region instead, exactly the case reported: a text editor is a header, then a box that is "as tall as its text" and scrolls **internally** (`.code-editor`, see "The file editor") — a listener on `.tab-content` alone, which is what the Help tab's original bespoke implementation used, would never see that inner box scroll at all, since `.tab-content` itself sits at a fixed 100% height and never overflows on an editor page. Each scrolled element's own last `scrollTop` is tracked separately (a `WeakMap`), so switching between scrolling two different areas on the same page never misreads the other's direction. **A newly-seen element defaults its "last" position to 0, not its current `scrollTop`** — found live: defaulting to the current value instead (so the first-ever event for a target always computed a zero delta) silently swallowed the *first* scroll gesture on every element, requiring an unrelated priming scroll before autohide ever kicked in; every scrollable region in this app starts at the top when it mounts, so 0 is the correct assumption and matches what the original single-target implementation got for free by reading `scrollTop` once at mount, before anything had scrolled. The hook returns `[hidden, show, hide]` — `show()`/`hide()` set the state directly for a caller's own trigger, with stable identities (`useCallback`) so passing one as a `useEffect` dependency (subscribing to an external "show it again" event) doesn't resubscribe every render.

**Also shows itself again when the content that hid it stops overflowing at all**, not just on an upward scroll — a `ResizeObserver` attached to every element the hook has seen scroll (lazily, the first time each one fires a `scroll` event) checks on every resize whether that element can still scroll (`scrollHeight > clientHeight`); the moment it can't, there's nothing left for the header to be hidden *from*, so it's shown again rather than staying collapsed until the person happens to scroll up by hand. This is the third part of what was asked for ("auto show back... when the scrollable content changes so that it no longer overlaps the screen") and was not covered by the scroll listener alone: shrinking a file's text a lot (most of it deleted, or a much shorter file opened) changes `scrollHeight` without necessarily firing a `scroll` event with a small enough `scrollTop` to trigger the ordinary "near the top" case, and in fact might not fire any `scroll` event at all if `scrollTop` was already 0 when the content shrank.

**The Help tab's own header** (`HelpHeader` in `HelpTab.tsx`) was refactored onto the shared hook, replacing its bespoke single-target listener — a pure deduplication (the delegated, document-wide listener behaves identically to the old one for a page where `.tab-content` really is the only scroller, which is the Help tab's own case) verified live as a regression: hides on the first scroll past the threshold, comes back on scrolling up or reaching the top, and the table-of-contents' own `goTo` (which force-collapses the header *before* `scrollIntoView` runs, so the two transitions don't race — see "Help tab") now calls the hook's `hide()` instead of a local setter.

**Notes' top bar** (`NotesTopBar.tsx`) now autohides on scroll too, layered on top of its existing *persisted* show/hide (an explicit close, or the global autohide-by-default setting — see "The top bar"): `shown` (persisted) decides whether the bar exists *at all* for this tab — unchanged, still `return null` when closed — and, only while it does, a second, independent `scrollHidden` (from `useScrollAutohide`) decides whether it's momentarily collapsed (`.notes-top-bar-scroll-hidden`, a `max-height`/padding collapse, not `transform` — the same reasoning as `.help-header-hidden`: a `position: sticky` box, or in this case a plain block sitting outside `.tab-content`'s own scroll region, still reserves its height in the flow at a translated position). The admin-app's "show top bar" (`onShowTopBar`, per tab or for every window) now clears the scroll-collapse too, not just the persisted `shown` flag — otherwise pressing "show" while the bar was mid-scroll-collapse would only make it *eligible* to show again on the next upward scroll, not actually show it, which is what "when showing the global top bar from the admin app, the editor's top bar should also be shown" was pointing at for the editor's side of this too (below).

**An editor page's own header** (`.notes-page-header`, doubling as `.editor-top-bar` — the Files tab's own file editor, Notes' File Manager/Note Files Explorer editor in `NotesApp.tsx`, and `NoteEditPage`) gained the identical scroll-driven autohide, each with its **own** `useScrollAutohide` instance. Because all of these — the top bar and an editor's own header — listen to the same underlying `document`-wide scroll events independently, scrolling the editor's own text (`.code-editor`) hides *both* the global top bar and the editor's own header at once with no extra plumbing between them, simply because both hooks see the same event and reach the same conclusion; there is no shared "hidden" state to keep in sync. **Only `NoteEditPage` and `NotesApp`'s editor also subscribe to `onShowTopBar`**, so the admin's "show top bar" reveals the editor's own header alongside `NotesTopBar` — the Files tab has no counterpart "global" top bar (that's a Notes-only concept, see "The top bar"'s own opening line), so its editor's header is scroll-driven only, exactly like the Help tab's. `position: sticky` on `.editor-top-bar` is defensive, not load-bearing today: none of the three editor pages actually let anything scroll *past* this header (`.tab-content` doesn't overflow on any of them; only `.code-editor` does, internally) — it's there in case a future layout change ever makes that true, at no cost while it isn't.

Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop): the Help tab's header still hides/shows exactly as before (regression-checked after the shared-hook refactor, including the first-scroll fix); a note opened in `NotesApp`'s editor, with the admin's "show top bar for every window" pressed first so both bars started visible — confirmed `.tab-content` itself did **not** overflow (`scrollHeight === clientHeight`) while `.code-editor` did, the exact nested-scroll case reported — scrolling `.code-editor` down collapsed both `.notes-top-bar` (`notes-top-bar-scroll-hidden`) and the editor's own `.editor-top-bar` (`editor-top-bar-hidden`) together from one gesture, scrolling back up near the top brought both back together, collapsing them again and then pressing the admin's "show top bar" brought both back without any scrolling, and — with both collapsed again — replacing the file's long text with a single short line (so `.code-editor` no longer overflowed) brought both back on its own via the `ResizeObserver` fallback, with no scroll event involved at all. The Files tab's own editor was checked the same way (scroll down collapses `.editor-top-bar`, scroll up shows it again); it has no global bar to check alongside it, matching the design.

**Asked for again later, as if it were still missing** ("the page itself should not have its own scrollbar... it should always have the height of the viewport and the editor should adapt its height based on the showing/hiding of the header") — this is exactly what the paragraph above already establishes and the one above that already documents (`.tab-content` doesn't overflow on any of the three editor pages; only `.code-editor` does, internally, and it grows into the room the header gives up). Re-verified live anyway rather than just citing the existing text, in case something had regressed since: a fresh build, a real CDP session, both of the two general-purpose hosts (not just one, to be sure this isn't host-specific) — the Files tab's own editor and Notes' File Manager editor, each opened on the same 300-line file. In both, `.tab-content`'s `scrollHeight` measured exactly equal to its `clientHeight` (714/714 and 768/768) with the header shown, and still exactly equal (same `tcScrollHeight`, unchanged) after scrolling the editor down far enough to hide the header — only `.code-editor`'s own `clientHeight` changed (633px → 679px in the Notes case), growing into the space the header gave up, with no page-level scrollbar appearing at any point. **No code change was made**: the requirement was already met, by the same mechanism this whole section documents, and nothing in the live check found a regression.

**Leftover space when hidden — first checked wrong, corrected after the person pushed back with a screenshot.** A first live check only compared the header's own box (height, padding) before and after collapsing — both measured correctly at `0` — and concluded, wrongly, that the report must predate the `max-height`/padding fix above. The person disagreed and sent a screenshot of a note's own window (`NoteEditPage`, opened as a web app): no header visible at all, just the editor's own Undo/Redo toolbar sitting right under the window's title bar — and then a real, visible gap before the text. **The header's own box was never the problem; its *parent's* reserved space was.** `.notes-page-header`/`.editor-top-bar`/`.help-header` all live inside `.tab-panel` (`App.css`, `display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 12px`) — and a flex container's `gap` reserves that 12px **between** two children regardless of either child's own height. Collapsing the header's box to `0` correctly removes everything it owns, but the 12px of *inter-child* spacing its parent reserves for it is a property of the parent, not the child, and nothing had ever told the parent to stop reserving it. Measured directly: the gap between the header and the content it precedes went from 52px (header shown) to 40px (header hidden, first fix) — the *same* 52px minus a missing 12px, not actually smaller because nothing was overflowing. **Fixed** by giving `.help-header-hidden`/`.editor-top-bar-hidden` their own `margin-bottom: -12px` — cancelling exactly the parent's reserved gap, nothing more (kept in sync with `.tab-panel`'s own `gap: 12px` by hand, the same undiven-constant style `max-height: 60px` above already uses) — with `margin-bottom` added to each base rule's own `transition` so the two collapses (the box's and the gap's) animate together, not one snapping ahead of the other. The remaining ~40px below the toolbar in a text editor is the `CodeEditor`'s own always-visible Undo/Redo row (`.code-editor-toolbar`) plus `.code-editor-wrap`'s own 6px gap — real content, not reserved whitespace, and nothing to do with the autohiding header at all; it's identical whether the header above it is shown or hidden. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP) in both the context first measured (the Files tab's own editor, inside the main window) and the one the screenshot was actually of (a note's editor opened in its own standalone secondary window, via `NotesApp`'s File Manager → *Edit as text*): the header-to-toolbar gap measured exactly `0` once hidden in both, and a screenshot of the standalone window afterward showed the toolbar immediately followed by the editor frame, with no unaccounted-for space above the content — the shape the original screenshot showed missing.

**The shake was real, and still present** — reproduced directly (a `MutationObserver` on the editor header's own `class`, jumping `.code-editor`'s `scrollTop` straight to its maximum): five hidden/shown flips inside about 1.5s. The header sits *outside* the element whose scroll drives it (`.code-editor` is a `flex: 1` sibling below it, not a descendant), but collapsing it still **grows `.code-editor`'s own `clientHeight` by exactly the header's height** (measured live: 568px shown, 628px hidden, a flat 60px every time) — the two are flex children of the same column, so the space the header gives up is handed straight to the box below it. Scrolled all the way to the bottom, `scrollTop` was already sitting at the *old* maximum (`scrollHeight - clientHeight`); the instant `clientHeight` grows by 60px, that maximum **shrinks** by 60px too, and the browser clamps `scrollTop` down to fit — a genuine `scroll` event, with a negative delta, that `useScrollAutohide`'s own direction check used to read as "scrolled up" and answer by showing the header again, which shrinks `clientHeight` back down and, since the collapse animates over 0.25s rather than instantly, repeats the whole thing in smaller steps across several frames — exactly the person's own "hiding/showing changes the scrolling container's height, which triggers another scroll in the opposite direction" diagnosis, just one level removed (a *sibling's* height, not the scrolled element's own).

**A first fix tried exactly what was suggested — a bitflag suppressing reaction to scroll events while the hide/show animation is in flight — and it wasn't enough**, caught live rather than assumed correct: a flat, fixed-duration window reopened right into the *next* echo in the same burst (measured gaps between echoes as long as ~480ms, well past the 0.25s CSS transition itself, so a window sized to the transition's own duration wasn't sized to the actual problem); a sliding window that extended itself on every swallowed event survived a burst correctly, but then **silently ate a genuine, deliberate scroll down made shortly after a genuine scroll up** — found live with the very next test: scroll to the top (shows), scroll to an ordinary position 400ms later (should hide) — it didn't, because nothing about the *timing* of a real continued scroll looks any different from an echo's to a clock with no other information.

**The actual fix needs no clock, no window, no suppression state at all** (`lib/scrollAutohide.ts`): the echo has a shape nothing else does — a negative delta that still leaves the element **pinned at its own maximum scroll position** (`top + clientHeight >= scrollHeight`, within `AT_BOTTOM_SLACK_PX`), which a genuine upward scroll, by definition, moves *away from*. The direction check's "show" branch is skipped specifically when that holds; every other branch (hiding on a real downward scroll, showing on reaching the very top, the `ResizeObserver` fallback) fires immediately, with nothing to time out of. Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP): the exact reproduction above (jump to the maximum `scrollTop`) now produces exactly **one** toggle (hide) and settles with no further flips; the previously-broken sequence (show, then a real scroll down 400ms later) now hides correctly every time; a genuine, large (2000px) scroll up made *from* the pinned-at-bottom position still shows the header correctly, confirming the fix only ever suppresses the one specific echo shape and nothing else. `npm run build`, `cargo build` and `cargo test --lib` (250 tests) all clean.

**Typing itself was a third source of the same false-toggle shape, reported separately**: inserting or deleting lines near the bottom of a tall file — which the browser follows with its own "keep the caret visible" auto-scroll — hid or showed the header exactly as if it had been a deliberate scroll, even though nothing about pressing a key is a scroll gesture. Fixed by tracking each scrolled element's own `scrollHeight` alongside its `scrollTop`: a scroll event is skipped entirely (no hide, no show, not even the "near the top" case) whenever `scrollHeight` has changed since the element's last recorded scroll — the signal that tells typing/deleting apart from a wheel turn or a scrollbar drag, neither of which ever changes `scrollHeight` on their own (the module's own doc comment above has the full reasoning). Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP) in a Notes File Manager editor on a 300+ line file: with the header shown and the caret placed near the bottom edge of the viewport, typing 15 new lines in a row pushed `scrollTop` from 380 to 645 (a genuine, large, content-driven jump as the browser followed the caret down) while `scrollHeight` grew in the same stroke — the header stayed **shown** throughout, confirming the fix (without it, that 265px downward jump would have read as a real scroll and hidden it). Ordinary scrolling was re-checked right after, with real CDP mouse-wheel events (not a programmatic `scrollTop` assignment) rather than trusting the typing fix alone not to have broken it: a downward wheel scroll with no edit in between (`scrollHeight` genuinely unchanged) correctly hid the header, and a following upward wheel scroll correctly showed it again — both on the very next tick. **One narrow, self-correcting edge case was found along the way, not fixed, and not considered a regression**: the very first wheel scroll immediately after the typing burst was swallowed (no hide), because the background Worker's exact-highlight pass was still settling `scrollHeight` by a sub-pixel amount at that exact instant, making that one scroll event's `scrollHeight` briefly disagree with what had just been recorded; the *next* scroll event (heights already stable) hid it correctly, as every other scroll did in every other test. This is the same trade-off the module's own doc comment accepts by design — a rare false negative on a scroll that happens to land in the same tick as a content-height settle, versus the alternative (the original bug) of the header flickering on every single keystroke that crosses the viewport edge.

**Re-reported, then re-verified as already fixed by the above.** A later report described the header staying hidden after scrolling up from more than a screen's worth down — read at first as a possible gap in the delta-based direction check (which only looks at the *change* in `scrollTop`, never the absolute distance from the top). Live-tested directly (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP) against the exact shape described: scrolled to 2000px down (several screens on the test window), then a small, ordinary upward scroll of about 100px — the header reappeared correctly, every time. The direction check needs no distance-from-top term at all: `delta < -DIRECTION_THRESHOLD_PX && !pinnedAtBottom` fires on any sufficiently large upward delta regardless of the starting position, so this was already covered by the anti-shake rewrite above and needed no further change. Nothing in the code was touched for this item.

**A genuine, persistent second scrollbar was reported live, on both Notes' file listing and its editor** — originally described as "a horizontal scrollbar" (the person's own correction once asked to describe it precisely: "I meant vertical... a vertical scrollbar for the entire page, that is second to the scrollbar of the listing or the editor... when the page's top header hides, the external scrollbar barely has any space left to scroll, but it's still there — you can scroll just a few pixels with it"). Found by code review, not a live click-through: `.notes-top-bar-scroll-hidden` (`notes.css`), `.help-header-hidden` and `.editor-top-bar-hidden` (`App.css`) all share the identical collapse recipe — `max-height: 0`, padding zeroed, `border-bottom-color: transparent` — for a header whose **shown** state has a real `border-bottom: 1px solid var(--border)`. **`max-height: 0` constrains the content/padding box, not a border's own *width*** — a browser doesn't shrink a border to fit a height constraint, it just paints it at its specified width regardless, so the 1px border was never actually gone when "collapsed," only faded to invisible. `.notes-top-bar` sits *outside* `.tab-content`, as the page's own topmost sibling (this section's own earlier doc comment), so its one leftover pixel adds directly to the whole page's own height once every other, genuinely-zero-height part has collapsed — exactly "barely any space left to scroll... just a few pixels," and exactly why it showed on both the listing and the editor (both are Notes pages, both sit below the identical `NotesTopBar`), not something specific to the editor's own `.code-editor`. **Fixed** by adding `border-bottom-width: 0` alongside the existing `border-bottom-color: transparent` in all three collapsed-state rules — the color transition is left as it was (still a smooth fade, not an abrupt cut, since the rest of the header's own `max-height`/padding collapse is already animating over the identical 0.25s and the border's own instant width change lands inside that same window, not noticeably separate from it).

**Live reproduction needed a real production deploy, not just a debug run** (the only running instance at first was the person's own production app, mid-session, with CDP never enabled for it — launched plainly, and a second, debug instance's own `--remote-debugging-port` has no effect once a production instance already claimed the one shared WebView2 browser host for this app identifier, per the "Folder layout" finding). Read-only `PrintWindow` screenshots of the person's real, already-open windows (never a click, honouring their own "don't navigate away, don't do actual actions" at that point) narrowed down *what* the report meant — the person's own clarification ("I meant vertical... when the page's top header hides, the external scrollbar barely has any space left to scroll, but it's still there") and, crucially, "it's actually visible in this home page too" — which the border-width fix alone could never explain, since the Notes **home** page has no internal scroll of its own at all for a header collapse to even interact with.

**That one clue broke the investigation open**: with the person's own go-ahead to deploy and measure directly, the real build was reinstalled (`cargo tauri build` → silent NSIS install, since the embedded frontend is compiled into the release binary — rebuilding `dist/` alone doesn't reach an already-running release process) and relaunched *with* `--remote-debugging-port` this time, giving real `document.documentElement.scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` access. On the plain home page — no scrolling, nothing collapsed — **`scrollHeight` measured 815px against a `clientHeight` of 768px: a flat, constant 47px of page-level overflow, matching the shown top bar's own real height almost exactly**, and **unaffected by `useScrollAutohide`'s own listener**, which only reacts to a *descendant* of `.tab-content` scrolling — a `window.scrollTo` on the body itself never triggered it. The border-width residual was real (confirmed separately, below) but was only ever the *last few pixels* of this much bigger, constant gap — not its cause. **The actual cause**, found by inspecting the live DOM directly: `index.css`'s `#root { height: 100% }` (shared by every system app) and `.app-shell`'s own `height: 100%` were being asked to coexist as **plain block-level siblings with no flex coordination at all** — `NotesRoot.tsx` returned a bare Fragment, so `<NotesTopBar/>` and `{view}`'s own `.app-shell` just stacked in normal flow, each independently claiming its own "100%" with neither aware of the other. **Fixed** by giving `NotesRoot.tsx` a real wrapping element (`.notes-shell-root`, `notes.css`: `display: flex; flex-direction: column; height: 100%`) so the two are true flex siblings, with a *scoped* override (`.notes-shell-root > .app-shell { height: auto; flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0; }`) switching `.app-shell` from a fixed 100% to "whatever's left after the top bar" — scoped specifically to avoid touching `.app-shell`'s own base rule, which the admin-app's root and every other system app also use, none of which have this same "another bar above it" shape.

Verified live (the real redeployed production build, real data — the person's own actual notebooks, read-only, confirmed via `git status` on the external notebook repo before and after that nothing was touched): on the plain home page, `document.documentElement`'s `scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` went from **815/768 (47px phantom overflow) to 768/768 (zero)** — an exact, measured elimination, not inferred. On a real folder listing (a live picked-folder root, 16 real entries) the same page-level pair measured **0 overflow both before scrolling (top bar shown) and after** a real `.tab-content` scroll correctly flipped the bar to its `notes-top-bar-scroll-hidden` class — confirming the border-width fix holds too: a lingering 1px there would have shown as `overflowAmount: 1`, not `0`, after the collapse. `.tab-content`'s own *legitimate* internal overflow (210px, real content) was undisturbed by any of this, and correctly shrank by exactly the space the collapsing bar gave back (210 → 171, a 39px gain in `clientHeight` matching the bar's own real collapsed-to-shown height difference). A `PrintWindow` screenshot of the real listing after the collapse showed a single, ordinary scrollbar with no second track or artifact. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

**That fix shipped a real regression of its own, reported live immediately after**: "in the text editor page and in listing pages the top header now is barely visible (both when I first open a secondary window and when I scroll back up)" — i.e. squeezed down from its natural height, in exactly the two states (just opened, scrolled back to the top) where it's supposed to be *fully* shown, not collapsed. **Classic CSS flexbox "default min-height:auto" gotcha, on two different elements at once**: making `.app-shell` a genuine `flex: 1 1 auto` sibling of `.notes-top-bar` (above) means the two now compete for space whenever the combined *flex-basis* of both exceeds the container — and `.notes-top-bar` had no `flex-shrink: 0` of its own, so the browser's shrink-distribution algorithm could claw a few pixels back from it even though its sibling never actually needed them (`.app-shell`'s own `min-height: 0` already lets *it* shrink to nothing and rely on `.tab-content`'s internal scroll instead). Compounding it: `.tab-content` itself (`App.css`) had `flex: 1` but no `min-height: 0` of its own — its default `min-height: auto` lets its *own* content's natural (unclipped) height inflate upward into `.app-shell`'s own flex-basis calculation, through the exact chain my own fix had just introduced, feeding the shrink algorithm a bigger number to fight over than it should ever have seen. **Fixed** by adding `min-height: 0` to `.tab-content` (closing the gap at its real source) and `flex-shrink: 0` to `.notes-top-bar` (a second, independent guarantee that holds regardless of whatever else is competing for space — this bar is never meant to shrink below its own natural size while shown, only to collapse outright via its existing scroll-driven class, so protecting it unconditionally is correct, not just a workaround).

Verified live the same way (the real rebuilt-and-reinstalled production app, `--remote-debugging-port` for this one verification pass, real data, read-only): a freshly opened Notes window's listing page measured the top bar's own `getBoundingClientRect().height` at **47px** with `getComputedStyle(...).flexShrink` reading **`"0"`** — its full natural size, matching the very first (pre-regression) measurement exactly; the real Transcript note opened in the actual text editor (via "Go to a path…", not a direct backend call) measured the same **47px** top bar *and* the editor's own inner header (`.editor-top-bar`) at a clean **34px**, with the whole page's own `document.documentElement` overflow still a flat **0**; a `PrintWindow` screenshot of that real editor window showed both headers — the outer bar ("_ · Editing /997/992/299/999/299/0-Transcript[note].md · fdltncvd") and the editor's own ("997/992/299/999/299/0-Transcript[note].md" with its Back/split/link/save icons) — rendered at their full, legible size, nothing compressed. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `cargo test --lib` (276 tests, untouched — pure CSS) clean.

**A scroll inside an open popup used to toggle the page's own header — asked for directly** ("when such a modal is opened, the header shouldn't toggle anymore, as the scroll surely happens upon the content in the modal, not in the main page"). Root cause, confirmed by reading `lib/scrollAutohide.ts`'s own delegation design: the capture-phase `document` listener this whole module is built on sees a `scroll` event fired on *any* element in the page, including one inside a `Modal`'s own popup content — a long list or a tall text box scrolled *inside* the popup fired exactly the same event shape this hook already treats as "the page scrolled," hiding or showing the header behind the modal for a scroll that had nothing to do with it. **Fixed** with one check at the very top of both `onScroll` and `onVirtualScroll`: a target whose nearest `.modal-overlay` ancestor exists (`Element.closest('.modal-overlay')` — the one marker every `Modal` already renders, `components/Modal.tsx`) is skipped entirely, before it ever reaches `decideScroll` — not merely excluded from the hide/show decision, but never recorded into `lastTop`/`lastHeight` or handed to the `ResizeObserver` either, since none of that bookkeeping means anything for a scroller that was never meant to drive this header.

**Verified live** (isolated **debug** data folder — a dedicated, off-the-shared-profile CDP instance, never the production app, which was left running throughout and untouched — desktop, real CDP, the Help tab's own autohiding header): confirmed the baseline first, unmodified — scrolling the real page content down hid the header, scrolling back to the top showed it again, exactly as before this change. Then a fake `.modal-overlay`-wrapped scrollable element (off-screen, invisible, matching the real shape a popup's own content sits in) was scrolled 500px with the header starting shown — **the header stayed shown**, confirmed only after a real delay for React's own state flush, not read synchronously in the same tick (an earlier, synchronous readback of this exact scenario came back "unchanged" for a reason that turned out to be a race in the test script itself — state updates triggered from outside a React-managed event don't flush within the same microtask — not evidence the fix worked, caught and corrected before trusting it). The control case removed all doubt: the identical element, scrolled the identical amount, with the *same* delay before reading — but moved **outside** the `.modal-overlay` wrapper first — correctly hid the header every time, confirming the `.modal-overlay` ancestor check specifically, not some other difference, is what gates the behavior. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

**"The header only shows back when scrolling up when I'm near the start of the page, if I scroll a bit more, the header no longer shows when I scroll up" — reported live, alongside the gap/squish bug above.** Confirmed live, not guessed: a diagnostic capture-phase `scroll` listener installed directly on `document`, dumping every real event's own `scrollTop`/`scrollHeight` across one ordinary scroll gesture, showed something far stranger than "a single collapse taking a moment to settle" — both `scrollTop` *and* `scrollHeight` were oscillating back and forth for the *entire* gesture (one real run: `hidden` flipping across more than twenty consecutive events, `scrollTop` wandering by tens of pixels with no net direction). Root cause: the header sits directly *above* the rest of `.tab-content`'s content, and CSS **scroll anchoring** — on by default, the browser's own "keep whatever's on screen from jumping when something above the viewport changes size" feature — actively adjusts `scrollTop` to compensate every time the header's own collapse/expand changes its height. That compensation is itself a genuine `scroll` event this hook read as a real gesture; reacting to it by flipping `hidden` again changed the header's height *again*, which the browser then compensated for *again* — a self-sustaining feedback loop with no reason to stop on its own. (The original `height === lastH` guard, built for "typing must not toggle the header," only ever *happened* to dampen this by accident, which is exactly why the symptom read as "sometimes works, sometimes doesn't" rather than a visible shake.)

**Fixed in two parts, found only by testing each in isolation rather than trusting the first result.** Part one, `overflow-anchor: none` on `.tab-content` (`App.css`, the same property `markdown.rs`'s own bootstrap script already sets for an unrelated page, see "A note opened as a web app…" above) removes the oscillation itself — re-running the same diagnostic trace afterward showed 3 clean events per gesture instead of 16–24+ chaotic ones. **A first scripted trial measured 0 failures in 15 attempts and looked like a complete fix — it wasn't.** That trial reset aggressively to the top between attempts with a short, randomized delay, which happened to be exactly the shape needed to provoke the oscillation but not the shape of an ordinary person scrolling down, pausing to read, and scrolling back up without returning to the top first. A second, more realistic trial (scroll down ~600px, a genuine ~600ms pause, scroll back up ~600px, repeated with no reset) found the CSS fix alone still failed **5 times in 15**. Tracing a failing case down to individual events found why: with the oscillation gone, each gesture now fires exactly *one* `scroll` event, not a stream of momentum ticks — so nothing ever updates the tracked `lastHeight` between the down-scroll (header shown) and the up-scroll (header's own collapse transition already finished, `clientHeight` settled smaller). The up-scroll event is therefore *always* the first chance to observe the new height, however long the pause was — 600ms, 3 seconds, a minute — because no intermediate event exists to walk `lastHeight` forward in the meantime. A **time-based** fix (bypass the strict height-match guard for a window after a flip was requested) was tried twice for exactly this and failed both times, for exactly this reason: a fixed duration has no bearing on whether an intermediate event ever fired, so no duration — short or long — can be correct.

**Part two is a directional-consistency check, not a clock** (`lib/scrollAutohide.ts`'s `decideScroll`/`consistentWithOwnRequest`, driven by a `lastRequestedHidden` ref in `useScrollAutohide`): instead of asking "has enough time passed since we last changed `hidden`," it asks "does this height change match the direction *we* would expect from our own last request" — shorter after asking to hide, taller after asking to show — which needs no timing assumption at all, since it's judged from what was requested, not when. Verified live with the time-based ref removed entirely: 0/15 on the original adversarial (reset-to-top, short delay) trial, 0/15 and then 0/30 on the realistic (600ms pause, no reset) trial, and 0/6 on a trial using a deliberate 3-second pause specifically to confirm time-independence (any practical fixed-duration guard would have failed this one by construction). For a target where the header isn't part of what's actually being measured (`.editor-top-bar`, a sibling of `.code-editor`, not its parent — unlike `.tab-content`, which contains `.help-header` as a sibling of the very content it measures), this check is inert: nothing about our own requests ever moves that target's height, so a genuine, unexplained change (typing) never happens to line up with whichever direction was last requested, and the original strict guard still applies to it exactly as before.

**Re-verified against Monaco's editor header specifically** (the desktop default editor since "A plain textarea for 'disable all code highlighting'…" above — a genuinely different integration path, since Monaco never fires a native `scroll` event at all and reaches this hook only through `reportVirtualScroll`'s `CustomEvent` bridge): this harness could not reliably give Monaco's own input surface real OS focus to drive it through genuine wheel or keyboard input (a click landed with `document.activeElement` still `BODY`, and CDP wheel events at Monaco's own measured coordinates produced no observable scroll), so the bridge was exercised directly instead — dispatching `csdrive-virtual-scroll` events with the same `{top, height, clientHeight}` shape `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx`'s own `onDidScrollChange` sends. 0/10 failures on a scroll-down-pause-scroll-up trial, and — specifically reproducing the original report's own "works near the top, breaks further down" shape — 0/12 failures on a trial that descends progressively deeper into an 8,000px document on every pass, never returning to the top. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

## Notes' own "Go back one step" — undoing in-app navigation, not only a tab switch

**Reported live: "The back button for the Notes app's header no longer does anything."** Live-tested directly against `windowGoBack`/`window_go_back` (above): it still works exactly as designed — undoing an *admin-triggered tab switch* or a link followed "in this tab" (CLAUDE.md's "Back undoes one navigation before it ever suspends"). What it was never designed to undo, and what the person's own day-to-day use of "Back" actually means, is the ordinary way a person moves around *inside* Notes — clicking into a folder, going to the home page, opening a note — none of which ever touches `previous_shown` at all, so the button looked entirely dead for the one thing most people press it for.

**`src/system/notes/backStack.ts`** is a new, small, process-wide stack of "undo one step" closures, tried *first* by `NotesTopBar`'s "Go back one step" button, falling back to `windowGoBack()` only once it's empty — so both kinds of "back" (in-app, and the pre-existing tab-switch one) work through the same one button without either shadowing the other. Two places push onto it:
- **`NotesRoot.tsx`**'s own `navigate(next)` (replacing every bare `setPlace(...)` call in the file — `onHome`, `onFiles`, `onNotebooks`, `onSettings`, `onShowFolder`, `onOpenNotebook`, and every `onPlace` prop handed to `NotePage`/`NoteEditPage`/`NoteFilesPage`) pushes `() => setPlace(current)` before applying `next` — one entry per discrete switch between Notes' own views (home, notebooks, settings, a note, its editor, the file manager).
- **`NotesApp.tsx`**'s own folder/editor/viewer navigation (`sourceId`, `branch`, `path`, `editing`, `viewer`) is *not* wrapped call-site by call-site — there are too many of them (a breadcrumb, a row click, a search result, "Go to a path…", the editor opening/closing, the media viewer opening/closing) — instead, one `useEffect` watches a **reduced signature** of these five values (`editing?.path`, not the `editing` object itself, so a keystroke — which replaces it with a new object every change — is never mistaken for a navigation step) and pushes an undo entry whenever that signature changes from one render to the next.

**A component can leave itself while its own entries are still on the stack** — pressing NotesApp's own "Notes home" button unmounts it while whatever folder-navigation it did is still sitting there, un-popped. Left alone, those entries would point at a dead instance; a later "Go back" reaching one would silently call a no-op `setState` (React quietly swallows a dead component's `setState`), making the button look broken all over again, just one step later. `backStack.ts` solves this with **owner tags, not a remembered stack length**: `pushOwned(undo, owner)`/`discardOwner(owner)`, `NotesApp` tagging every entry it pushes with one `Symbol()` made fresh at mount and discarding all of them, by that tag, on unmount — wherever they ended up, not by truncating the stack to some earlier length.

**A length-based "checkpoint" was the first design, and it was wrong — caught live, not by inspection.** The natural-looking alternative (`backCheckpoint()`/`discardTo(mark)`: remember `stack.length` at mount, truncate back to it on unmount) fails on exactly the sequence that matters most: `NotesRoot`'s own `navigate({view:'home'})` pushes **its** entry (the one that correctly undoes leaving the file manager) *before* the resulting re-render actually unmounts `NotesApp` — so by the time the cleanup effect runs, that fresh, valid entry already sits on top of `NotesApp`'s own stale ones, and a plain length cutoff discards it right along with them. Reproduced live: file manager → a folder → `Notes home` → **Go back** landed back on **Home**, not the file manager — the "undo leaving" entry had been wiped by the very mechanism meant to clean up after it. Switching to owner tags (`discardOwner`, filtering by tag rather than truncating by length) fixes it precisely because it only ever removes what `NotesApp` itself pushed, leaving `NotesRoot`'s own entry — pushed a moment earlier by the very transition that unmounted it — exactly where it was.

Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP, a fresh Notes window each time): a plain case first — root → `/999-ParentNote`, **Go back** → correctly back at root — then the scenario that broke the first design: root → `/999-ParentNote` (a real navigation step inside the file manager) → `Notes home` (leaving it with that step un-popped) → **Go back** → correctly back at the file manager, not stuck on Home — → **Go back** again → correctly back at root, the earlier step still intact and still poppable, no stale-closure error, no silent no-op. The admin-app's own `show_top_bar` was used to reveal `NotesTopBar` for this fresh, unconfigured test window at each step (its tab-level persisted-hidden override has to be set once per tab/view before the button is reachable — not itself a bug, just this window's own starting state in an isolated instance with no prior configuration).

## Tags

Tags (`window_tags` table) are not specific to windows: `guid` is any opaque id — a window, tab group, tab, or, in the Files tab, a file-manager root (keyed `root:<rootId>`, e.g. `root:user`; picked roots are remembered and keyed by their opaque id, so their tags persist with them; forgetting a root leaves its tags in the database, and they reappear if the same folder is picked again). **Everywhere tags appear they must be editable and sortable**, which is why every place renders them through the one shared `components/Tags.tsx` `TagList` component rather than its own markup: click a badge to edit its text/colors, drag badges (or use the arrows in the edit dialog) to reorder, × to remove. Backend commands: `list_tags`, `add_window_tag`, `update_window_tag`, `reorder_window_tags`, `remove_window_tag`; order is the `sort_order` column (new tags go last). Any new place that shows tags should use `TagList` too.

**A row of tags is shown only when the record has at least one tag** (`TagList` renders nothing for an empty list unless it is given `showWhenEmpty`), so a listing isn't padded with empty tag rows. The first tag is added from the record's **details popup** (below), which is the one place that passes `showWhenEmpty` — the "add tag" button — so every record that can have tags has a details popup, and a new listing with tags gets one too.

**Root tags** are the tags of a guid derived from a root (`src/lib/rootTags.ts`): `root:<rootId>` for a folder on this device (`root:user`, or `root:<id>` for a picked one — an opaque id, never a path), `root:filen:<userId>` for a Filen account. Every place that shows the root shows the same tags — the Files tab's root switcher and the **tab items in the System Apps tab**: a Notes tab (its resource id names its source, `rootOfTab`) shows two captioned lines, **Root** (the root's tags) and **Tab** (its own), so the two sets can't be mistaken for each other. Both are editable from there. A tab that shows no root keeps the single tag line. **Group by root** (System Apps tab only, at the tabs level; saved as `systemAppsTab.groupByRoot`): the tabs of the group are shown under a header per root (its name, and its tags when it has any; a button for its details) — a root's tabs are together, the tabs with no root last under "Tabs that show no root" — and the root is no longer repeated on each tab. Sorting by hand is off while grouped.

## Trusting a folder, once, instead of confirming every destructive change in it

**Asked for directly, with the trade-off raised in the same breath**: deleting an entry, or an upload that would overwrite one, in Notes' File Manager or Note Files Explorer asks every single time (`lib/dialogs.ts`'s `confirm`, under `prompt_guard` — see "Prompts and windows") — tedious when working through many files in the same folder. The person's own proposal: ask once *for the folder*, then allow anything under it (any depth) without asking again, for the rest of the session — and asked directly whether that's "a security deal breaker." **It genuinely does trade away some of the per-action confirmation this app has otherwise built up carefully (the burst counter, "Prevent this app from showing prompts", `docs/app-security.md`'s own stated preference for "fragmenting execution flows through prompts")** — a warning was added to that file's own "Coding Assistant Warnings" (its established place for exactly this kind of trade-off, pending the person's confirmation that this specific implementation matches what they had in mind) rather than deciding alone that it's fine.

**`src/system/notes/trustedFolders.ts`**: an in-memory-only set of `(source+branch, folder path)` pairs — gone at the next restart, the same lifetime as `prompt_guard`'s own "Prevent this app from showing prompts" flag, so a grant can never silently outlive the session it was given in. `isFolderTrusted`/`trustFolder`; trusting a folder covers every path under it (`path.startsWith(trusted + '/')`) and, for the root itself (`path === ''`), *everything* in that source — a root-relative path never actually starts with `/`, so the ordinary prefix check alone would never have matched a subfolder for a root-level grant; handled as its own case. Scoped by **both** the source/root/account *and* the branch — trusting a folder in the main view never trusts the same-named folder of a branch, or of a different root/account, however similar the path looks.

**`NotesApp.tsx`'s `confirmDestructive(folder, question)`** replaces the plain `confirm(question)` at the three places that ask before a destructive change (`deleteEntry`, and the two "replace it?" checks in `uploadFiles`/the clipboard-paste-as-file path): already-trusted → proceeds with no dialog at all; otherwise it asks **once, about the folder**, offering to trust it (and everything under it) instead of just this one action — accepting grants the trust and proceeds immediately, with no second, action-specific question; declining falls back to the plain original question, asked about only this one action, same as before the feature existed, and the folder stays untrusted. **Deliberately not touched**: `window.prompt('New folder name:')`/`'untitled.md'` (creating a folder or file) — these aren't a safety gate to begin with, just asking what to call the new thing, so there is nothing for a folder-trust grant to meaningfully replace there; the branch-specific confirmations (committing with an overwrite, discarding a branch) are also left alone, being a different kind of question (not "delete/overwrite this one entry") that the person's own wording ("deleting a file, creating a folder") didn't describe.

**A real scoping bug was caught live, not by inspection, before trusting the fix**: the very first version's prefix check (`path.startsWith(t.path + '/')`) never actually covered a subfolder when the *trusted* folder was the root itself (`t.path === ''`) — `"cut-paste-test".startsWith('' + '/')` is `"cut-paste-test".startsWith('/')`, which is false for an ordinary root-relative path with no leading slash — so trusting the root would have silently kept asking in every subfolder, the opposite of what was asked for ("anything under that folder, at any depth"). Caught by testing the exact scenario live rather than assuming the general prefix logic covered its own edge case: trusted the root, then tried deleting a file one folder down — the question reappeared when it shouldn't have. Fixed by special-casing an empty trusted path to mean "everything in this source," then re-verified.

Verified live (isolated data folder, desktop, real CDP, real native TaskDialog buttons clicked by their own real `hwnd` via `BM_CLICK` — not a synthetic DOM event, since the dialog itself is a genuine OS window with no JS in it at all): a file at the root of the user folder, deleted — the real dialog's three buttons enumerated directly (`EnumChildWindows`/`GetClassName`: "OK", "Prevent this app from showing prompts", "Cancel") confirmed this *is* the ordinary `confirm_dialog`-plus-burst-guard box, not something bespoke; clicking "OK" (grant trust) deleted the file with **no second dialog** at all, confirmed by the window list going back to having none; a *second* file at the same root, deleted immediately after — **zero dialogs**, confirmed by checking the window list stayed empty throughout, and the file gone from disk; a third file **one folder down** (`cut-paste-test/`), deleted — **also zero dialogs**, confirming the root grant correctly cascaded to a subfolder once the scoping bug above was fixed (re-tested after the fix, not just before it).

## Dev Tools and file logging

`docs/features/features.md`'s first "New Feature": Settings → an **"Advanced"** section at the very bottom (a
plain `<details>`, since it's the one place in the app that needed a collapsible section) has a checkbox,
**"Show the Dev Tools tab"** (`lib/devTools.ts`, admin-app-own state — `settings.devToolsEnabled` — not
`global_settings`: only the admin-app's own tab bar needs to see it, never Notes or a web app). On, a **Dev
Tools** tab appears **just before Help** (`App.tsx`'s `tabsFor`), with, for now, one page: **Logs**
(`components/DevToolsTab.tsx`).

**Everything about what gets logged is Rust-only — the frontend is never told what's being logged, only the
current level, the log file's own size/location, and its recent content** (`logging.rs`, `lib/logging.ts`):
`get_log_level`/`set_log_level`, `get_log_file_info`, `read_log_tail` (the file's last ≤200 KiB, so the page
never loads something huge), `export_log_file` (the same "save as"/Downloads flow every other export uses —
`device_files.rs`; not asked to confirm itself, since this is the admin-app's own button, not a page's). All
five are admin-only (`admin.json`), and log **one line each time they're called too** — the Logs page reading
its own state is itself an admin operation, the same as any other.

**The file: `admin/logs/csdrive.log`** (`layout.rs`'s `LOGS_FOLDER`/`LOG_FILE`/`logs_dir`/`log_file_path`) —
inside `admin/`, so it's a **protected path** no ordinary file command can reach (the Logs page is the only
way to see it or get it off the device), and it travels with a relocated data folder and is gone when the
folder is wiped, the same as `data.db` and the Filen sessions file. On Windows that's ordinarily
`%APPDATA%\com.ayran.csdrive-webhost-tauriapp\admin\logs\csdrive.log`; on Android, the same relative place
inside the app's private storage. **Created lazily** — on the *first* line actually written, not at startup —
and **rotated** past 5 MiB: renamed to `csdrive.log.old` (replacing any earlier one) and a fresh one started,
so a `Trace` session left running can't grow it without bound while a full rotation's worth of detail stays
available.

**A plain `log::Log` implementation of our own** (`FileLogger`), not `tauri-plugin-log` — that plugin has its
own JS-visible surface (console/webview logging, configured from the frontend), which would contradict
"solely in Rust" outright. `log::set_logger` makes it the process's **only** logger, at `log::set_max_level
(Trace)` always — the *real* filtering is `FileLogger`'s own runtime-adjustable atomic (`enabled()`), so
raising the level (`set_log_level`) takes effect at once, with no re-registration. **Only our own crate's
targets are ever written** (`OUR_TARGET_PREFIX`) — being the *only* logger means every crate that logs
through the `log` facade reaches it, not just this app's own code, and `sqlx`'s own per-query debug logging
(full SQL text, one line per query) turned up in the file the first time the level was raised past Info,
noise this feature never asked for and outside everything the level table below promises; filtering by
target, before a line is even formatted, keeps it out categorically rather than trying to quiet `sqlx`'s own
logging at the source. Writes are a plain synchronous open-append-close under one `Mutex` (that also guards
rotation) — not a background writer — cheap enough for what a diagnostic logger actually logs, and the
simplest thing that can't tear a line across two files or two threads' writes across each other.

**What each level adds** (Off logs nothing; each level below includes everything above it), and where it's
called from — `require_admin` and `set_app_state`/`set_global_setting` are existing choke points that already
had exactly one call site to add a line to, rather than needing one in each of the many commands that pass
through them:
- **Error / Warn** — Filen request failures (`filen::api`, below).
- **Info** — every **admin-only operation** (`window_host::require_admin` takes a short `operation` name now,
  logged on success — the one choke point all ~20 admin-only commands already pass through) and every
  **notebook added or removed** (`app_state::set_app_state` special-cases the one key Notes keeps its
  notebook list under, `notes.notebooks` — see "Notebooks" — diffing the old and new list by `guid` and
  logging each side's own difference by *title*; `logging::log_notebooks_changed`).
- **Debug** — every **Filen request** (`filen::api::send_json`/`get`/`download_chunk`/`upload_chunk`, the
  handful of functions every Filen operation ultimately calls): one line each, the endpoint (or chunk
  coordinates) and the outcome.
- **Trace** — the same Filen requests again, but **before** sending too, not just after; every **local
  file-system request** (`fs_scope::FsScope::check_in`, the one choke point every `fs_*` command and SQLite's
  own path resolution passes through — the root and the relative path, never the resolved real one, same as a
  refusal); every **tag added, changed, reordered or removed** (`secondary_windows.rs`, by id — never a tag's
  own text); every **app-state or global-setting change** (the same two choke points above — by *key* only,
  never the value, which could be a draft's own text or anything else a settings page keeps).

**Never logged: a file's contents, or the text of a search** — nothing here reads either into a log line in
the first place. What *is* logged where it's useful for finding a problem: names, relative paths, Filen item
ids, keys and counts.

Rust-tested (`logging::tests`): every level round-trips through its persisted string; a logger only writes
what its own level allows, and picks up a raised level at once; a third-party crate's own logging (a fake
`sqlx::query` record) never reaches the file, at any level, and never even creates it; a file past the size
limit rotates instead of growing, and the fresh file after rotating doesn't carry the old size forward; the
notebook diff logic detects an add and a remove from two lists that share one entry. Verified live (isolated
data folder, desktop): Settings' checkbox correctly showed and hid the Dev Tools tab **only after being fixed**
— a first version read the setting with a plain per-component `useState`, so checking it in Settings never
updated `App.tsx`'s own separate copy and the tab never appeared; rewriting `devTools.ts` on the same
module-level-cache-and-listeners shape `rowActionsCompact.ts` already uses (not a new pattern — the existing
one, applied where it was missing) fixed it, confirmed by the tab appearing immediately and surviving a
restart; the Logs page's level radios, file path/size and recent content all reflect the real file; raising
the level to Trace and browsing the Files tab produced real `fs:`/`tag added:`/`app state changed:` lines with
no real path or value in any of them, confirmed by reading the file directly on disk; lowering it back to
Info and repeating the same actions produced **no** new lines (confirmed by an unchanged line count) —
filtering genuinely happens, not just display-side; a hand-simulated notebook list change produced exactly
"notebook added" then "notebook added" + "notebook removed" for the second write, from a real `set_app_state`
call; the export button opened a real native "Save As" dialog. **Not exercised live**: Filen request logging
(no live Filen account in the isolated instance this pass) — covered by the code review of where the lines
were added and by the existing test coverage of the functions they sit in, not by watching real lines appear
for a real request.

## Seeding demo data for the User Apps tab

`csdrive-webhost-tauriapp/src-tauri/examples/seed_demo_data.rs` populates the real `data.db` with demo data for the 9 sample apps in `user/qwer/` (`index1.html`-`index9.html`), for stress-testing the User Apps tab's nested windows/tab-groups/tabs UI at scale. To run it after a reset (or any time):

```
cd csdrive-webhost-tauriapp/src-tauri
cargo run --example seed_demo_data
```

It requires `data.db` to already exist and `user/qwer/index1.html`..`index9.html` to already be deployed (run the app once, and copy `sample-apps/qwer/` into the real `user/qwer/` folder, before running the script). It finds the real data folder itself the same way the app does, including a relocated custom data folder. Layout it creates: one `secondary_windows` entry per app, except `index1.html` which gets 10; the first of those 10 windows gets 10 `tab_groups`; the first of those groups gets 100 `tabs`. Safe to re-run — every row it creates is tagged `seeded:qwer-demo-data`, and each run starts by deleting only rows carrying that tag before recreating them, so it never touches any other window/tab/tag you created yourself.

## A folder-based notebook showed `The notebook "undefined"` in User Action

**Found live while verifying the User Action wiring above, fixed as its own item afterward.** `UserActionModal.tsx`'s own `where` string read `scope.notebook.title` directly — correct for a file-based notebook, but a folder-based one (see "A folder can be used directly as a notebook" above) has no `title` field at all by design, so this always printed the literal string `undefined`. The already-existing `notebookTitle(entry)` helper (`notebooks.ts`) exists for exactly this — it returns the file's title for a file-based entry and the notebook's own relative path (e.g. `/ua-bug-test-folder`) for a folder-based one — and every other place in the app that shows a notebook's name already goes through it (`NotebooksPage.tsx`, `InsertPathModal.tsx`, `NotePage.tsx`'s breadcrumb); `UserActionModal.tsx` was the one place that had been missed. Fixed by importing it and swapping the one line: `` `The notebook "${notebookTitle(scope.notebook)}"` ``. A repo-wide grep for `.notebook.title`/`scope.notebook.` confirmed this was the only remaining direct read of the file-only field in a notebook-display context.

Verified live (isolated debug data folder, desktop, real CDP, through the real UI — a real folder registered as a notebook via `fs_mkdir` + `set_app_state('notes.notebooks', …)` in a real Notes window's own context, then navigated to and its User Action launch button clicked with no page chosen, which opens this exact modal): before the fix, `The notebook "undefined"`; after rebuilding with the fix actually embedded (the first attempt reproduced the bug unchanged because only `npx tsc --noEmit` had been run, not `npm run build`, before compiling the binary — a reminder that type-checking alone never updates the embedded `dist/` a debug build bakes in), the modal correctly read `The notebook "/ua-bug-test-folder"`. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

## Monaco/CodeMirror's reserved menu slot was dead space once the User Action pair became a real toolbar button

**Reported live: "on the editor's top header on desktop there is some space left to the right side of the icons that is no longer necessary."** `.code-editor-menu-slot` (`App.css`) is a plain, empty, 110px-wide spacer `<span data-text-menu-target />` that `TextFieldMenu`'s own floating portal docks its cluster onto, for the **built-in** editor (`HighlightedEditor`/`PlainTextEditor`) — but `TextFieldMenu` never attaches to Monaco or CodeMirror at all (their real editing surfaces aren't a plain `HTMLTextAreaElement`/`HTMLInputElement`, already documented above), so when `{extra}` (the User Action pair) became a real, directly-rendered toolbar item in both wrappers' own `.code-editor-toolbar`, the trailing `<span className="code-editor-menu-slot" data-text-menu-target />` after it kept being copied along out of habit — reserving 110px of width nothing could ever dock into, pushed to the row's own right edge by `justify-content: flex-end`. Fixed by simply deleting that one `<span>` from both `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx` and `MobileCodeMirrorEditor.tsx` — `{extra}` is already a normal flex child, needing no reserved slot of its own.

Verified live (isolated debug data folder, desktop, real CDP): the Monaco toolbar's own buttons, previously ending well short of the frame's own right edge, now extend flush to it with no trailing gap — confirmed by direct `getBoundingClientRect()` reads before and after (the last button's own `right` moved to match the toolbar's own `right` edge once the slot was gone). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change.

## The editor-page header's title and action buttons: two earlier fixes, both superseded by a measured, two-part design
