**The editor-page header (`.editor-top-bar`) went through three designs before this one, each reported live after the last shipped** — condensed here rather than told in full a third time (the git history has the earlier two's own complete accounts): first, letting the title wrap across several lines, clipped unreadably by the header's old fixed 60px height; then `nowrap` + the title's own `min-width: 0`, which could squeeze the title to **literally zero width** once there wasn't room for it beside the header's own buttons — wrapping it one *character* per line — while buttons that didn't fit ran off the edge of the screen entirely, unreachable.

**Asked for directly, after both of those**: the title should never wrap to more than one line at all (a long one "covering half the screen" is worse than losing its tail to an ellipsis) — on a wide window the title and every action button always share its one row, the title truncating as needed; on a narrow one the title is still always one line, but the action buttons only join it on that same line when there's genuinely room for both — otherwise all of them drop to a row of their own below it, instead of some of them barely squeezing in beside a cramped title.

**The design, in `App.css` and a new shared hook (`lib/editorHeaderActions.ts`)**: the title (`h2`) gets `white-space: nowrap; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis` unconditionally — no breakpoint needed, truncating what doesn't fit is correct at any width. The header's trailing buttons (everything after the title — Discard, Save, the web-app buttons, Insert a path, the User Action pair, and so on) are now wrapped in their own `<div className="editor-header-actions">` in all three hosts (`NoteEditPage.tsx`, `NotesApp.tsx`'s File Manager/Note Files Explorer editor, `FilesTab.tsx`'s own editor) — a single flex item, `flex: 0 0 auto`, that either sits on the title's own row or gets pushed to a full line of its own (`flex-basis: 100%` via `.editor-header-actions-wrap`) depending on **measured** width, not a guessed breakpoint: `useEditorHeaderActionsWrap` compares the actions group's own natural width (the sum of its children — fixed-size icon buttons, but also a plain "unsaved changes" text span and `CacheMenu`, which aren't) against the header's real available space (minus the leading "Back" button and a reserved minimum for the title), toggling the wrap class accordingly — because how many buttons a given host has varies enormously (four for the Files tab's own editor, ten for `NoteEditPage`'s own once the User Action pair and the note's three web-app buttons are counted), so no single fixed pixel threshold could work for all three. **Wide screens (asked for as "more than 800 or 1000px," picked the middle, 900px) never wrap at all** — a plain `@media (min-width: 900px)` rule cancels the wrap class outright regardless of what the hook measured, so the title simply truncates further instead.

**Two real, independent bugs were found live getting the measurement itself right, both before this ever shipped — not after:**

1. **The actions group's own natural width can't be read from its own box** (`scrollWidth`/`offsetWidth`) **once it's already been pushed to its own line.** `.editor-header-actions-wrap`'s `flex-basis: 100%` *stretches* the group's box to that row's full width regardless of how little its content actually needs — so measuring the box itself, after the fact, reads the row's width back, not the buttons'; acting on a measurement that changes purely as a side effect of having just acted on it is exactly the shape of a feedback loop. Fixed by summing each **child's** own `offsetWidth` instead (`IconButton`, the text span, `CacheMenu` alike) — a child's own natural size is unaffected by how wide its parent's box happens to be, so this stays stable in every state the group can be in.
2. **A real React pitfall, caught live, not by inspection: `useLayoutEffect(fn, [])` only runs once, immediately after the component's *first* commit — and all three hosts conditionally render `null` until their own sources finish loading**, so on that very first commit the header doesn't exist at all, and the refs the effect captured are permanently `null`; the effect never gets a second chance once the header actually mounts on a later render. Confirmed directly: a temporary debug marker written at the very top of the effect showed `{hasHeader: false, hasActions: false, hasLeading: false}` on every check, including a *fresh* remount of the same component performed well after the page had settled — ruling out a timing race and pointing squarely at the empty-deps effect never re-running. Fixed by switching to **callback refs** (`useCallback`, not `useRef` + a mount effect) — React calls a callback ref the moment its DOM node actually appears, on whatever render that happens to be, so the `ResizeObserver` gets armed exactly when there's something real to observe, regardless of how many renders it took to get there.

## The editor's own row of icon buttons (Undo/Redo/clipboard/etc.) now wraps instead of overflowing

**Reported live, specifically about mobile: "the editor's row of icon buttons contains a lot of buttons and all of them need to stay where they are. But on mobile the undo button is already not visible because it's overflowing the screen width."** This is `.code-editor-toolbar` (`App.css`) — the one class shared, verbatim, by all four editor-surface JSX usages (`CodeEditor.tsx`'s own `HighlightedEditor` and `PlainTextEditor`, `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx`, `MobileCodeMirrorEditor.tsx`) — which had `display: flex; justify-content: flex-end` and no `flex-wrap` at all, so a row too wide for its container simply stayed one line, overflowing. **The reported symptom's own direction was the tell**: `justify-content: flex-end` packs an *unwrapped*, oversized line against the container's *right* edge — pushing whatever sits at the row's own *left* end (Undo, always first in every one of the four JSX lists) off the *left* edge of the screen, not merely crowded but genuinely unreachable; CodeMirror's own mobile toolbar is the longest of the four (up to ten buttons once the User Action pair is counted via `extra`), so it's the one most likely to actually hit this on a real phone's own width.

**Fixed with one property**: `flex-wrap: wrap` on `.code-editor-toolbar`. A flex container distributes items onto lines strictly in DOM order, filling each line with as many as fit before starting the next — `justify-content` only ever decides how the items **already placed** on a given line are packed within it, never which line an item lands on — so Undo, being first in every one of the four button lists, is guaranteed to land on the row's own first line regardless of how many trailing buttons wrap to a line below it; nothing about the buttons' own identity, order, or icon changed, matching "all of them need to stay where they are" literally. Inert whenever the row already fits (every desktop case, and most mobile ones too, per the live measurements below) — wrapping only ever activates once the content genuinely exceeds the container's width, exactly "wrap when its length is greater than the viewport width," not an unconditional multi-row layout for every editor regardless of room.

Verified live (isolated **toolbarwraptest** scratch data folder, reverted and confirmed by a clean `git diff` on `layout.rs` afterward; desktop, real CDP, a real file opened in the Files tab's own Monaco editor — chosen for a fast iteration loop, since the fix is pure CSS shared identically by all four JSX usages, not something that needed re-proving once per editor): at the window's own ordinary width (1024px) and at a real phone width (390px, `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`, `mobile: true`), the 7-button Monaco toolbar already fit on one line in both cases (`right: 374` against a 390px viewport) — a useful negative control confirming the fix doesn't force wrapping where none is needed. Forced to an artificially narrow 200px (there being no real device narrower than the smallest phone width already checked, and the point is the wrapping *mechanism* itself, which is viewport-and-button-count agnostic CSS, not a property of Monaco's own 7-button count specifically) correctly produced two lines — the first four buttons (Undo, Redo, Select paragraphs, Copy) at `top: 158`, Undo itself at `left: 30, right: 64`, fully inside the 200px viewport; the remaining three (Paste, Open link, Edit/Insert link) wrapped to a second line at `top: 198` — every button's own `right` edge within the viewport, none off-screen in either direction. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; pure CSS, no Rust change, so `cargo build`/`cargo test --lib` don't apply. **Not separately exercised live**: the real CodeMirror mobile toolbar's own ten-button case on the Android emulator — the fix is the identical shared CSS rule already proven correct above, with nothing kind-specific to CodeMirror that could make it wrap differently.

Verified live (isolated debug data folder, desktop, real CDP — the second bug specifically needed `cargo tauri dev` instead of the usual embedded-build cycle, since iterating on a React-internals bug through a full Rust rebuild each time was too slow; confirmed the dev-mode and embedded-build code paths are otherwise equivalent by reproducing the final result both ways): a real folder-based notebook and a real note created through the actual "New note…" dialog, a 55-character title, reused across all three hosts. At 390px: `NoteEditPage`'s own ten-button header showed the title truncated to one real line (`"A Reasonably Long Note Title For …"`, `ellipsis` computed, never multi-line) sharing its row with "Back" only, and all nine trailing buttons wrapped cleanly to a dedicated second row below — a screenshot confirmed no overlap with the editor's own inner toolbar on the third row; the Files tab's own four-button case and `NotesApp.tsx`'s own File Manager editor both correctly kept their (fewer) trailing buttons **on the title's own line**, truncating the title further instead of wrapping — confirming the "enough room" measurement goes the right way in both directions, not just the crowded one. At a wide 1400px: the identical ten-button `NoteEditPage` header stayed on one single line, title shown in full (room enough that no truncation was even needed). The 900px override was verified directly, independent of whether this specific button count would ever naturally trigger it at that width: forcing the wrap class on by hand at 850px showed `flex-basis: 100%, flex-wrap: wrap` taking effect; the identical forced class at 950px showed `flex-basis: auto, flex-wrap: nowrap` — the override cancelling it outright, exactly the "wide screens never wrap, full stop" guarantee asked for. The listing-page header variant (`NotebooksPage`/`NotePage`/etc.) is provably untouched by this change — nothing in it was edited, only new `.editor-top-bar`-specific rules were added alongside its own, pre-existing ones. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change, so `cargo test --lib` doesn't apply.

## The `window.ayran` global object's own name is now configurable

**"About the ayran javascript global object from previous todo: instead of simply hardcoding it, let's make
it configurable through the app settings in the admin app. The default still will be 'ayran'."** A direct
follow-up to the `window.ayran.events.onTabInitialized` namespace the markdown bootstrap script
(`markdown.rs`'s `BOOTSTRAP`) creates for a note/markdown web app, above — originally hardcoded as
`window.ayran` so a future addition had "a home that isn't the top-level `window` object." The name itself
is now a setting, not a literal.

**`ayran_global.rs` is the exact shape `ayran_tags.rs` already established**, for a single string instead of
three: one choice for the whole app in `global_settings` (`ayranGlobal.name`), cached in memory (`CURRENT`)
so `markdown.rs`'s rendering path — hit on every markdown page a window opens — never touches the database;
`init()` reads it once at startup. **Admin-only** (`get_/set_ayran_global_name`, `admin.json`, the same
trust level `ayranTag.*` already has): this decides the shape of *every* markdown web app's own global JS
surface, not a page's own business. **Unlike the ayran tag's own `tag_name`, this one can never be
blanked** — the global itself is core plumbing (`onTabInitialized`'s cache-and-replay mechanism), not an
optional feature a file opts into, so `set_ayran_global_name` refuses anything that isn't a real, unquoted
JavaScript identifier (`is_valid_identifier`: an ASCII letter/`_`/`$` first, then letters/digits/`_`/`$`,
≤ 60 characters) — the name is spliced directly into `window.<name>` in the bootstrap script's own source,
never through a `window['...']` lookup that could tolerate an arbitrary string.

**The substitution is scoped to the bootstrap script's own text, never the whole rendered page.**
`render_page` gained a fourth parameter, `global_name: &str`, and does `BOOTSTRAP.replace("ayran", global_name)`
— deliberately *not* a replace over the final, assembled page (title and body included): a person's own
markdown can perfectly well contain the literal word "ayran" (it is a product name in its own right, with
nothing to do with this feature), and rewriting that would be a real, silent content-corruption bug for no
reason connected to what this setting actually configures. Every occurrence of the substring "ayran" inside
`BOOTSTRAP` itself — the three live `window.ayran...` statements and the handful of comments that mention
the same namespace by name — belongs to this one feature and nothing else (confirmed directly: `BOOTSTRAP`'s
own source has no other use of the word), so replacing all of them together, including inside its own
comments, is correct and keeps the shipped comment text accurate to whatever name is actually configured.

**Settings → "Markdown: the ayran global object"** (`SettingsTab.tsx`, right below the existing ayran-tag
fields): one text box, committed on blur like the tag fields beside it — but unlike those, the backend can
genuinely refuse this one, so a rejection reverts the box to whatever is still actually stored (a fresh
`get_ayran_global_name`) rather than leaving it showing a value that was never saved.

Rust-tested (`ayran_global.rs`): the default is `"ayran"` and it is itself a valid identifier;
`is_valid_identifier` accepts letters/`_`/`$`-led names up to 60 characters and refuses an empty string, a
digit-led one, anything with a hyphen/dot/space, and a 61-character one. `markdown.rs`'s own new test,
`a_custom_global_name_renames_the_bootstraps_own_namespace_and_nothing_else`: a custom name ("csdrive")
correctly renames all three `window.<name>` statements with no `window.ayran` left over, **and** a markdown
file whose own title and body both genuinely contain the word "ayran" (`# ayran` / `I like ayran, the
drink.`) renders with that text completely untouched — the regression test for exactly the scoping mistake
the design above calls out.

Verified live (isolated **ayranglobaltest** scratch data folder — its own `DEFAULT_DATA_FOLDER` and a
dedicated `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER`/CDP port, built with `cargo build --features tauri/custom-protocol` so
the embedded frontend is actually served rather than Tauri's dev-mode `devUrl` — reverted and confirmed by a
clean `git diff` on `layout.rs` afterward; desktop, real CDP, through the real command stack, a real markdown
file written with genuine bytes — `new TextEncoder().encode(...)` passed directly as the invoke body, **not**
`Array.from(...)` around it, which silently serializes as literal comma-separated decimal text instead of
real bytes, the exact test-script mistake this project's own history already warns about and which this pass
walked into once before catching it from the symptom (the title falling back to the file name and the body
missing its expected text) and fixing the script, not the feature): `get_ayran_global_name` read back the
default `"ayran"`; fetching the file's own rendered page (same-origin, from another already-open web app
window, to sidestep a one-off CDP window-discovery flake documented below) showed the default
`window.ayran = window.ayran || {}`/`window.ayran.events.onTabInitialized`, the literal body text `I like
ayran, the drink.` preserved, and the title correctly `ayran` (the heading); calling `set_ayran_global_name`
with `"csdrive"` and refetching the same page showed `window.csdrive`/`window.csdrive.events.onTabInitialized`
with **zero** occurrences of `window.ayran =` left, while the body's own `"I like ayran, the drink."` and the
`ayran` title were **both still exactly as they were** — the scoped-substitution guarantee holding live, not
just in the Rust test; a malformed name (`"1-bad"`) was refused with the exact validation message and left
the stored name unchanged; a non-admin secondary window's own attempt to call `set_ayran_global_name` was
refused by the capability system itself ("not allowed on window... referenced by: capability: admin-app,
permission: allow-set-ayran-global-name"), the same enforcement `ayranTag.*` already gets; reverted to
`"ayran"` afterward. A real markdown file was also opened as a brand-new secondary window (not just fetched
same-origin) and confirmed to carry a genuine, working `window.ayran` with the correct page title, proving
the feature works through the ordinary "open as web app" path too, not only a same-origin `fetch`.
**One live flake, not a bug in this feature**: the very first attempt to open a *new* window for the test
markdown file left it stuck with no CDP target ever appearing (the OS window existed, visible, but blank) —
traced to the test write having used the `Array.from(...)` mistake above, which made the "page" being
rendered contain no real heading or body text at all; a direct same-origin `fetch` of the same (bad) content
from an already-open window returned correctly and quickly, proving the backend itself was never hung —
re-running the identical open-a-new-window step once the file held real bytes succeeded within 500ms, with
no further occurrence across several repeats. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build`, `cargo build` and
`cargo test --lib` (292 tests) all clean throughout.

## ArrowDown/ArrowUp between a page's own top row and its second, page-specific header

**"Regarding the system of keyboard shortcuts... there are pages in our app where there is a second
page-specific top header (mainly our text editor has its own row of buttons). The buttons on that second
page-specific header should also be navigable in the same fashion the page's top header. So pressing Down
Key will navigate to the first button on that row. Likewise, pressing Up Key will navigate back to the
first button on the page's top row."** A follow-up to the keyboard-shortcuts feature in
`docs/features/features.md`/CLAUDE.md's own "Keyboard" section — not a new mechanism, one more marker and
one more pair of keys inside the existing `lib/buttonRowKeyboard.ts`.

**`[data-secondary-actions]`** is the new marker, the counterpart of the existing `[data-primary-actions]`
(the page's own top row, reached with Alt+Shift+J) — added to the one real instance of "a second
page-specific header" in the app today: `.code-editor-toolbar`, in all three places it's rendered
(`CodeEditor.tsx`'s `HighlightedEditor`/`PlainTextEditor`, `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx`, `MobileCodeMirrorEditor.tsx`
— the Undo/Redo/Find/etc. row that sits directly below an editor page's own `.editor-top-bar
[data-primary-actions]`). `ROW_SELECTOR` (what `.closest()`/the "move within a row" logic already treats
as *a* row of buttons) grew the new selector too, so Left/Right/Home/End/PageUp/PageDown/the fast-nav keys/
Escape all already applied to this row for free, with no change needed — matching "navigable in the same
fashion" for everything except the one new thing that wasn't already true: moving *between* the two rows.

**One new `if` block, placed after the existing Alt+Shift+J/Alt+J handling and before the "move within a
row" switch**: with no modifier keys held, **ArrowDown** while focus is inside `[data-primary-actions]`
jumps to the first (enabled) button of `[data-secondary-actions]`; **ArrowUp** while focus is inside
`[data-secondary-actions]` jumps back to the first button of `[data-primary-actions]` — found the same way
Alt+Shift+J's own lookup already finds the primary row (the open modal if there is one, else the page's own
`.tab-content`), so this can only ever jump between the one primary and the one secondary row that actually
belong to whatever's on screen. A page with no secondary row (everything except the three editor hosts
today) simply finds nothing to jump to and falls through unchanged — no new behavior, no regression, since
neither key was ever handled by the "move within a row" switch below it.

Verified live (`cargo tauri dev`, no Rust change at all so the debug build's own already-isolated default
data folder — see "Folder layout" — needed no scratch override this time; real CDP, real trusted
`Input.dispatchKeyEvent` sequences, not synthetic DOM events, through the actual Files tab and a real text
file opened for editing — Monaco, the desktop default editor): a real Alt+Shift+J correctly focused "Back"
(`parentRow: primary`); a real ArrowDown correctly jumped to the secondary row's first *enabled* button
("Select paragraphs" — Undo/Redo start disabled on a freshly-opened file with nothing to undo yet, and
`buttonsIn`'s existing `:not(:disabled)` filter already skips them, exactly as it already does for the
primary/list rows); a real ArrowRight then moved correctly to the next secondary-row button ("Copy the
selection to the app's clipboard"), confirming Left/Right still work inside this row unchanged; a real
ArrowUp from there correctly jumped back to "Back" (`parentRow: primary`) — proving the return jump isn't
tied to having started from the secondary row's own first button. **Regression-checked on a page with no
secondary row** (Settings): Alt+Shift+J focused its own first primary-row button, and a real ArrowDown
afterward left the focus completely unchanged — confirmed by comparing the focused element before and
after, byte for byte. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change, so `cargo build`/
`cargo test --lib` don't apply.

## Alt+Shift+J toggles back to the list, and a real event-listener registration-order race, found live

**A follow-up to the item above, asked for directly: "while a page's top header or page-specific header is
being focused, pressing Alt+Shift+J again will refocus the current item in the main list. Note: while a
page's top header or page-specific header is being focused, Down and Up arrows no longer move across the
main list, even when there is no page-specific header (just the page top header), and the left and right
arrows no longer navigate up or down the list hierarchy, but through the top row of buttons."** The second
half of that sentence — read at first as simply restating the two rows' own intended behavior — turned out
to describe something that wasn't actually true yet: a real, live-reproduced bug, not a wording
clarification.

**Toggling back** needed one new branch in `buttonRowKeyboard.ts`'s own Alt+Shift+J handler: pressed while
`document.activeElement` already sits inside `[data-primary-actions]` or `[data-secondary-actions]`
(`currentRow()`, a small new helper doing the same `.closest(ROW_SELECTOR)` lookup the rest of the module
already relies on), it blurs the focused button instead of re-finding and re-focusing the same row. A plain
`blur()` is enough — `useListKeyboard`'s own `[data-kbd-focused]` marker is independent React state, never
tied to real DOM focus in the first place (a list item is never actually focusable — see `keyboard.ts`'s own
`kbdItem`), so it was never disturbed by a button having the browser's own focus; giving that focus up is
already "refocusing the current item in the main list," since the very next arrow key now has nothing
competing for it. Deliberately scoped to the primary/secondary rows only, not `.row-actions` — a list item's
own action row keeps its existing behavior (Alt+Shift+J from there still jumps to the page's own top row).

**The real bug: Up/Down/Left/Right, with focus genuinely inside the primary or secondary row, were
*actually reaching* `useListKeyboard`'s own handler too** — not merely a risk, confirmed live on a real
listing page (a toolbar button focused via Alt+Shift+J, a list item already keyboard-focused below it):
ArrowDown/ArrowUp/ArrowRight all silently moved the list's own `[data-kbd-focused]` marker (ArrowRight even
opened the focused folder, through the list's `onOpen`) while the visibly-focused button never moved —
exactly backwards from what both this module's own code and the person's report said should happen.
**Root cause: two independent, plain bubble-phase `window.addEventListener('keydown', ...)` listeners**
(`useButtonRowKeyboard` here, `useListKeyboard` in `keyboard.ts`) **cooperate only through
`e.defaultPrevented`** — whichever one calls `preventDefault()` *first* is the one whose own reaction
actually "wins," and which one that is depends entirely on React's own effect-mount order for a given page,
not on anything either module's code says. On this page, the list's own listener happened to mount (and
thus run) first for these keys.

**Two matched fixes, confirmed live to each be necessary and together sufficient — neither one alone was
enough:**
1. **Up/Down now call `preventDefault()` unconditionally** the moment focus is in either row, before this
   only called it when a cross-row jump actually found somewhere to go — so ArrowDown with no secondary row
   on screen (or ArrowUp with no primary row, kept symmetric even though it can't happen in practice) fell
   through unclaimed. Left/Right already called `preventDefault()` unconditionally once any row had focus
   and needed no change of their own here — but, per fix 2 below, "already calls it" was never sufficient
   by itself when the *other* listener could still run first.
2. **The whole listener moved from the bubble phase to the *capture* phase**
   (`window.addEventListener('keydown', onKeyDown, true)`, with the matching `removeEventListener(...,
   true)`) — the one ordering guarantee the DOM event model actually makes: a capture-phase listener on
   `window` always runs before *any* bubble-phase listener anywhere in the tree, including another
   bubble-phase `window` listener, regardless of which one registered first. This codebase already had
   precedent for exactly this (`ChordHost.tsx`'s own Ctrl+K chord handler), just not yet applied here. Fix 1
   alone, re-tested without the capture-phase change, did **not** stop the leak — `useListKeyboard`'s
   handler could still run first and claim the key before this module's own (now-unconditional)
   `preventDefault()` ever executed; only the two together close the gap for good, which is why the module's
   own doc comment now calls them "a matched pair."

Verified live (isolated `cargo tauri dev` instance, a freshly-created `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER` to force a
genuinely new WebView2 browser host with its own working CDP debugging port — the shared-host quirk
documented elsewhere in this file; real CDP, real trusted `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` sequences):
- **On a plain listing page** (no secondary row): a list row keyboard-focused, then Alt+Shift+J to the
  toolbar's first button — a real ArrowDown and a real ArrowUp both left the list's own
  `[data-kbd-focused]` text completely unchanged (previously, before either fix, both silently changed it);
  a real ArrowRight moved *within* the button row (confirmed by the focused button's own title changing,
  e.g. "Go to a path…" → "Details of this folder") rather than leaking to the list's `onOpen`/`onParent`; a
  second Alt+Shift+J correctly blurred the button (`document.activeElement` back to `BODY`); a further
  ArrowDown afterward correctly moved the list normally again, confirming nothing is left over-claimed once
  focus has been handed back.
- **On a real editor page** (both a primary and a secondary row genuinely present, confirmed via
  `!!document.querySelector('[data-primary-actions]')`/`[data-secondary-actions]` both reading `true`): a
  real Alt+Shift+J correctly focused "Back" (`row: primary`); a real ArrowDown correctly jumped to the
  secondary row's own first enabled button ("Select paragraphs") — proving the pre-existing primary→
  secondary jump from the previous section still works unchanged under the new capture-phase registration;
  a further Alt+Shift+J (now from the secondary row) correctly blurred (`BODY/none`); and a fresh
  Alt+Shift+J from that blurred state correctly returned to "Back" in the primary row — confirming the
  toggle-back is not a one-way trip and a subsequent press re-finds the primary row exactly as the very
  first press did.

`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean; no Rust change, so `cargo build`/`cargo test --lib` don't
apply. Not yet deployed to either the phone or this machine's production build as of this writing — this
was a pure dev-mode verification pass.

## User Action's close button moved into its own configuration dialog; a notebook-scoped three-dots menu for the editors; a danger-zone explanation; a note editor's own overflow menu

Four items from the same todo file, taken in order.

**1. "Keep just the launch button; right-clicking it opens the configuration dialog, which gets its own
Close button."** `UserActionButton.tsx` (the header control) and `UserActionFieldButtons` (the text-field
strip) each drop their `ZapOff` "close" icon down to one `Zap` launch button — `contextTrigger(choose)`
(already how a right click/long-press reached `UserActionModal` for *configuring* the page) is now also
the only way to reach **closing** it. `useUserActionOpen` (the hook both the button and the modal need to
know whether a window is open) moved out of `UserActionButton.tsx` into the plain logic module `userAction.ts`
— keeping it in the button file would have made `UserActionModal.tsx` import from `UserActionButton.tsx`
while the button already imports the modal, a genuine circular import. `UserActionModal.tsx` gained a
`ZapOff` **"Close the User Action window"** button, between Reset and "What is this?", calling the same
`closeUserAction()` the old adjacent button already called, disabled while nothing is open.

**2. The notebook-scoped three-dots menu** — the one genuinely large item, covered in its own section
below.

**3. "The two delete buttons in Settings' danger zone look identical — explain what tells them apart."**
One new paragraph in `SettingsTab.tsx`, shown only while `info?.canRelocate` (so it only appears where the
distinction it explains can actually matter), between the existing danger-zone text and the button row:
*Delete custom folder contents* (left) empties the folder the app is **actually** reading and writing right
now, keeping the folder itself; *Delete app data* (right) empties the **default** folder and forgets the
relocation pointer, leaving a custom folder's own files completely untouched on disk, just unreachable
until pointed at again by hand. So with a custom folder set, the left button is the one that actually wipes
what's in use.

**4. "Move Discard/Open-as-web-app/Show-its-top-bar/Its-files on the note editor's own header into a
three-dots menu."** New `MoreMenuButton.tsx` — a generic, always-on overflow button (unlike `RowActions.tsx`'s
own "collapse into one only if the global compact-row-actions setting is on") that opens `ContextMenu` at
its own corner; `NoteEditPage.tsx`'s header replaces those four `IconButton`s with one `MoreMenuButton`
carrying them as items, Save/Refresh-the-web-app/Insert-a-path/`UserActionButton` staying inline.

**Right-click/long-press menus gained real keyboard navigation, project-wide, not just for this one new
button.** `ContextMenu.tsx` (used by every existing right-click menu in the app) now focuses its first
enabled item the moment it mounts and handles arrow/Home/End/PageUp/PageDown/the fast-nav modifiers
(`lib/keyboard.ts`'s `fastNavAmount`/`PAGE_JUMP`, the same constants the row-of-buttons and list keyboard
handlers already share) by moving real DOM focus among its `button[role="menuitem"]`s — the same "real,
natively focusable buttons, not a virtual `[data-kbd-focused]`" shape `buttonRowKeyboard.ts` already uses for
a row of icon buttons, applied here to a vertical menu instead.

### A notebook's three-dots menu: moved items, "Create image from the clipboard…", and "Insert custom text at the caret…"

**"For editors inside a notebook's scope, add a three-dots menu with Create-image-from-clipboard and
Insert-custom-text, and move Search/Select-paragraphs/Open-link/Edit-insert-link into it too — Search
should also be visible on desktop, which it wasn't before."** `CodeEditor.tsx`'s `Props` gained
`notebookMenu?: { onCreateImageFromClipboard, onInsertCustomText }` — set only by a caller that has already
worked out the file being edited sits inside a listed notebook, `undefined` otherwise (so the menu, and
everything it moves, simply doesn't exist for a file outside every notebook — the existing inline buttons
stay exactly as they were there). All **four** real editor surfaces take it — `HighlightedEditor`/
`PlainTextEditor` (`CodeEditor.tsx` itself), `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx` (the desktop default) and
`MobileCodeMirrorEditor.tsx` — each swapping its own inline Search/Select-paragraphs/Open-link/
Edit-insert-link buttons for one `MoreMenuButton` carrying all six items (the four moved ones plus the two
new ones) the moment `notebookMenu` is set; `TextFieldMenu.tsx`'s own floating clipboard-menu checks a new
`data-notebook-editor-menu` attribute on the built-in editors' real `<textarea>` to suppress its own
"Insert a link"/"Select paragraphs" items when they've moved into this menu instead, so nothing is offered
twice.

**Detecting "inside a notebook" needed its own effect in each of the two hosts, and the Note Files
Explorer's own scoping had to be translated back to a real path first.** `NoteEditPage.tsx` resolves the
note's own notebook once (`notebookContaining(await loadNotebooks(), sourceId, folder)`) and keeps it in
state, reused both for gating the menu and for `pathPicked`'s existing absolute-path root (replacing its own
ad hoc re-load on every pick). `NotesApp.tsx` resolves the *editor's* notebook the same way, but from the
**real**, source-root-relative folder: inside the Note Files Explorer (`scope` set), `editing.path` is
relative to `scope.root`, not the source's own root, so `notebookContaining` would otherwise compare a
scope-relative path against a notebook's source-relative `folder` and never match — `joinRelative(scope.root,
parentPath(editing.path))` first, scope or not, is what the effect actually hands it.

**"Create image from the clipboard"** (`CreateImageFromClipboardModal.tsx`) is "we already have this in the
file explorer" (`NotesApp.tsx`'s own `pasteClipboardImage`) reached from an *editor*, which has no "folder
currently shown" of its own — so it gets a small paginated browser of its own, confined to the same `source`
the file being edited is already in (never a cross-account picker, since a relative markdown image reference
only makes sense within one storage), with its own current folder persisted per `persistKey` (the file being
edited). Picking a name warns, rather than silently overwriting, if it collides with something already
there. The caller wraps the result the same way "Insert a path…" already does — the same relative/absolute
`PathStyleModal` choice — just prefixed `![](...)` instead of inserted bare.

**"Insert custom text at the caret"** is the one genuinely new piece of language in this app: a small
templating mini-language, a per-notebook, multi-location, JSON-aware file/folder **merge**, and a browsing
UI, documented together since none of the three stand alone.

**The template language** (`lib/customTextTemplate.ts`, pure, no DOM — Node-tested with an ad-hoc script,
this project's own established pattern for a module with no committed test file yet): `{name}` (not
`$`-prefixed) is a **user variable** — `{name:single:default}`/`{name:multi:default}` picks a single- or
multi-line textbox (default: single), with an optional trailing `:nopersist` opting that one variable out of
being remembered for next time; `{$d:FORMAT}`/`{$d:FORMAT:OFFSET}` is a **dynamic date** token (`YYYY`/`MM`/
`DD`/`hh`/`mm`/`ss`/`SSS` in the format, an optional offset like `+2h-15m` applied against "now"); `{$p}` is
the **caret-pointer** token — it renders to nothing, but marks exactly where the caret should land once the
rendered text is inserted; `{{`/`}}` escape to a literal `{`/`}`. **Two real parsing bugs were caught by the
module's own Node smoke test before this ever reached a browser**: the offset's own trailing segment was
first peeled off by a fixed `split(':')`, which wrongly treated a legitimate `hh:mm:ss.SSS` format as a bogus
offset — fixed by only peeling a trailing segment that *validates* as a real offset, leaving the format
untouched otherwise; and a variable's own default value, split the same fixed way, lost everything past its
own first embedded colon (`{time:single:10:30:00}` kept only `"10"`) — fixed by splitting only `name`/`mode`
positionally and treating everything else as one `default`, peeling only a genuine trailing `:nopersist` off
the very end of *that*. (The offset regex's own alternation order matters too: `mo`/`ms` have to be tried
before `m`/`s`, or the shorter unit would shadow the longer one.)

**The merge** (`lib/customTextHierarchy.ts`, also pure and Node-smoke-tested): a notebook's own configured
**locations** (below) are each listed and merged into one `HierarchyLevel` tree — real files and folders as
themselves, and **every `.json` file's own parsed content merged in too**, recursively, in place of the
`.json` file itself (which never appears as its own row): a plain string/number/boolean property becomes a
file (its name the key, its content the stringified value); a nested object becomes a folder; an array of
plain strings/literals becomes a folder of files named (and containing) themselves; an array of objects
merges each object's own properties directly into that folder, one level flatter, rather than nesting them
under a numbered index. Identical names are **not** deduplicated — a real file and a json-derived entry
sharing a name both show up, since a template file and a generated snippet genuinely can collide in practice
and hiding one would be surprising. Everything is merged **eagerly**, not lazily per folder, specifically so
search can match a file's own *content*, not just its name.

**Locations** (`system/notes/customTextLocations.ts`) are a notebook's own `{ "Locations": [...] }`, kept —
like User Action's and the viewer script's own per-notebook settings — in `03/[custom-text-locations].json`
inside the notebook's internals pair, a plain local constant (not `lib/appConfig.ts`) since nothing on the
Rust side ever reads this file's name. **`CustomTextLocationsModal.tsx`** (reached from the notebook's own
row, "Set its custom-text locations…") browses *the notebook's whole source* (never scoped to the notebook's
own folder — a template's text has no path to reference, so there's no technical reason to restrict it
further, but the spec's own wording, "inside the current root," is one fixed root, not a free choice of
any), letting several folders be added and removed, each taking effect immediately with no separate "Save."

**A real, live-reproduced bug, not caught by `tsc`: the folder browser inside `CustomTextLocationsModal.tsx`
got permanently stuck on "Loading…".** `source = sourceOf(notebook.sourceId)` was called directly in the
render body — but `sourceOf` builds a *fresh* `FileSource` object (`localSource`/`filenSource` aren't
cached) on every call, so `source` had a new identity on every single render. The browse effect depends on
it (`[source, browsePath, browsing]`): each run set `setListing(null)` first, which (once the *previous*
run's listing had actually resolved and rendered) was itself a state change, triggering a re-render, which
computed a *new* `source`, which re-triggered the effect — forever. The browser only ever glimpsed the real
listing in the instant between one loop's resolve and the next loop's reset, which is why this looked, live,
like a permanently blank "Loading…" rather than a flicker. **Fixed with `useMemo(() => sourceOf(notebook
.sourceId), [sourceOf, notebook.sourceId])`** — keeping `source`'s identity stable across renders as long as
the notebook and the loaded roots/accounts don't actually change. **The identical pattern was found,
live, in the pre-existing `NoteViewerScriptModal.tsx`** (`const source = sourceOf(notebook.sourceId)` feeding
a `useCallback`'s own dependency array, the same shape, just with no `alive`-guard to make it look
*permanently* stuck rather than a fast, continuous re-fetch loop) — fixed the identical way, as a drive-by
correctness fix rather than leaving a known-bad pattern in place right next to its own just-fixed sibling.

**`InsertCustomTextModal.tsx`** browses the merged hierarchy with breadcrumbs, a search box (name or text,
case-insensitive), a folder list and a 2-column name/text table for files (`.custom-text-table`/
`.custom-text-preview`, new `App.css` rules matching `.file-table`'s own plain look, the preview column
truncated with an ellipsis rather than pushing the table wide). Clicking a file with no variables inserts its
rendered text immediately; one with variables opens a small form (one textbox per variable, pre-filled from
what was typed last time for *this exact snippet* — keyed by its own breadcrumb path, deliberately **not**
by the editor's `persistKey`, since the same snippet is meant to be reused from many different notes and the
whole point of remembering a value is so it isn't retyped regardless of where it's inserted from next). Both
the browse state (breadcrumb, query) and the typed variable values persist through `getAppState`/
`setAppState`, the same storage every other remembered place in this app uses.

**`CodeEditorHandle.insertAtCursor` gained an optional second parameter, `caretOffset`** — needed for `{$p}`:
without it, the caret always lands right after the inserted text, never *inside* it. All four
implementations (`HighlightedEditor`/`PlainTextEditor` in `CodeEditor.tsx`, `MonacoCodeEditor.tsx`,
`MobileCodeMirrorEditor.tsx`) honor it, landing the caret at `start + (caretOffset ?? text.length)` — Monaco's
own version additionally calls `editor.setPosition(model.getPositionAt(...))` after `executeEdits`, since
Monaco's own edit API doesn't otherwise let the caret land anywhere but the edit's own end.

**A second real bug, found live, in `CreateImageFromClipboardModal.tsx` itself**: a rejected clipboard read
(the ordinary case in a sandbox with no real OS clipboard, and a real possibility on a genuine machine too —
a denied permission, a webview that refuses to read it) only ever called `setError(...)`, never advanced
`stage` out of `'checking'` — and `stage === 'checking'` unconditionally `return`s `null`, so the modal
showed nothing at all, forever, with the error message set but never actually displayed anywhere. Fixed by
also calling `setStage('none')` in that branch — the `'none'` branch already shows `error` when it's set
(falling back to "There is no image on the clipboard." otherwise), so advancing to it is the whole fix.

Verified live (the debug build's own isolated data folder — see "Folder layout" — `cargo tauri dev` with a
dedicated WebView2 debugging profile, real CDP throughout, a real folder-based test notebook registered
from a real Notes window's own `app_state` call, a real `.txt` template file and a real markdown file
created via `fs_write_file`): the Monaco toolbar's **"More actions"** menu, opened on a file inside the
notebook, listed exactly `Find (Ctrl+F) | Select paragraphs | Open link at the caret | Edit the link at the
caret, or insert a new one | Create image from the clipboard… | Insert custom text at the caret…`, in that
order; a real ArrowDown moved real DOM focus from "Find" to "Select paragraphs" inside the open menu. The
same six items, plus the four from item 4, showed correctly in the note editor's own two-row toolbar when
the new note (created live through the real "New note…" dialog) was shown to sit inside the same notebook —
confirming the identical `notebookMenu` wiring reaches `NoteEditPage.tsx` too, not only `NotesApp.tsx`'s
plain-file editor. "No locations chosen yet" showed correctly before any location was configured; configuring
one through the real `CustomTextLocationsModal.tsx` UI (Add a location… → browse → Add this folder) — after
the `useMemo` fix, since before it the browser never got past "Loading…" — persisted correctly and showed up
in the Insert-custom-text modal's own root listing a moment later. **The full template pipeline was verified
end to end, through the real UI, not assumed from the pure-function tests alone**: a template file
containing `Hi {name:single:World}! Pointer->X{$p}Y<- today is {$d:YYYY-MM-DD}.` — opened through the real
variable-fill form, its default `World` confirmed pre-filled, changed to `Ayran` via a real React-tracked
input change — inserted, through the real `insertAtCursor` path, as `Hi Ayran! Pointer->XY<- today is
2026-10-26.` (matching the environment's own real current date) at the real caret position in Monaco; a
real CDP-dispatched keystroke typed immediately afterward landed *between* the X and the Y, confirming
`{$p}`'s own caret-offset placement was honored exactly, not just approximated. User Action's single button
plus its right-click-reached **"Close the User Action window"** (correctly disabled with no window open) was
confirmed through the same real notebook; the Settings danger-zone paragraph read correctly describing the
asymmetry between the two delete buttons; `NoteEditPage.tsx`'s own **"More actions"** correctly listed
`Discard your unsaved changes | Open it as a web app in a window of its own — it follows this editor | Show
its top bar — the web app's own window isn't listed to do this from elsewhere | Its files`. **Both
found-live bugs were reproduced first against the unfixed build and re-confirmed working against the fixed
one** (the stuck-"Loading…" browser, and the stuck-blank clipboard-error modal), not merely fixed and trusted.
`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean throughout; no Rust change, so `cargo build`/
`cargo test --lib` don't apply.

## Custom-text follow-ups: item names, repeatable locations, tags, cache, and date/timezone formats

Five more asks about the same "Insert custom text" feature, all from one todo file, taken in order.

**1. "Let's also make that option available for when typing the item name... the set of locations will be the
same in both cases; but for each location the user will specify... if the location is for item names or for
the text editor or for both."** `CustomTextLocation` (`customTextLocations.ts`) is no longer a bare path —
`{ id, path, appliesTo }`, `appliesTo: 'editor' | 'itemName' | 'both'` — and `pathsForMode(locations, mode)`
is the one thing both callers (the editor's own menu, and the new item-name button below) filter through
before building a hierarchy, so neither needs to know the other exists. **`id` is new too, and it's the one
field that has to survive every edit untouched** — it's what a location's own colored tags (item 3) are
attached to; a location read from the *old*, plain-string-array format gets one made for it the first time
it's read, and that read immediately writes the migrated form back (`readCustomTextLocations`), so the same
id — and so the same tags — come back on every later read, not a fresh one each time.

**`InsertCustomTextButton.tsx`** is the item-name counterpart of the editor's own menu entry — self-contained
the way `UserActionButton` is: given `sourceId`/`folder`, it resolves its own notebook, renders nothing
outside one, and reads that notebook's locations fresh every time it's opened, filtered to `itemName`/`both`.
Unlike the editor there's no `CodeEditorHandle` to splice into — just a plain `<input>` the caller already
owns the `value`/`onChange` of — so insertion reads the field's own current selection (`inputRef`) and writes
the result back through the caller's `onChange`, restoring focus and the new caret position on the next
frame (the field hasn't re-rendered with the new value yet at the moment `onChange` is called).

Wired into **`NewNoteModal.tsx`** and **`FolderPairModal.tsx`**'s own title fields (both already had a
`source`/notebook-adjacent context in scope — `FolderPairModal` reuses `source.id` rather than taking a new
prop, since `scopedSource` already preserves the real source id under `.id` for exactly this kind of lookup).
`FolderPairModal` also gained a `realFolder` prop, separate from `folder` — `folder` is relative to whatever
`source` represents (a note's own `01` folder, inside the Note Files Explorer), but notebook detection needs
the *real*, un-scoped path; defaults to `folder` itself, which is already real for every caller except that
one scoped case.

**"New file…"/"New folder…" used plain `window.prompt()`, which can't host a button** — converted to a small
modal, `NamePromptModal.tsx` (a name field, the same `InsertCustomTextButton`, OK/Cancel), reused by both
`NotesApp.tsx`'s `createFolder`/`createFile` (now two state flags opening the modal, with `submitNewFolder`/
`submitNewFile` doing what the prompt's own continuation used to). `pasteClipboardImage`'s own "name the
clipboard image" prompt is deliberately **left as a native prompt** — out of scope for this pass, disclosed
rather than silently fixed, since it wasn't named directly and converting it would mean the same clipboard
image's own content has to be known before the button could resolve anything useful to insert near it
anyway, a different shape of problem than a plain name field.

**A real comment-syntax bug was found by the build, not by inspection**: `NamePromptModal.tsx`'s own first
doc comment read `...**New folder…**/**New file…**...` — the sequence `…**/` is a literal `*/`, closing the
`/** ... */` JSDoc comment several sentences early; everything after that point was then parsed as real
TypeScript, producing dozens of cascading syntax errors from `tsc -b` (but not from a bare `npx tsc --noEmit`,
which — confirmed directly — doesn't actually check this project's app source at all under its own project-
references setup, only `tsc -b`/`npm run build`'s own `tsc -b` step does; a useful, if sobering, correction to
this file's own "verified live" habit of citing `npx tsc --noEmit` as if it were a second, independent check —
from now on `npm run build` is the one that actually matters). Fixed by rewording the sentence to avoid the
`**X**/` shape; every other new/edited file in this pass was grepped for the same `**/` pattern afterward,
with none found.

**2. "The user should be able to keep adding / removing locations all the time; right now, I can only add
locations when none have been added yet."** Read carefully against the already-shipped `CustomTextLocationsModal.tsx`
(whose own `useMemo` fix for the *previous* "stuck on Loading…" bug — see "A notebook's three-dots menu" —
looked, on paper, like it should already cover this): nothing in the code actually reproduced a
one-shot-only limit by inspection. Given the file needed a full rewrite anyway for items 1/3/4 below, it was
rebuilt with explicit care here and **verified live, specifically for a second add in the same session**
(the earlier verification only ever exercised one) — through the real UI: a first location added
successfully, the "Add a location…" browser reopened at the root with a real listing (not stuck), navigated
to a second real folder, and added — both locations then showing correctly, side by side. The most likely
explanation, not chased further since the bug didn't reproduce against the current code either way: the
report describes the *already-fixed*, pre-`useMemo` "stuck on Loading…" symptom (from before this session),
where the **first** add could still be clicked through blind (the "Add this folder" button was never gated
on the listing actually loading) while a **second** attempt, compounding the same re-render loop, became bad
enough to look genuinely stuck — consistent with "after that I'm stuck," but not something this pass could
make happen again to confirm.

**3. "The user should be able to add colored tags to locations."** The exact same mechanism root tags already
use (`lib/rootTags.ts`'s own doc comment: "a tag is attached to an opaque guid... which means every place
that shows the root shows the same tags") — `customTextLocationTagGuid(id)` → `` `customtext-location:${id}` ``,
and the existing `TagList`/`listTags` components and commands need nothing new. The per-location row moved
into the same `.window-item`/`.window-item-row`/`.window-item-tags` markup every other tagged list in this app
already uses (not a bespoke layout) — `.window-item-row`'s `justify-content: space-between` with the
entry-name wearing `.window-item-main` (`flex: 1`) absorbs the slack correctly even with the new mode
`<select>` as a third sibling, the same shape `AppsTab.tsx`'s own rows already rely on.

**4. "I saw that the contents of locations are being cached... but when removing a location, its cache
should be wiped. Likewise, we should also add an option to refresh a location's cache."** `FileSource.cache`
(already built for the Filen cache's own "Cache options of one item" section) is exactly the right shape
with no new backend command needed: `removeLocation` calls `source.cache?.clear(location.path)` — already
documented as recursive for a folder — **before** removing the entry, with a failure shown as an error but
never blocking the removal itself (a cache-clear failing shouldn't trap the person into being unable to drop
a misconfigured or offline location); a new `RefreshCw` **"Refresh its cache"** button per location calls
`source.cache.hardRefresh(location.path)`. **Both are gated on `source?.cache` existing at all** — absent for
a folder on this device, exactly the same gate `CacheMenu` already uses elsewhere, confirmed live: the
button simply isn't rendered for a local-device notebook (verified against the real folder-based test
notebook below, which has no Filen account to exercise the cache path itself against — the same disclosed
gap every other cache-touching feature in this app's own history already carries when no live Filen account
is available in the isolated test instance).

**5. Date/timezone formats — answered directly, and implemented to match** (`lib/customTextTemplate.ts`).
The person's own prepared formats (`{$d:yyyy-MM-dd}`, `{$d:hh-mm-ss}`, a final `{$d:...-zzz}`) line up almost
exactly with **.NET's own custom date-and-time format specifiers** — `yyyy`/`MM`/`dd`/`HH`/`hh`/`mm`/`ss`/
`fff`/`zzz` — which is the convention adopted (rather than a third, invented one), added *alongside* the
module's own original uppercase tokens (`YYYY`/`DD`/`SSS`), not replacing them: `yyyy`/`YYYY` (year),
`MM` (month), `dd`/`DD` (day), `HH`/`hh` (hour — **both spellings mean 24-hour**; there is no 12-hour/AM-PM
token, since nothing in any example asked for one), `mm`/`ss` (unchanged), `SSS`/`fff` (millisecond), and
**`zzz`** — new — which renders the *resolved* UTC offset as `±HH:MM`, matching .NET's own meaning for that
exact token: "yes, these formats work," once `yyyy`/`dd` are recognized (the module only ever had uppercase
`YYYY`/`DD` before this).

**The offset grammar** (add/subtract date components) already matched the person's own second draft almost
exactly — `{$d:format:offset}`, units `y`/`d`/`h`/`m`/`s`/`ms` — except their `"-1y+2M"` used uppercase `M`
for *month*, which the module only had as `mo`: added as a direct alias (`mo`/`M` both mean month now;
offset units stay case-sensitive, so lowercase `m` still means minute, with no ambiguity introduced).
**"Will it be something like the following?" — yes, with that one addition.**

**The colon-escaping ask** ("let's give the user the option to use \[the colon] as a string literal by
escaping it by repeating it; so `{$d:hh::mm:+1h}` will print something like `10:30`") was **not** already
implemented — the module's old `$d` parsing found the format/offset boundary with a plain `lastIndexOf(':')`,
which happened to still locate the right boundary by coincidence in that exact example, but never actually
collapsed the doubled colon, so the real output would have kept both (`"10::30"`, not `"10:30"`). Replaced
with a real escape-aware scanner, `splitDateBodySegments`: a doubled colon (`"::"`) becomes one literal `:`
kept in whichever segment is currently being built, and a single `:` starts a new one — so `"hh::mm:+1h"`
becomes exactly the two segments `["hh:mm", "+1h"]`, the first (the format) keeping its own real colon. This
is also why the person's own prepared formats all use dashes/underscores instead of colons (`yyyy-MM-dd`,
`hh-mm`) — not because of this new escaping rule, which didn't exist yet when those were written, but almost
certainly because these templates are meant for **file/item names**, where a literal colon isn't even a
valid character on Windows; in practice a format rarely needs the escape at all.

**The timezone ask — "let's give the user the option to specify the time zone... I think the best way would
be to specify it after the second colon, after the (optionally) added/subtracted date components: the time
zone info like +0300 or -0200 should come after the letter T, and the UTC time zone should be something like
TZ or TK (please suggest the best option)."** **`TZ` is the better of the two, and is exactly what got
built**: `Z` is already the universal ISO-8601 shorthand for UTC (`2024-01-01T00:00:00Z`) — `K` has no
established meaning to lean on — so `T` + `Z` (case-insensitive) means UTC, and `T+0300`/`T-0200` (always
two digits of hours then two of minutes, no colon, matching the person's own examples exactly) is a fixed
offset applied regardless of the system's own local timezone. **A segment's position doesn't matter** —
`{$d:format:offset:T<tz>}` and `{$d:format:T<tz>}` (no offset at all) both work, since every segment after
the format is classified by its own shape (timezone-shaped? offset-shaped?), never by which position it
happens to occupy — so there's no awkward "leave the middle segment empty" syntax needed when only a
timezone, not an offset, is wanted. The one draft format that used a timezone-shaped guess before this
question was asked explicitly (`"...-zzz:-4z"`, a bare `-4z` offset with no real meaning — there's no `z`
date-math unit) is superseded by this mechanism, not supported as written: the intended effect is now
`{$d:...-zzz:T+0400}` or similar.

**A real correctness question was checked directly before shipping, not assumed**: whether UTC's own
`Date.getUTC*()` getters, or a plain offset-shifted timestamp read back with the same getters, actually
produce the "wall clock at that offset" a person expects — confirmed with hand-built cases crossing a day
boundary (`T-0530` shifting a UTC noon timestamp back past midnight, `T+0300` forward across one) in the
module's own Node test (below), not just trusted from the formula.

Node-tested (`lib/customTextTemplate.ts`, an ad-hoc script matching this project's own "Node-tested"
convention for a module with no committed test file yet, run via `node --experimental-strip-types`): every
one of the person's own six prepared formats (with `yyyy`/`dd` now recognized); the pre-existing uppercase
tokens and `SSS` still working unchanged; the new `fff` alias; the `-1y+2M` offset (both `M` and the
pre-existing `mo` spelling, cross-checked against hand-computed calendar math — `2025-12-20` minus a year
then plus two months lands on `2025-02-20`, not the year before, since December plus two months rolls into
the next year); the colon escape, with and without a trailing offset; `TZ` rendering a UTC date/time and
`zzz` correctly as `+00:00`; `T+0300` and `T-0530` both shifting the displayed time correctly (including
across a day boundary) and `zzz` reporting the matching offset in each; an offset and a timezone combined,
and a timezone with no offset segment at all; case-insensitive `tz`/`t+0300`; and every "never throws" case
(an unterminated brace, an unrecognized `$`-token, a trailing segment that's neither a valid offset nor a
valid timezone, a malformed `T+030` with the wrong digit count) all resolving to the plain, sensible fallback
rather than an exception.

**Verified live** (isolated **debug** data folder — a dedicated `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER`/CDP port, desktop,
real CDP throughout, a real folder-based notebook (`customtext-test`) registered the same way earlier
sessions' own folder-as-notebook tests were, with two real locations — `loc-a` (containing a real
`greeting.txt`: `Hello {name:single:World}! Today is {$d:yyyy-MM-dd}.{$p}`) set to **item names only**, and
`loc-b` (containing a real `sig.txt`: `Best, {name:single:Me:nopersist}`) set to **editor only** —, both
locations' mode changes confirmed persisted to the real `[custom-text-locations].json` on disk with the new
`Id`/`Path`/`AppliesTo` shape):
- **The "stuck after one add" bug**: two real, sequential adds through the actual "Add a location…" browser
  both succeeded, the second reopening at the root with a genuine listing (not stuck), both locations shown
  side by side afterward.
- **Colored tags**: added a real tag ("snippets", a real color) to `loc-a` through the actual dialog; a
  direct `list_tags` call against `customtext-location:<loc-a's real id>` confirmed it was stored correctly,
  keyed to the location's own stable id, not the notebook's or anything else's.
- **Mode filtering, both directions, through the real UI — not assumed from the stored data alone**: opening
  the editor's own **"Insert custom text at the caret…"** on a file inside the notebook showed **only**
  `sig.txt` (`loc-b`, editor mode) — `greeting.txt` (`loc-a`, item-name mode) was correctly absent; filling
  and inserting it landed `"Best, Me"` at the real Monaco caret position. Separately, opening **"New note…"**
  from the notebook's own notes page showed the new **"Insert custom text here…"** button on the title field,
  and its own picker showed **only** `greeting.txt` — `sig.txt` was correctly absent; filling the `name`
  variable with `"Ayran"` and inserting produced, in the real `<input>`: `"Hello Ayran! Today is 2026-10-26."`
  (the live system date, confirming `yyyy-MM-dd` renders correctly outside the Node test too) with the real
  caret (`selectionStart === selectionEnd === 33`) landing exactly where `{$p}` marked it, at the very end.
- **"New file…"/"New folder…" as real modals, not native prompts**: "New file…" opened with "File name"
  pre-filled `untitled.md` and the new "Insert custom text here…" button present; submitting a real name
  (`test-new-file.txt`) created the file on disk (confirmed via a direct `fs_exists` call) and opened it in
  the editor, exactly as the old prompt-based flow did. "New folder…" correctly refused a name already taken
  (`"loc-a" already exists.`) without creating anything, matching the pre-existing `createFolder`'s own
  behavior.
- **The cache buttons' gating**: confirmed absent for both locations in this local-device notebook (no
  `RefreshCw` button rendered for either row) — matching `source?.cache` being `undefined` for a folder on
  this device, the same gate `CacheMenu` already uses. **Not exercised live**: the actual clear-on-remove and
  refresh calls against a real Filen-backed location — no Filen account was connected in this isolated
  instance, the same disclosed gap this app's own cache-feature history already carries elsewhere; this rests
  on `FileSource.cache`'s own already-proven `clear`/`hardRefresh` methods (used identically by `CacheMenu`
  and the Filen cache's own "Delete all cache for a branch" feature) rather than a fresh live call here.

All test data (the notebook registration, the `customtext-test` folder and its contents) was removed from
the isolated debug data folder afterward. `npm run build` clean throughout (after the comment-syntax fix
above); no Rust change, so `cargo build`/`cargo test --lib` don't apply.
